| ¶1 fr.527 umn.424 | 13. 7 000 av. J.-C. · Appareil de capture -----<br><br>Proposition X : L'Etat et ses pôles. Revenons aux thèses de Dumézil : 1 ) la souveraineté politique aurait deux pôles, l'Empereur terrible et magicien, opérant par capture, liens, nœuds et filets, le Roi prêtre et juriste, procédant par traités, pactes, contrats (c'est le couple Varuna-Mitra, Oddhin-Tyr, Votan-Tiwaz, Ouranos-Zeus, Romulus-Numa . . . ) ; 2 ) une fonction de guerre est extérieure à la souveraineté politique, et se distingue d'un pôle autant que de l'autre ( c'est Indra, ou Thor, ou Tullus Hostilius ... 1 ). 1 . Le livre principal de Dumézil à cet égard est Mitra-Varuna (on y trouve aussi l'analyse du « Borgne » et du « Manchot »). 1 ) C'est un curieux rythme qui anime ainsi l'appareil d'Etat, et c'est d'abord un grand mystère, celui des Dieux-lieurs ou des empereurs magiques, Borgnes émettant d'un œil unique les signes qui capturent, qui nouent à distance. Les rois-juristes sont plutôt des Manchots) qui lèvent l'unique main comme élément du droit et de la technique, de la loi et de l'outil. Dans la succession des hommes d'Etat, cherchez toujours le Borgne et le Manchot, Horatius Coclès et Mucius Scaevola ( de Gaulle et Pompidou ? ). Ce n'est pas que l'un ait l'exclusivité des signes, et l'autre, des outils. L'empereur terrible est déjà maître des grands travaux ; le roi sage emporte et transforme tout le régime des signes. C'est que la combinaison signes-outils constitue de toute façon le trait différentiel de la souveraineté politique, ou la complémentarité d'Etat 2. 2 ) Bien sûr, les deux hommes d'Etat ne cessent pas d'être mêlés à des histoires de guerre. | 1 3 . 7000 B.C . : <br><br>PROPOSITION X. The State and its poles. Let us return to Dumezil's theses: ( 1 ) Political sovereignty has two poles, the fearsome magician-emperor, operating by capture, bonds, knots, and nets, and the jurist-priest-king, proceeding by treaties, pacts, contracts (the couples Varuna-Mitra, Odin-Tyr, Wotan-Tiwaz, Uranus-Zeus, RomulusNuma . . . ); (2) the war function is exterior to political sovereignty and is equally distinct from both its poles (Indra or Thor or Tullus Hostilius . . . ).1 1 . The State apparatus is thus animated by a curious rhythm, which is first of all a great mystery: that of the Binder-Gods or magic emperors, One-Eyed men emitting from their single eye signs that capture, tie knots at a distance. The jurist-kings, on the other hand, are One-Armed men who raise their single arm as an element of right and technology, the law and the tool. In the succession of men of State, look always for the One-Eyed and the One-Armed, Horatius Cocles and Mucius Scaevola (de Gaulle and Pompidou?). This is not to say that one has exclusive right to signs, the other to tools. The fearsome emperor is already the master of large-scale works; the wise king takes up and transf orms the entire regime of signs. What it means is that the combination, signs-tools, constitutes the differential trait of political sovereignty, or the complementarity of the State.2 2. Of course, the two men of State are always getting mixed up in affairs of war. | 13. 13 7000 B.C. ·<br>***Appareil de capture*** <span style="color:green">[apparatus of capture — appareil also the workaday register: device, machinery, appareil photo = camera; capture the hunting/fishing register: the seizure of prey, the catch]</span><br><br>Proposition X: The State and its poles.<br>Let us return to Dumézil's theses: 1) political sovereignty<br>***aurait*** <span style="color:green">[would have — conditional of reported thesis: Dumézil's claim under examination, not asserted]</span> two poles, the terrible and magician Emperor,<br>***opérant*** <span style="color:green">[operating — the operation: surgical and military register, action done to a body]</span> by capture,<br>***liens*** <span style="color:green">[bonds, ties — from lier, to bind; head of the bind-chain (liens, nœuds, nouer, Dieux-lieurs) running through the plateau; also the legal lien on property]</span>, knots, and nets, the priest and jurist King,<br>***procédant*** <span style="color:green">[proceeding — legal procedure register: to proceed as a court proceeds, by forms]</span> by treaties, pacts, contracts (it is the couple Varuna-Mitra, Oddhin-Tyr, Wotan-Tiwaz, Ouranos-Zeus, Romulus-Numa…); 2) a war function is exterior to political sovereignty, and distinguishes itself from the one pole as much as from the other (it is Indra, or Thor, or Tullus Hostilius…¹).<br>1 It is a curious rhythm that thus animates the State apparatus, and it is first of all a great mystery, that of the<br>***Dieux-lieurs*** <span style="color:green">[Binder-Gods — lieur, agent noun of lier: the bind-chain personified]</span> or the magic emperors,<br>***Borgnes*** <span style="color:green">[One-Eyed men — borgne, blind in one eye; also pejorative: shady, disreputable (hôtel borgne)]</span> emitting from a single eye the signs that capture, that<br>***nouent à distance*** <span style="color:green">[knot at a distance — nouer, verb of nœuds; action at a distance, the trap sprung without contact]</span>. The jurist-kings are rather<br>***Manchots*** <span style="color:green">[One-Armed men — manchot, lacking a hand or arm; colloquially ne pas être manchot = to be handy, deft]</span> who<br>***lèvent l'unique main*** <span style="color:green">[raise the single hand — lever la main: the oath-swearing gesture; the jurist's raised hand is the sworn hand, right and law by oath]</span> as element of right and of technique, of law and of tool. In the succession of the men of State,<br>***cherchez toujours*** <span style="color:green">[look always for — the detective formula, cherchez la femme register: the standing instruction for what to hunt in any case]</span> the Borgne and the Manchot, Horatius Cocles and Mucius Scaevola (de Gaulle and Pompidou?). It is not that the one has exclusivity over signs, and the other, over tools. The terrible emperor is already master of the<br>***grands travaux*** <span style="color:green">[great works — the set phrase for State public works, monuments and corvées; adjacent to travaux forcés, penal labor]</span>; the wise king<br>***emporte*** <span style="color:green">[carries off — the spoils register: to bear away as booty; also l'emporter, to win out — the king captures the whole regime of signs in the act of transforming it]</span> and transforms the whole regime of signs. It is that the signs-tools combination constitutes in any case the differential trait of political sovereignty, or the State complementarity².<br>2 Of course, the two men of State do not cease to be<br>***mêlés à des histoires de guerre*** <span style="color:green">[mixed up in war business — des histoires, colloquially trouble, messy affairs, not just "stories"; se mêler, to meddle, get entangled]</span>.<br>¹ Dumézil's principal book in this regard is *Mitra-Varuna* (one also finds there the analysis of the « Borgne » and the « Manchot »). <span style="color:green">(Fr: 1; Minn: 1 — footnote text not present in supplied Minnesota chunk, endnote edition)</span> |
| ¶2 fr.528 umn.425 | Mais précisément, ou bien l'empereur magique fait se battre des guerriers qui ne sont pas les siens, qu'il met à son service par capture ; ou bien, surtout, il suspend les armes quand il surgit sur le champ de bataille, il lance son filet sur les guerriers, il leur inspire d'un seul œil une catatonie pétrifiée, « il lie sans combat », il encaste la machine de guerre ( on ne confondra certes pas, donc, cette capture d'Etat avec les captures de guerre, conquêtes, prisonniers, butins 3). Quant à l'autre pôle, le roi juriste est un grand organisateur de la guerre ; mais il lui donne des lois, lui aménage un champ, lui invente un droit, lui impose une discipline, la subordonne à des fins politiques. Il fait de la machine de guerre une institution militaire, il approprie la machine de guerre à l'appareil d'Etat 4. On ne parlera pas trop vite d'adoucissement, d'humanisation : peut-être est-ce là, au contraire, que la machine de guerre n'a plus qu'un objet, la guerre elle-même. La violence, on la trouve partout, mais sous des régimes et des économies différentes. La violence de l'empereur magique son nœud, son filet, son « coup 2. Le thème du Dieu-lieur et du nœud magique a fait l'objet d'études mythologiques globales : notamment Mircea Eliade, Images et symboles, Gallimard, ch. III. Mais ces études sont ambiguës, parce qu'elles utilisent une méthode syncrétique ou archétypale. La méthode de Dumézil est au contraire différentielle : le thème de la capture ou du lien ne rassemble des données diverses que sous un trait différentiel, précisément constitué par la souveraineté politique. Sur l'opposition de ces deux méthodes, on se reportera à Ortigues, Le discours et le symbole, Aubier. 3. Dumézil, Mitra-Varuna, pp. 1 13-1 14, 151, 202-203. 4. Idem) p. 150 : « Il y a bien des manières d'être dieu de la guerre, et Tiwaz en définit une qui serait très mal exprimée par les étiquettes dieu guerrier, dieu combattant... Tiwaz est autre chose : le juriste de la guerre, et en même temps une manière de diplomate » (de même, Mars). une fois pour toutes » | But either the magic emperor sends to battle warriors who are not his own, whom he takes into his service by capture; or, more important, when he makes his appearance on the battlefield, he suspends the use of weapons, he throws his net over the warriors, his single eye throws them into petrified catatonia, "he binds without combat," he encasts the war machine (this State capture is not to be conf used with the captures of war: conquests, prisoners, spoils). 3 As for the other pole, the jurist-king is a great organizer of war; but he gives it laws, lays out a field for it, makes it principled, imposes a discipline upon it, subordinates it to political ends. He turns the war machine into a military institution, he appropriates the war machine for the State apparatus.4 We should not be too hasty in speaking of a sof tening, a humanization: on the contrary, this is perhaps when the war machine has only one remaining object, that of war itself. Violence is found everywhere, but under diff erent regimes and economies. The violence of the magic emperor: his knot, his net, his way of "making his moves once and for all" | But precisely, either the magic emperor<br>***fait se battre*** <span style="color:green">[makes fight — the causative: he does not fight, he sets fighting going]</span> warriors who are not his own, whom he<br>***met à son service*** <span style="color:green">[puts into his service — pressed into service: the levy, service owed]</span> by capture; or else, above all, he<br>***suspend les armes*** <span style="color:green">[suspends arms — suspension d'armes, the diplomatic set phrase for truce: the emperor's apparition is itself a cease-fire]</span> when he<br>***surgit*** <span style="color:green">[looms up, surges forth — sudden apparition, not mere arrival]</span> on the field of battle, he<br>***lance son filet*** <span style="color:green">[casts his net — the fisherman's or retiarius's cast: the coup de filet, the haul]</span> over the warriors, he inspires in them with a single eye a petrified catatonia, « he<br>***lie*** <span style="color:green">[binds — lier: the bind-chain, here in Dumézil's own formula]</span> without combat », he<br>***encaste*** <span style="color:green">[encasts — rare verb hearing both encastrer, to embed or fit in, and caste: locks the war machine into a caste]</span> the war machine (<br>***on ne confondra certes pas*** <span style="color:green">[one will certainly not confuse — impersonal on + future of instruction: the treatise's command-form, not "we should"]</span>, then, this State capture with the captures of war, conquests, prisoners,<br>***butins***³ <span style="color:green">[spoils, booty — butin: the war-capture proper, against which State capture is being distinguished; cf. emporter ¶1]</span>). As for the other pole, the jurist king is a great organizer of war; but he gives it laws,<br>***aménage*** <span style="color:green">[lays out, fits up — aménagement, the town-planning and land-development register]</span> a field for it, invents a right for it, imposes a discipline on it, subordinates it to political<br>***fins*** <span style="color:green">[ends — finality: the pole of ends and laws, cf. ¶3 "in virtue of ends"]</span>. He makes of the<br>***machine de guerre*** <span style="color:green">[war machine — machine kept distinct from appareil throughout: engine, working assembly vs. State apparatus]</span> a military institution, he appropriates the war machine to the State apparatus<br>⁴<br>.<br>***On ne parlera pas trop vite*** <span style="color:green">[one will not speak too soon — parler trop vite, the idiom of premature judgment, again in the instruction-future]</span> of softening, of humanization: perhaps it is there, on the contrary, that the war machine no longer has anything but one object, war itself.<br>***La violence, on la trouve partout*** <span style="color:green">[Violence — one finds it everywhere: left-dislocated syntax, the object thrown forward then resumed by pronoun; spoken-French attack]</span>, but under different regimes and economies. The violence of the magic emperor: his knot, his net, his «<br>***coup*** … once and for all » <span style="color:green">[stroke, blow, move — also coup de filet, the haul of the cast net just made; sentence continues into next row]</span><br>² The theme of the Binder-God and the magic knot has been the object of global mythological studies: notably Mircea Eliade, *Images et symboles*, Gallimard, ch. III. But these studies are ambiguous, because they use a syncretic or archetypal method. Dumézil's method is on the contrary differential: the theme of capture or of the bond gathers diverse data only under a differential trait, precisely constituted by political sovereignty. On the opposition of these two methods, one will refer to Ortigues, *Le discours et le symbole*, Aubier. <span style="color:green">(Fr: 2; Minn: endnote, absent from supplied chunk)</span><br>³ Dumézil, *Mitra-Varuna*, pp. 113–114, 151, 202–203.<br><br>⁴<br>Idem, p. 150: « There are many ways of being a god of war, and Tiwaz defines one that would be very badly expressed by the labels warrior god, combatant god… Tiwaz is something else: the jurist of war, and at the same time a kind of diplomat » (likewise, Mars). |
| ¶3 fr.529 umn.425 | . .. La violence du roi Juriste, son recommencement à chaque coup, toujours en vertu des fins, des alliances et des lois . . . A la limite, la violence de la machine de guerre pourrait paraître plus douce et plus souple que celle de l'appareil d'Etat : c'est qu'elle n'a pas encore la guerre comme « objet » , c'est qu'elle échappe aux deux pôles de l'Etat. C'est pourquoi l'homme de guerre, dans son extériorité, ne cesse pas de protester contre les alliances et pactes du roi juriste, mais aussi de défaire les liens de l'empereur magique. Il est délieur autant que parjure : deux fois traître s. Il a une autre économie, une autre cruauté, mais aussi une autre justice, une autre pitié. Aux signes et outils de l'Etat, l'homme de guerre oppose ses armes et ses bijoux. Là encore, qui dira le meilleur et le pire ? Il est bien vrai que la guerre tue, et mutile affreusement. Mais elle le fait d'autant plus que l'Etat s'approprie la machine de guerre. Et surtout l'appareil d'Etat fait que la mutilation et même la mort viennent avant. Il a besoin qu'elles soient déjà faites, et que les hommes naissent ainsi, infirmes et zombies. Le mythe du zombie, du mort-vivant, est un mythe du travail et non de la guerre. | . . . The violence of the jurist-king: his way of beginning over again every move, always with attention to ends, alliances, and laws . . . All things considered, the violence of the war machine might appear softer and more supple than that of the State apparatus because it does not yet have war as its "object," because it eludes both poles of the State. That is why the man of war, in his exteriority, is always protesting the alliances and pacts of the jurist-king, as well as severing the bonds of the magic emperor. He is equally an unbinder and a betrayer: twice the traitor.5 He has another economy, another cruelty, but also another justice, another pity. To the signs and tools of the State, the man of war opposes his weapons and jewelry. Once again, who could say which is better and which is worse? It is true that war kills, and hideously mutilates. But it is especially true after the State has appropriated the war machine. Above all, the State apparatus makes the mutilation, and even death, come first. It needs them preaccomplished, for people to be born that way, crippled and zombielike. The myth of the zombie, of the living dead, is a work myth and not a war myth. | … The violence of the jurist king, its<br>***recommencement à chaque coup*** <span style="color:green">[starting-over at each stroke — coup again: the jurist's repeated coup against the emperor's « coup once and for all » of ¶2]</span>, always<br>***en vertu des*** <span style="color:green">[in virtue of — the statute formula: en vertu de la loi, by force of, under the authority of; the jurist's violence comes with legal citation attached]</span> ends, alliances, and laws…<br>***À la limite*** <span style="color:green">[at the limit — the limit-case, mathematical register: pushed to its extreme point, not "all things considered"]</span>, the violence of the machine de guerre could appear softer and more supple than that of the State apparatus: it is that it does not yet have war as « object », it is that it escapes the two poles of the State. That is why the man of war, in his exteriority, does not cease to protest against the alliances and pacts of the jurist king, but also to<br>***défaire les liens*** <span style="color:green">[undo the bonds — dé-faire, the un-making that pairs with délieur below: the bind-chain worked in reverse]</span> of the magic emperor. He is<br>***délieur*** <span style="color:green">[unbinder — dé-lier, the bind-chain negated: coinage matching Dieux-lieurs]</span> as much as<br>***parjure*** <span style="color:green">[perjurer, oath-breaker — the sworn pact violated: the raised hand of ¶1 forsworn; not generic betrayal]</span>: twice a traitor<br>⁵<br>. He has another economy, another cruelty, but also another justice, another pity. To the signs and tools of the State, the man of war opposes his weapons and his jewels. There again,<br>***qui dira le meilleur et le pire ?*** <span style="color:green">[who will say the better and the worse? — sounding the marriage vow, pour le meilleur et pour le pire, "for better or for worse": the State and its war machine as espoused, no one to judge between the spouses]</span><br>***Il est bien vrai que*** <span style="color:green">[it is quite true that — the concessive set phrase: granting the objection before turning it]</span> war kills, and mutilates frightfully. But it does so<br>***d'autant plus que*** <span style="color:green">[all the more as — proportional-causal: mutilation scales with appropriation, no temporal "after"]</span> the State appropriates itself the war machine. And above all the State apparatus<br>***fait que la mutilation et même la mort viennent avant*** <span style="color:green">[makes it so that mutilation and even death come before — the causative faire again (cf. fait se battre ¶2): the State produces precedence itself, reorders time]</span>. It needs them to be<br>***déjà faites*** <span style="color:green">[already done — the déjà chain begins: the fait/faire of accomplished fact, needed as pre-accomplished]</span>, and that men be born thus, infirm and zombies. The myth of the zombie, of the living-dead, is a myth of labor and not of war. |
| ¶4 fr.529 umn.425 | La mutilation est une conséquence de la guerre, mais une condition, un présupposé de l'appareil d'Etat et de l'organisation du travail (d'où l'infirmité native non seulement du travailleur, mais de l'homme d'Etat lui-même, du type Borgne ou Manchot) : « Cet étalage brutal de morceaux de chair coupée m'avait consterné. ( . . . ) N'était-ce pas une partie intégrante de la perfection technique et de son ivresse ( . . . ) ? Les hommes se font la guerre depuis les premiers temps, mais je ne me souviens pas dans toute l'Iliade d'un seul exemple où un guerrier ait perdu un bras ou une jambe. Le mythe réservait les mutilations aux monstres, aux bêtes humaines de la race de Tantale ou de Procuste. ( . . . ) C'est une illusion d'optique qui nous fait ramener ces mutilations à l'accident. En fait, les accidents procèdent des mutilations qu'ont déjà subies les germes de notre monde ; et l'accroissement numérique des amputations est l'un des symptômes qui trahissent le triomphe de la morale du scalpel. La perte a eu lieu bien avant d'entrer visiblement en ligne de compte . . . 6 » C'est l'appareil d'Etat qui a besoin, à son sommet comme à sa base, d'handicapés préalables, de mutilés préexistants ou de morts-nés, d'infirmes congénitaux, de borgnes et de manchots. Alors, il y aurait une hypothèse tentante, en trois temps : la machine de guerre serait « entre » les deux pôles de la souve5. Idem, pp. 124-132. 6. Jünger, Abeilles de verre, Bourgois, p . 182. A PPAREIL DE CAPTURE rai lle t pol i t i q u e , et assurerait le passage d'un pôle à l'autre. C 'est bien dans cet ordre, 1 - 2 - 3 , que les choses semblent se présen ter dans le mythe ou dans l'histoire. | Mutilation is a consequence of war, but it is a necessary condition, a presupposition of the State apparatus and the organization of work (hence the native infirmity not only of the worker but also of the man of State himself, whether of the One-Eyed or the One-Armed type): "The brutal exhibition of severed flesh shocked me . . . . Wasn't it an integral part of technical per- : <br><br>Thus there is a tempting three-part hypothesis: the war machine is "between" the two poles of political sovereignty and assures the passage from one pole to the other. It is indeed in that order, 1 -2-3, that things seem to present themselves in myth and history. | [Massumi likely used the Miller translation of Junger, rather than translating the French] Mutilation is a consequence of war, but a<br>***condition, un présupposé*** <span style="color:green">[a condition, a presupposition — the pre- chain opens: présupposé, préalable, préexistant, all in this paragraph; the State's time runs backward]</span> of the State apparatus and of the organization of labor (<br>***d'où*** <span style="color:green">[whence — logical derivation, the geometer's "from which follows"]</span> the native infirmity not only of the worker, but of the man of State himself, of the Borgne or Manchot type): « This brutal display of pieces of cut flesh had dismayed me. (…) Was it not an integral part of technical perfection and its intoxication (…)? Men<br>***se font la guerre*** <span style="color:green">[make war on one another — se faire la guerre, the set phrase; the faire-causative family again: war as something made]</span> since the earliest times, but I do not recall in the whole Iliad a single example where a warrior lost an arm or a leg. Myth reserved mutilations for monsters, for the human beasts of the race of Tantalus or Procrustes. (…) It is an optical illusion that makes us<br>***ramener*** <span style="color:green">[bring back — reduction: lead the phenomenon back to a lesser cause]</span> these mutilations to accident. In fact, accidents proceed from the mutilations already undergone by the<br>***germes*** <span style="color:green">[germs, seeds — embryonic register: the seed-stock of our world, already mutilated]</span> of our world; and the numerical increase of amputations is one of the symptoms that betray the triumph of the morality of the scalpel. The loss<br>***a eu lieu*** <span style="color:green">[took place — avoir lieu: had its place, occurred as event]</span> well before<br>***entrer visiblement en ligne de compte***…<br>⁶<br>» <span style="color:green">[entering visibly into the reckoning — the accounting idiom: figuring in the tally; head of the compte chain that runs to ¶16's pour leur compte and the plateau's fiscal apparatus of rente and impôt]</span> <span style="color:green">[translated from the supplied French pending verified English edition]</span> It is the State apparatus that needs, at its summit as at its base,<br>***préalables*** handicapped ones <span style="color:green">[prior — préalable: the pre- chain again, the administrative word for prerequisite]</span>, preexisting<br>***mutilés*** <span style="color:green">[mutilated men — also the fixed post-1918 French term for disabled veterans, mutilés de guerre]</span> or stillborn ones, congenital infirm ones, borgnes and manchots. So,<br>***il y aurait une hypothèse tentante*** <span style="color:green">[there would be a tempting hypothesis — conditional of the staged proposal: floated to be complicated, not asserted]</span>,<br>***en trois temps*** <span style="color:green">[in three beats — musical/temporal register: a three-stroke movement, tempo not mere enumeration]</span>: the war machine would be « between » the two poles of political sovereignty, and would<br>***assurer le passage*** <span style="color:green">[assure the passage — assurer, also the underwriter's register: to insure, guarantee against risk; the war machine as guarantor of the crossing]</span> from one pole to the other. It is indeed in this order, 1-2-3, that things seem to present themselves in myth or in history.<br><br>⁵<br>Idem, pp. 124–132. <span style="color:green">(text for ¶3's marker, delivered in this cell — segmentation note)</span><br><br>⁶<br>Jünger, *Abeilles de verre*, Bourgois, p. 182. |
| ¶5 fr.530 umn.426 | Soit deux versions du Borgne et du Manchot analysées par Dumézil : 1 ) Le dieu Oddhin , à l 'œil unique, ligote ou lie le Loup de guerre, le prend dans son lien magique ; 2 ) mais le loup se méfiait, et disposait de toute sa puissance d'extériorité ; 3 ) le dieu Tyr donne un gage juridique au loup, il lui laisse une main dans la gueule, pour que le loup puisse la couper s'il n'arrive pas à se défaire du lien. - 1 ) Horatius Coclès, le borgne, par son seul visage, sa grimace et sa puissance magique, empêche le chef étrusque de donner l'assaut à Rome ; 2 ) le chef de guerre décide alors de faire le siège ; 3 ) Mucius Scaevola prend le relais politique, et donne sa main en gage, pour persuader le guerrier qu'il vaut mieux renoncer au siège et conclure un pacte. - Dans un tout autre contexte, historique, Marcel Détienne suggère un schéma en trois temps d'un type analogue pour la Grèce antique : 1 ) Le souverain magique, le « Maître de vérité », dispose d'une machine de guerre, qui ne vient sans doute pas de lui-même, et qui jouit dans son empire d'une relative autonomie ; 2 ) cette classe de guerriers a des règles qui lui sont propres, définies par une « isonomie », un espace isotrope, un « milieu » ( le butin est au milieu, celui qui parle se met au milieu de l'assemblée) : c'est un autre espace, ce sont d'autres règles que celles du souverain, qui capture et qui parle d'en haut ; 3 ) la réforme hoplitique, préparée dans la classe guerrière, va essaimer dans l'ensemble du corps social, promouvoir une armée de soldats-citoyens, en même temps que les derniers restes d'un pôle impérial de la souveraineté font place au pôle juridique de l'Etat-cité (isonomie comme loi, isotropie comme espace 7). Voilà que, dans tous ces cas, la machine de guerre semble intervenir « entre » les deux pôles de l'appareil d'Etat, pour assurer et nécessiter le passage de l'un à l'autre. | Take two versions of the OneEyed and the One-Armed gods analyzed by Dumezil: ( 1 ) the god Odin, who has a single eye, ties up the wolf of war and holds him in his magic bond; (2) but the wolfis wary and has at its disposal all its power ofexteriority; (3) the god Tyr gives the wolf a legal security by leaving one of his hands in the wolfs mouth so the wolf can bite it off if it does not succeed in extricating itself from the bond. ( 1 ) Horatius Cocles, the One-Eyed, using only his face, his grimace and magic power, prevents the Etruscan commander from attacking Rome; (2) the war commander then decides to lay siege; (3) Mucius Scaevola takes a political tack, offering his hand as a security in order to persuade the warrior that it would be best to abandon the siege and conclude a pact.<br><br>In an entirely different, historical, context, Marcel Detienne suggests an analogous schema in three moments for ancient Greece: ( 1 ) The magic sovereign, the "Master of Truth," has at his disposal a war machine that doubtless does not originate with him, and which enjoys a relative autonomy within his empire; (2) this class of warriors has its own rules, defined by "isonomy," an isotropic space, and a "milieu" (war spoils are in the middle [au milieu], he who speaks places himself in the middle of the assembly); this is another space, the rules are diff erent from those of the sovereign, who captures and speaks from on high; (3) the hoplite reform, the groundwork for which was laid in the warrior class, spread throughout the social body, promoting the formation of an army of citizen-soldiers; at the same time, the last vestiges of the imperial pole of sovereignty were replaced by the juridical pole of the city-state (with isonomy as its law, and isotropy as its space). 7 Thus in every case, the war machine seems to intervene "between" the two poles of the State apparatus, assuring and necessitating the passage from one to the other. | Take two versions of the Borgne and the Manchot analyzed by Dumézil: 1) The god Oddhin, with the single eye,<br>***ligote ou lie*** <span style="color:green">[trusses up or binds — ligoter, rope-binding of a captive, doubled with lier, the bind-chain term]</span> the Wolf of war, takes him in his magic lien; 2) but the wolf<br>***se méfiait, et disposait de*** <span style="color:green">[was wary, and disposed of — imperfect tense: standing vigilance; disposer de, the legal-military idiom "to have at one's disposal," the disposer chain that returns below with the sovereign's war machine]</span> all its power of exteriority; 3) the god Tyr gives a juridical<br>***gage*** <span style="color:green">[pledge, gage — the thing left in pawn; also the gage of the duel: surety deposited in advance]</span> to the wolf, he leaves it a hand in its maw, so that the wolf can<br>***la couper*** <span style="color:green">[cut it off — couper, clean instrumental severing: the cut that answers a bond one cannot untie, vs. the délieur's défaire ¶3]</span> if it does not manage to<br>***se défaire du lien*** <span style="color:green">[undo itself from the bond — se défaire de: to get free of by untying; the unbind option the cut replaces]</span>. — 1) Horatius Cocles, the borgne, by his face alone, his grimace and his magic power, prevents the Etruscan chief from<br>***donner l'assaut*** <span style="color:green">[giving the assault — the military set phrase: ordering the storm]</span> on Rome; 2) the war chief then decides to<br>***faire le siège*** <span style="color:green">[lay the siege — the paired set phrase: from storm to siege, force to duration]</span>; 3) Mucius Scaevola<br>***prend le relais*** <span style="color:green">[takes over the relay — relay-race and post-horse register: relieves, takes the handoff]</span> politically, and<br>***donne sa main en gage*** <span style="color:green">[gives his hand as gage — donner sa main is also the marriage idiom, to give one's hand: the pledge-gesture and the espousal-gesture in one, sealing the pact as ¶3's meilleur et pire sealed the vow]</span>, to persuade the warrior that<br>***il vaut mieux*** <span style="color:green">[it is better to — the weighing idiom: valoir, worth; the warrior computes value, renouncing siege for pact]</span> renounce the siege and conclude a pact. — In an entirely different, historical context, Marcel Détienne suggests a schema en trois temps of an analogous type for ancient Greece: 1) The magic sovereign, the « Master of truth »,<br>***dispose d'une machine de guerre*** <span style="color:green">[disposes of a war machine — disposer de again: at his disposal, not his own; the wolf's word now the sovereign's]</span>, which doubtless does not come from himself, and which<br>***jouit dans son empire d'une relative autonomie*** <span style="color:green">[enjoys in his empire a relative autonomy — jouir de: legal enjoyment, the usufruct register: holding the use of a right without owning it]</span>; 2) this class of warriors has rules that are its own, defined by an « isonomy », an isotropic space, a «<br>***milieu*** » <span style="color:green">[middle, milieu — both central position and surrounding medium; the parenthesis spells the spatial sense]</span> (the<br>***butin*** <span style="color:green">[booty — the butin chain of ¶2: war-capture placed in common, in the middle, against the despot's stock]</span> is au milieu, he who speaks places himself au milieu of the assembly): it is another space, they are other rules than those of the sovereign, who captures and who speaks<br>***d'en haut*** <span style="color:green">[from on high — the vertical position-word paired against au milieu: height vs. middle as two political geometries]</span>; 3) the hoplite reform, prepared within the warrior class, will<br>***essaimer*** <span style="color:green">[swarm — apiary register: hive off in swarms through the social body]</span> in the whole of the social body, promote an army of soldier-citizens, at the same time as the last remains of an imperial pole of sovereignty<br>***font place au*** <span style="color:green">[make place for — yield their place to: the spatial idiom of succession, one pole stepping aside]</span> the juridical pole of the city-State (isonomy as law, isotropy as space<br>⁷<br>). And so, in all these cases, the war machine seems to intervene « between » the two poles of the State apparatus, to assure and necessitate the passage from the one to the other. |
| ¶6 fr.530 umn.426 | On ne peut pas toutefois donner à ce schéma un sens causal ( et les auteurs invoqués ne le font pas ). En premier lieu, la machine de guerre n'explique rien ; car ou bien elle est extérieure à l'Etat, et dirigée contre lui ; ou bien elle lui appartient déjà, encastée ou appropriée, et elle le suppose. Si elle intervient dans une évolution de l'Etat, c'est donc nécessairement en conjonction avec d'autres facteurs internes. Et c'est ce qui apparaît en second lieu : s'il y a une évolution de l'Etat, il faut bien que le second pôle, le pôle évolué, soit en résonance avec le pre7. Marcel Detienne, Les maîtres de vérité . . . ; et « La phalange, problèmes et controverses » (in Problèmes de la guerre en Grèce ancienne, Mouton). Cf. aussi J.-P. Vernant, Les origines de la pensée grecque. 53 1 mier, qu'il ne cesse de le recharger d'une certaine manière, et que l'Etat n'ait qu'un seul milieu d'intériorité, c'est-à-dire une unité de composition) malgré toutes les différences d'organisation et de développement des Etats. Il faut même que chaque Etat ait les deux pôles, comme les moments essentiels de son existence, bien que l'organisation des deux varie. En troisième lieu, si l'on appelle « capture » cette essence intérieure ou cette unité de l'Etat, nous devons dire que les mots de « capture magique » décrivent bien la situation, puisqu'elle apparaît toujours comme déjà faite et se présupposant ; mais comment l'expliquer dès lors, si elle ne se rapporte à aucune cause assignable distincte ? C'est pourquoi les thèses sur l'origine de l'Etat sont toujours tautologiques. | We cannot, however, assign this schema a causal meaning (the authors cited do not do SO). In the first place, the war machine explains nothing; for it is either exterior to the State, and directed against it; or else it already belongs to the State, encasted and appropriated, and presupposes it. If the war machine has a part in the evolution of the State, it is therefore necessarily in conjunction with other internal factors. And this is the second point: if there is an evolution of the State, the second pole, the evolved pole, must be in resonance with the first, it must continually recharge it in some way, and the State must have only one milieu of interiority; in other words, it must have a unity of composition, in spite of all the differences in organization and development among States. It is even necessary for each State to have both poles, as the essential moments of its existence, even though the organization of the two varies. Third, if we call this interior essence or this unity of the State "capture," we must say that the words "magic capture" describe the situation well because it always appears as preaccomplished and self -presupposing; but how is this capture to be explained then, if it leads back to no distinct assignable cause? That is why theses on the origin of the State are always tautological. | One cannot however give this schema a causal sense (and the authors<br>***invoqués*** <span style="color:green">[invoked — the legal register: to invoke an authority, cite in one's support; the scholars summoned as one invokes precedent]</span> do not do so). In the first place, the war machine explains nothing; for either it is exterior to the State, and directed against it; or else it<br>***lui appartient déjà*** <span style="color:green">[already belongs to it — appartenir: ownership language, the déjà chain in property form]</span>, encastée or appropriated, and it<br>***le suppose*** <span style="color:green">[supposes it — the supposer chain opens: the war machine supposes the State, as property will suppose public property, money the tax (¶7)]</span>. If it intervenes in an evolution of the State, it is therefore necessarily in conjunction with other internal factors. And that is what appears in the second place: if there is an evolution of the State,<br>***il faut bien que*** <span style="color:green">[it must indeed be that — il faut bien: the concessive-necessity idiom, "there's no getting around it": necessity granted under protest]</span> the second pole, the evolved pole, be in resonance with the first, that it<br>***ne cesse de le recharger*** <span style="color:green">[does not cease to recharge it — ne cesser de, the plateau's continuous-aspect formula (cf. ¶1 ne cessent pas, ¶3 ne cesse pas); recharger: electrical and reloading register, the archaic pole kept charged, never superseded]</span> in a certain manner, and that the State have only one milieu of interiority, that is to say a unity of composition, despite all the differences of organization and development among States.<br>***Il faut même que*** <span style="color:green">[it must even be that — the necessity escalated: falloir twice, the argument proceeding by constraint, not observation]</span> each State have the two poles, as the essential moments of its existence, although the organization of the two varies. In the third place, if one calls « capture » this interior essence or this unity of the State, we must say that the words « magic capture »<br>***décrivent bien la situation*** <span style="color:green">[describe the situation well — bien as concessive grant: the words do fit, precisely because the thing is inexplicable]</span>, since it always appears as<br>***déjà faite et se présupposant*** <span style="color:green">[already done and presupposing itself — the déjà chain meets the pre- chain of ¶4: accomplished fact that is its own prerequisite, the tautology stated in two idioms]</span>; but how to explain it<br>***dès lors*** <span style="color:green">[from then on, that being so — the temporal-logical hinge: once this is granted, explanation is foreclosed]</span>, if it<br>***se rapporte à*** <span style="color:green">[relates back to, refers to — relation of reference, not a trail to be followed back]</span> no distinct assignable cause? That is why theses on the origin of the State are always tautological.<br><br>⁷<br>Marcel Détienne, *Les maîtres de vérité…*; and « La phalange, problèmes et controverses » (in *Problèmes de la guerre en Grèce ancienne*, Mouton). Cf. also J.-P. Vernant, *Les origines de la pensée grecque*. |
| ¶7 fr.531 umn.427 | Tantôt l'on invoque des facteurs exogènes, liés à la guerre et à la machine de guerre ; tantôt des facteurs endogènes, qui feraient naître la propriété privée, la monnaie, etc. ; tantôt enfin des facteurs spécifiques qui détermineraient la formation de « fonctions publiques » . On trouve les trois thèses chez Engels, suivant une conception de la diversité des voies de la Domination. Mais elles supposent ce qui est en question. La guerre ne produit d'Etat que si l'une des deux parties au moins est un Etat préalable ; et l'organisation de guerre n'est facteur d'Etat que si elle lui appartient. Ou bien l'Etat ne comporte pas de machine de guerre (il a des policiers et des geôliers avant d'avoir des soldats), ou bien il en comporte, mais sous forme d'institution militaire ou de fonction publique 8. De même la propriété privée suppose une propriété publique d'Etat, elle coule à travers ses mailles ; et la monnaie suppose l'impôt. Et l'on voit plus mal encore comment des fonctions publiques pourraient préexister à l'Etat qu'elles impliquent. On est toujours renvoyé à un Etat qui naît adulte et qui surgit d'un coup, Urstaat inconditionné. | At times, exogenous factors, tied to war and the war machine, are invoked; at times endogenous factors, thought to engender private property, money, etc.; and at times specific factors, thought to determine the formation of "public functions." All three of these theses are found in Engels, in relation to a conception of the diversity of the roads to Domination. But they beg the question. War produces the State only if at least one of the two parts is a preexistent State; and the organization of war is a State factor only if that organization is a part of the State. Either the State has no war machine (and has policemen and jailers before having soldiers), or else it has one, but in the form of a military institution or public function.8 Similarly, private property presupposes State public property, it slips through its net; and money presupposes taxation. It is even more difficult to see how public functions could have existed before the State they imply. We are always brought back to the idea of a State that comes into the world fully formed and rises up in a single stroke, the unconditioned Urstaat. | Sometimes one<br>***invoque*** <span style="color:green">[invokes — the legal summons again (¶6, authors invoqués): factors called as authorities, and found insufficient]</span> exogenous factors, tied to war and the war machine; at times endogenous factors, which<br>***feraient naître*** <span style="color:green">[would give birth to — faire naître, the causative-of-birth idiom; conditional kept: alleged parentage]</span> private property, money, etc.; at times finally specific factors which<br>***détermineraient*** <span style="color:green">[would determine — conditional of the reported claim, second of the pair]</span> the formation of « public functions ». One finds the three theses in Engels, following a conception of the diversity of the<br>***voies de la Domination*** <span style="color:green">[ways of Domination — voie: the road register, also the legal voie (voie de droit, legal avenue); capital D in the French]</span>. But they<br>***supposent*** <span style="color:green">[suppose — the supposer chain of ¶6, now the refutation-engine: each thesis supposes what it must explain]</span> what is in question. War produces a State only if one of the two<br>***parties*** <span style="color:green">[parties — juridical: parties to a conflict or contract, not portions; confirmed by fn. 10's parties contractantes]</span> at least is a<br>***préalable*** State <span style="color:green">[prior — préalable: the pre- chain of ¶4, the administrative prerequisite]</span>; and the organization of war is a factor of State only if it belongs to the State. Either the State<br>***ne comporte pas de*** war machine <span style="color:green">[does not comprise — comporter: contain as component; the machine as included part or not, an inventory question]</span> (it has policemen and jailers before having soldiers), or else it comprises one, but under the form of a military institution or a public function<br>⁸<br>.<br>***De même*** <span style="color:green">[likewise — the parallel-case operator: the argument proceeds by matched instances, war/property/money]</span> private property supposes a State public property, it<br>***coule à travers ses mailles*** <span style="color:green">[flows through its meshes — mailles, the mesh of the net: the filet chain of ¶1–2; property as liquid running through State netting]</span>; and money supposes<br>***l'impôt*** <span style="color:green">[the tax — the compte chain: the fiscal apparatus behind money itself, developed at ¶9's rente]</span>. And<br>***l'on voit plus mal encore*** <span style="color:green">[one sees still worse — the voir idiom of comprehension graded by eyesight: each thesis harder to "see" than the last]</span> how public functions could preexist the State that they<br>***impliquent*** <span style="color:green">[imply — logical implication: the functions entail the State in their very concept]</span>.<br>***On est toujours renvoyé à*** <span style="color:green">[one is always sent back to — renvoyer: the procedural word, to remand, refer a case to another court; every origin-thesis remands the question to a prior State]</span> a State that<br>***naît adulte*** <span style="color:green">[is born adult — the birth idiom against faire naître above: no parentage, no infancy]</span> and that<br>***surgit d'un coup*** <span style="color:green">[looms up at a stroke — surgir, the apparition-verb of ¶2; d'un coup, the coup chain: all at once]</span>, unconditioned Urstaat. |
| ¶8 fr.532 umn.427 | Proposition XI " Ce qui est premier ?<br><br>Le premier pôle de capture, on l'appellera impérial ou despotique. Il correspond à la formation asiatique de Marx. L'archéologie 8. Jacques Harmand (La guerre antique) P. U. F., p. 28) cite « l'entre· prise à gros effectifs conduite singulièrement par un fonctionnaire civil, Ouni, wus le pharaon Pepi !('r vers 1400 » . Même la démocratie militaire telle que Morgan la décrivait suppose un Etat archaïque de type impérial, et ne l'explique pas (c'est ce qui résulte des travaux de Detienne et de Vernant). Cet Etat impérial lui-même procède d'abord avec des geôliers et des policiers, plutôt qu'avec des guerriers : cf. Dumézil, Mitra-Varuna) pp. 200-204. le découvre partout, souvent recouvert par l'oubli, à l'horizon de tous les systèmes ou Etats, non seulement en Asie, mais en Afrique, en Amérique, en Grèce, à Rome. Urstaat immémorial, dès le néolithique, et peut-être encore plus haut. Suivant la description marxiste : un appareil d'Etat s'érige sur les communautés primitives agricoles, gui ont déjà des codes lignagers-territoriaux ; mais il les surcode, les soumet au pouvoir d'un empereur despote, propriétaire public unique et transcendant, maître du surplus ou du stock, organisateur des grands travaux ( surtravail), source de fonctions publiques et de bureaucratie. C'est le paradigme du lien, du nœud. Tel est le régime de signes de l'Etat : le surcodage ou le Signifiant. C'est un système d'asservissement machinique : la première « mégamachine » à proprement parler, comme dit Mumford. Prodigieuse réussite en un coup : les autres Etats ne seront que des avortons par rapport à ce modèle. L'empereur-despote n'est pas un roi ou un tyran ; ceux-ci n'existeront qu'en fonction d'une propriété déjà privée 9. Tandis que tout est public dans le régime impérial : la possession de la terre y est communautaire, chacun ne possède qu'en tant que membre d'une communauté ; la propriété éminente du despote est celle de l'Unité supposée des communes ; et les fonctionnaires euxmêmes n'ont que des terres de fonction, même héréditaires. L'argent peut exister, notamment dans l'impôt que les fonctionnaires doivent à l'empereur, mais il sert pas à une vente-achat, puisque la terre n'existe pas comme marchandise aliénable. | PROPOSITION XI. Which comes first?<br><br>We shall call the first pole of capture imperial or despotic. It corresponds to Marx's Asiatic formation. Archaeology discovers it everywhere, often lost in oblivion, at the horizon of all systems or States-not only in Asia, but also in Africa, America, Greece, Rome. Immemorial Urstaat, dating as far back as Neolithic times, and perhaps farther still. Following the Marxist description: a State apparatus is erected upon the primitive agricultural communities, which already have lineal-territorial codes; but it overcodes : | Proposition XI:<br>***Ce qui est premier ?*** <span style="color:green">[What is first? — nominal: priority of essence, not sequence; not "which comes first"]</span><br>The first pole of capture, one will call it imperial or despotic. It corresponds to Marx's Asiatic formation. Archaeology discovers it everywhere, often<br>***recouvert*** <span style="color:green">[covered over — archaeological layering: buried under forgetting, not lost in it]</span> by forgetting, at the horizon of all systems or States, not only in Asia, but in Africa, in America, in Greece, at Rome. Immemorial Urstaat, from the Neolithic on, and perhaps from<br>***encore plus haut*** <span style="color:green">[higher still — the stratigrapher's vertical: earlier as higher up the dig, time measured in layers]</span>. Following the Marxist description: a State apparatus<br>***s'érige sur*** <span style="color:green">[erects itself upon — ériger: the monument register, a thing raised; reflexive: self-raising, no builder named]</span> the primitive agricultural communities, which already have lineage-territorial codes; but it<br>***surcode*** <span style="color:green">[overcodes — sur-, over: a coding laid on top of codes already there; the plateau's regime-of-signs term]</span> them,<br>***soumet au pouvoir*** <span style="color:green">[submits to the power — soumettre: subjection as formal act, the vocabulary of vassalage]</span> of a despot emperor, unique and transcendent public proprietor,<br>***maître du surplus ou du stock*** <span style="color:green">[master of the surplus or the stock — maître de: title of ownership and command, the ¶5 Master of truth now master of grain]</span>, organizer of the<br>***grands travaux*** <span style="color:green">[great works — the ¶1 set phrase returns: State public works, corvée register]</span> (<br>***surtravail*** <span style="color:green">[surplus labor — Marx's Mehrarbeit; the same sur- as surcodage: labor over and above]</span>), source of public<br>***fonctions*** and of bureaucracy <span style="color:green">[functions — the fonction chain opens: fonctions publiques, fonctionnaires, terres de fonction, en fonction de — office, official, office-land, and the mathematical "as a function of," one word through all four]</span>. It is the paradigm of the lien, of the knot. Such is the regime of signs of the State: surcodage, or the Signifier. It is a system of<br>***asservissement machinique*** <span style="color:green">[machinic enslavement — asservir, to enslave; also the engineering register: to make into a servomechanism, slaved components]</span>: the first « megamachine »<br>***à proprement parler*** <span style="color:green">[properly speaking — the parler idiom family (¶2 parler trop vite): speech policing its own exactness]</span>, as Mumford says. Prodigious<br>***réussite en un coup*** <span style="color:green">[success at one stroke — the coup chain: cf. the Urstaat surging d'un coup, ¶7]</span>: the other States will be only<br>***avortons*** <span style="color:green">[runts, abortlings — avorter, to abort: States as miscarriages of the model; the birth-frame of ¶7 continued — born adult vs. born runt]</span> in relation to this model. The emperor-despot is not a king or a tyrant; those will exist only<br>***en fonction d'une propriété déjà privée***⁹<br><span style="color:green">[as a function of a property already private — the fonction chain gone mathematical: king and tyrant as dependent variables of private property; déjà chain]</span>. Whereas everything is public in the imperial regime: possession of the land there is communal, each possesses only<br>***en tant que*** member of a community <span style="color:green">[in the capacity of — the title-formula family (au titre de, ¶16): possession by status, not person]</span>; the<br>***propriété éminente*** <span style="color:green">[eminent property — legal term of art: dominium eminens, the overlord's superior title above all holdings]</span> of the despot is that of the supposed Unity of the communes; and the<br>***fonctionnaires*** themselves have only<br>***terres de fonction*** <span style="color:green">[function-lands — lands attached to the office, prebendal tenure: the fonction chain in soil]</span>, even hereditary ones. Money can exist, notably in<br>***l'impôt*** <span style="color:green">[the tax — the compte chain: the fiscal relation preceding commerce, developed at ¶9's rente]</span> that the functionaries owe the emperor, but it does not serve a<br>***vente-achat*** <span style="color:green">[sale-purchase — the hyphenated pair as one act: commerce as a single two-faced operation, not yet existing]</span>, since the land does not exist as alienable merchandise.<br><br>⁸<br>Jacques Harmand (*La guerre antique*, P.U.F., p. 28) cites « the large-scale enterprise conducted singularly by a civil functionary, Uni, under the pharaoh Pepi I, around 1400 ». <span style="color:green">[Harmand is French-original: translated from the supplied French. Date flag stands: "vers 1400" almost certainly OCR or edition error — Uni served Pepi I, c. 2300 B.C.; check print Minuit for « vers 2400 »; if print reads 1400, preserve with sic]</span> Even military democracy as Morgan described it supposes an archaic State of imperial type, and does not explain it (this is what results from the works of Détienne and Vernant). This imperial State itself proceeds at first with jailers and policemen, rather than with warriors: cf. Dumézil, *Mitra-Varuna*, pp. 200–204. <span style="color:green">(text for ¶7's marker, delivered here)</span> |
| ¶9 fr.532 umn.428 | C'est le régime du nexum, le lien : quelque chose est prêté ou même donné sans transfert de propriété, sans appropriation privée, et dont la contrepartie ne présente pas un intérêt ni un profit pour le donateur, mais plutôt une « rente » qui lui revient, accompagnant le prêt d'usage ou la donation de revenu 10 . 9. L'idée même d'une formation despotique asiatique apparaît au xvnre siècle, notamment chez Montesquieu, mais pour décrire un état évolué des empires, et en rorrespondance avec la monarchie absolue. Tout autre est le point de vue de Marx, qui recrée la notion pour définir les empires archaïques. Les textes principaux à cet égard sont : Marx, Grundrisse, Pléiade II, pp. 312 sq. ; Wittfogel, Le despotisme oriental, Ed. de Minuit (et l'avant-propos de Vidal-Naquet dans la première édition, mais qui fut supprimé dans la seconde à la demande de Wittfogel) ; Tokei, Sur le mode de production asiatique, Studia historica 1966 ; l'étude d'ensemble du C. E. R. M., Sur le mode de production asiatique\ Ed. Sociales. 10. Varron faisait un jeu de mots célèbre entre nexum et nec suum fit ( = la chose ne devient pas la propriété de celui qui la reçoit). En effet, le nexttm est une forme fondamentale du droit romain archaïque, où ce qui oblige n'est pas un accord entre parties contractantes, mais uniquement la parole du prêteur ou du donateur, sur un mode magico-religieux. | This is the regime of the nexum, the bond: something is lent or even given without a transfer of ownership, without private appropriation, and the compensation for it does not come in the form of interest or profit for the donor but rather as a "rent" that accrues to him, accompanying the lending of something for another's use or the granting of revenue. 1 0 | It is the regime of the<br>***nexum*** <span style="color:green">[nexum — archaic Roman-law bond of the debtor; Latin nectere, to bind: the lien/knot chain in juridical form]</span>, the lien: something is<br>***prêté ou même donné*** <span style="color:green">[lent or even given — the prêter/donner pair: two grades of one gesture, both without alienation; the pair returns below as prêt d'usage / donation de revenu, lender / donor]</span> without transfer of property, without private appropriation, and whose<br>***contrepartie*** <span style="color:green">[counterpart, counter-performance — the bookkeeper's contrepartie, the balancing entry on the other side of the ledger: the compte chain, not "compensation" for a loss]</span> presents neither an<br>***intérêt*** nor a<br>***profit*** for the donor <span style="color:green">[interest nor profit — the two commercial returns named to be excluded: the fiscal relation defined by subtraction, no usury, no margin]</span>, but rather a «<br>***rente*** » <span style="color:green">[rent — the economic term of art: return owed without sale, interest, or profit; ground-rent register, a keyword of this plateau]</span> that<br>***lui revient*** <span style="color:green">[comes back to him — revenir: what returns by right, the idiom of rightful accrual (cela lui revient, "that is his due"); sounding revenu, revenue: the return that returns as income]</span>, accompanying the<br>***prêt d'usage*** <span style="color:green">[loan for use — commodatum: the use is lent, the thing is not alienated; usage, the usufruct register of ¶5's jouit]</span> or the<br>***donation de revenu***¹<br>⁰<br><span style="color:green">[donation of revenue — the gift whose content is income itself: giving the flow, keeping the source]</span>.<br><br>⁹<br>The very idea of an Asiatic despotic formation appears in the eighteenth century, notably in Montesquieu, but to describe an<br>***état évolué*** of the empires <span style="color:green">[evolved state — état, lowercase: condition, stage; the same word as État hovering under it — a "state of the empires" that is not yet the State]</span>, and in correspondence with absolute monarchy.<br>***Tout autre est le point de vue de Marx*** <span style="color:green">[entirely other is Marx's point of view — inverted attack, predicate first: the treatise's gesture of turning on its heel]</span>, who recreates the notion to define the archaic empires. The principal texts in this regard are: Marx, *Grundrisse*, Pléiade II, pp. 312 ff.; Wittfogel, *Le despotisme oriental*, Éd. de Minuit (and the foreword by Vidal-Naquet in the first edition, but which was suppressed in the second at Wittfogel's request); Tokei, *Sur le mode de production asiatique*, Studia historica 1966; the collective study of the C.E.R.M., *Sur le mode de production asiatique*, Éd. Sociales. <span style="color:green">[Marx flag closed as bibliographic — no wording to verify]</span> <span style="color:green">(text for ¶8's marker, delivered here)</span><br>¹<br>⁰<br>Varro made a famous<br>***jeu de mots*** <span style="color:green">[play on words — D&G naming wordplay as such: the pun as juridical evidence]</span> between nexum and nec suum fit (= the thing does not become the property of the one who receives it). Indeed, the nexum is a fundamental form of archaic Roman law, where<br>***ce qui oblige*** <span style="color:green">[what obliges — obliger: the root of obligation, ob-ligare, to bind toward: the lien chain hiding inside legal "obligation" itself]</span> is not an accord between contracting<br>***parties***, but solely<br>***la parole*** of the lender or of the donor <span style="color:green">[the word — parole: the spoken word as binding force, the magico-religious speech-act; the same parole that ¶13 will make presuppose the State]</span>, on a magico-religious mode. |
| ¶10 fr.532 umn.428 | Ce n'est pas un contrat (mancipatio), et il ne comporte ni vente-achat, même différé, ni intérêt, bien qu'il puisse, nous semble-t-il, comporter une Marx historien, Childe archéologue, s'accordent sur le point suivant : l'Etat impérial archaïque, qui vient surcoder des communautés agricoles, suppose au moins un certain développement de leurs forces productives, puisqu'il faut un surplus potentiel capable de constituer le stock d'Etat, d'entretenir un artisanat spécialisé (métallurgie), et de susciter progressivement des fonctions publiques. Ce pourquoi Marx liait l'Etat archaïque à un certain < mode de production » . Toutefois, on n'a pas fini de reculer dans le temps l'origine de ces Etats néolithiques. Or, quand on conjecture des empires presque paléolithiques, il ne s'agit pas seulement d'une quantité de temps, c'est le problème qualitatif qui change. Çatal-Hüyük, en Anatolie, rend possible un paradigme impérial singulièrement renforcé : c'est un stock de graines sauvages et d'animaux relativement paisibles, issus de territoires différents, qui opère et permet d'opérer, d'abord au hasard, des hybridations et des sélections d'où l'agriculture et le petit élevage sortiront 1 1 . On voit l'importance de ce changement dans les données du problème. Ce n'est plus le stock qui suppose un surplus potentiel, mais l'inverse. Ce n'est plus l'Etat qui suppose des communautés agricoles élaborées, et des forces productives développées ; au contraire, il s'établit directement dans un milieu de cueilleurs-chasseurs sans agriculture ni métallurgie préalables, et c'est lui qui crée l'agriculture, l'élevage et la métallurgie, d'abord sur son propre sol, puis les impose au monde environnant. Ce n'est plus la campagne qui crée progressivement la ville, c'est la ville qui crée la campagne. | Marx, the historian, and Childe, the archaeologist, are in agreement on the following point: the archaic imperial State, which steps in to overcode agricultural communities, presupposes at least a certain level of development of these communities' productive forces since there must be a potential surplus capable of constituting a State stock, of supporting a specialized handicrafts class (metallurgy), and of progressively giving rise to public functions. That is why Marx links the archaic State to a certain "mode of production." However, the origin of these Neolithic States is still being pushed back in time. What is at issue when the existence of nearPaleolithic empires is conjectured is not simply the quantity of time; the qualitative problem changes. (atal Htiytik, in Anatolia, makes possible a singularly reinforced imperial paradigm: it is a stock of uncultivated seeds and relatively tame animals from diff erent territories that perf orms, and makes it possible to perform, at first by chance, hybridizations and selections from which agriculture and small-scale animal raising arise. I I It is easy to see the significance of this change in the givens of the problem. It is no longer the stock that presupposes a potential surplus, but the other way around. It is no longer the State that presupposes advanced agricultural communities and developed forces of production. On the contrary, the State is established directly in a milieu of hunter-gatherers having no prior 7000 B.C.: | It is not a contract (<br>***mancipatio***) <span style="color:green">[mancipatio — Roman law: the formal conveyance by bronze and scales, transfer of ownership proper; named to mark exactly what the nexum is not]</span>, and it<br>***comporte*** neither<br>***vente-achat, même différé***, nor interest <span style="color:green">[comprises neither sale-purchase, even deferred — the ¶8 pair again; différé: the credit-sale, purchase postponed — even commerce-on-delay is excluded]</span>, although it can,<br>***nous semble-t-il***, comprise a… <span style="color:green">[it seems to us — the authors' hedge, rare first-person plural: D&G marking their own conjecture inside a legal exposition]</span><br>Marx the historian, Childe the archaeologist,<br>***s'accordent sur le point suivant*** <span style="color:green">[accord themselves on the following point — s'accorder: the treaty-word, parties reaching accord (¶9 fn.: accord entre parties); historian and archaeologist as contracting parties]</span>: the archaic imperial State, which<br>***vient surcoder*** <span style="color:green">[comes to overcode — venir + infinitive: the State arrives upon communities already there, overcoding as intervention from outside]</span> agricultural communities, supposes at least a certain development of their productive forces,<br>***puisqu'il faut*** <span style="color:green">[since there must be — falloir again (¶6): the argument by necessity, here granted to the adversary's case]</span> a potential surplus capable of<br>***constituer le stock d'État*** <span style="color:green">[constituting the State stock — stock: the granary register, the despot's mastered reserve (¶8 maître du stock)]</span>, of<br>***entretenir*** a specialized artisanry <span style="color:green">[maintaining — entretenir: to keep up, keep fed: the State as household head provisioning its crafts]</span> (metallurgy), and of progressively<br>***susciter*** public functions <span style="color:green">[calling forth — susciter: to raise up, summon into being; near-theological register, cf. s'ériger ¶8]</span>.<br>***Ce pourquoi*** <span style="color:green">[which is why — clipped connective, drier than "c'est pourquoi": the treatise's shorthand]</span> Marx tied the archaic State to a certain « mode of production ». However,<br>***on n'a pas fini de reculer dans le temps*** <span style="color:green">[one has not finished pushing back in time — n'avoir pas fini de: the idiom of the still-unrolling process, "we haven't seen the end of it"; reculer: the origin retreating as the diggers advance]</span> the origin of these Neolithic States. Now, when one<br>***conjecture*** almost-Paleolithic empires <span style="color:green">[conjectures — the mathematician's verb: empires posited as conjecture, awaiting proof]</span>,<br>***il ne s'agit pas seulement de*** a quantity of time <span style="color:green">[it is not a matter only of — s'agir de: the impersonal what-is-at-stake formula, tracked on-machinery]</span>, it is the qualitative problem that changes. Çatal Hüyük, in Anatolia,<br>***rend possible*** <span style="color:green">[renders possible — the causative rendre: the site does not prove but enables the paradigm]</span> a singularly reinforced imperial paradigm: it is a<br>***stock*** of wild seeds and of relatively<br>***paisibles*** animals <span style="color:green">[peaceable — temperament, not domestication: not yet tame, merely tractable; the stock precedes the breeding]</span>,<br>***issus de*** different territories <span style="color:green">[issuing from — issu de: born of, descended from: the birth-frame again, seeds and beasts with territorial parentage]</span>, that<br>***opère et permet d'opérer, d'abord au hasard*** <span style="color:green">[operates and permits operating, at first by chance — opérer, the ¶1 emperor's verb now the stock's own; au hasard: the aleatory, selection before any selector]</span>, hybridizations and selections from which agriculture and small<br>***élevage*** will come out¹¹ <span style="color:green">[stock-raising — élevage, from élever, to raise: rearing as elevation, the herd raised as the State s'érige]</span>. One sees the importance of this change in the<br>***données*** of the problem <span style="color:green">[givens — the data of a problem, schoolbook register: what the problem statement supplies]</span>. It is no longer the stock that supposes a potential surplus, but<br>***l'inverse*** <span style="color:green">[the inverse — the mathematical flip: the plateau's recurring operator (¶11 twice), whole causal orders reversed in one word]</span>. It is no longer the State that supposes elaborated agricultural communities, and developed productive forces; on the contrary, it<br>***s'établit directement*** <span style="color:green">[establishes itself directly — the reflexive again (s'érige ¶8): self-installing, no prior ground needed]</span> in a milieu of<br>***cueilleurs-chasseurs*** <span style="color:green">[gatherers-hunters — French order: gathering first, the seed-stock not the hunt as the State's material]</span> without<br>***préalables*** agriculture or metallurgy <span style="color:green">[prior — préalable: the pre- chain, prerequisite denied]</span>, and it is the State that creates agriculture, élevage and metallurgy, at first<br>***sur son propre sol*** <span style="color:green">[on its own soil — the proprietary ground: the State experiments at home, then exports]</span>, then imposes them on the surrounding world. It is no longer the countryside that progressively creates the town, it is the town that creates the countryside. |
| ¶11 fr.533 umn.429 | Ce n'est pas l'Etat qui suppose un mode de production, mais l'inverse, c'est l'Etat qui fait de la production un « mode » . Les dernières raisons de supposer un développement progressif s'annulent. C'est comme les graines dans un sac : tout commence par un mélange au hasard. La « révolution étatique et urbaine » peut être paléolithique, et non pas néolithique comme le croyait Childe. L'évolutionnisme a été mis en question de multiples façons sorte de rente. Cf. notamment Pierre Noailles, Fas et Jus, Les Belles Lettres ; et Dumézil, qui insiste sur le rapport du nexum et du lien magique, Mitra-Varuna, pp. 1 18-124. 1 1 . Cf. les fouilles et travaux de ]. Mellaart, Earlie st Civilizations in the Near East, et Çatal Hüyük, Londres. L'urbaniste Jane Jacobs en a tiré un modèle impérial qu'elle appelle « Nouvelle Obsidienne » (du nom des laves qui servaient à faire des outils), et qui pourrait remonter au début du néolithique et même beaucoup plus haut. Elle insiste sur l'origine « urbaine » de l'agriculture, et sur le rôle des hybridations qui se produisent dans les stocks urbains de graines : c'est l'agriculture qui suppose le stock, et non l'inverse. Dans une étude à paraître, Jean Robert analyse les thèses de Mellaart et l'hypothèse de Jane Jacobs, et les utilise dans de nouvelles perspectives : Décoloniser l'espace. ( mouvements en zigzag, étapes qui manquent ici ou là, coupures d'ensemble irréductibles ). | It is not the State that presupposes a mode of production; quite the opposite, it is the State that makes production a "mode." The last reasons for presuming a progressive development are invalidated. Like seeds in a sack: It all begins with a chance intermixing. The "state and urban revolution" may be Paleolithic, not Neolithic as Childe believed.<br><br>Evolutionism has been challenged in many diff erent ways (zigzag movements, stages skipped here or there, irreducible overall breaks). | t is not the State that supposes a<br>***mode de production***, but<br>***l'inverse*** <span style="color:green">[the inverse — the mathematical flip again (¶10): the operator of the whole sequence]</span>, it is the State that<br>***fait de la production un « mode »*** <span style="color:green">[makes of production a « mode » — faire de X un Y, the transformative idiom (cf. ¶2, il fait de la machine de guerre une institution militaire: the State's signature operation, twice now); the scare-quoted « mode » detaches Marx's word and hands it back as the State's product]</span>. The last reasons for supposing a progressive development<br>***s'annulent*** <span style="color:green">[cancel themselves out — annuler: the reflexive again, self-annulment; also the mathematical sense: the terms go to zero]</span>. It is like the<br>***graines dans un sac*** <span style="color:green">[seeds in a sack — the ¶10 wild-seed stock become figure: everything begins as mixture, no ordered sowing; the sack, not the furrow]</span>: everything begins by a<br>***mélange au hasard*** <span style="color:green">[mixture by chance — au hasard again (¶10): the aleatory at the origin, chance before selection]</span>. The «<br>***révolution étatique et urbaine*** » <span style="color:green">[State-and-urban revolution — étatique, the adjective of-the-State: State-founding and city-founding as one event, revised against Childe's own phrase "urban revolution"]</span> can be Paleolithic, and not Neolithic as Childe believed. Evolutionism has been<br>***mis en question*** <span style="color:green">[put in question — the examination idiom: not refuted, summoned for questioning; procedural register]</span> in multiple ways (zigzag movements, stages that<br>***manquent ici ou là*** <span style="color:green">[are missing here or there — manquer: the gap-verb, absence as positive feature of the record]</span>, irreducible<br>***coupures d'ensemble*** <span style="color:green">[overall cuts — coupure, the couper chain of ¶5/¶12; d'ensemble: wholesale, of the whole set — breaks that take the entire series at once]</span>).<br>¹<br>⁰<br>ᶜ<br>. …a kind of<br>***rente***. <span style="color:green">[the ¶10 truncation resolves: the deferred word is « rente », tying the nexum to ¶9's keyword — the bond yields rent, not interest, even in its suspended clause]</span> Cf. notably Pierre Noailles, *Fas et Jus*, Les Belles Lettres; and Dumézil, who insists on the<br>***rapport du nexum et du lien magique*** <span style="color:green">[relation of the nexum and the magic bond — the two chains (juridical bind, magic bind) joined by Dumézil himself: D&G's ¶1 structure with a footnote pedigree]</span>, *Mitra-Varuna*, pp. 118–124.<br>¹¹ Cf. the excavations and works of J. Mellaart, *Earliest Civilizations in the Near East*, and *Çatal Hüyük*, London. The urbanist Jane Jacobs<br>***en a tiré*** <span style="color:green">[drew from it — tirer: the tirage register (¶12, tirent des photos): a model pulled from the site as a print is pulled]</span> an imperial model she calls «<br>***Nouvelle Obsidienne*** » <span style="color:green">[New Obsidian — from the name of the lavas that served to make tools: the model named for the trade-good, commerce before agriculture]</span>, which could go back to the beginning of the Neolithic and even<br>***beaucoup plus haut*** <span style="color:green">[much higher — the stratigrapher's vertical again (¶8, encore plus haut): earlier as higher]</span>. She insists on the « urban » origin of agriculture, and on the role of the hybridizations that<br>***se produisent*** in the urban stocks of seeds <span style="color:green">[produce themselves — the reflexive at the origin once more (s'érige, s'établit, s'annulent): hybridization as self-production, no producer]</span>: it is agriculture that supposes the stock, and not<br>***l'inverse***. In a study to appear, Jean Robert analyzes Mellaart's theses and Jane Jacobs's hypothesis, and uses them in new perspectives: *Décoloniser l'espace*. |
| ¶12 fr.534 umn.429 | Nous avons vu notamment comment Pierre Clastres avait tenté de briser le cadre évolutionniste, en fonction de deux thèses : 1 ) les sociétés dites primitives n'étaient pas des sociétés sans Etat, au sens où elles n'auraient pas atteint un certain stade, mais des sociétés contre-Etat, organisant des mécanismes qui conjuraient la forme-Etat, qui en rendaient la cristallisation impossible ; 2) quand l'Etat surgit, c'est sous forme d'une coupure irréductible, puisqu'il n'est pas la conséquence d'un développement progressif des forces productives (même la « révolution néolithique » ne peut pas se définir en fonction d'une infrastructure économique 12). Toutefois, on ne rompt pas avec l'évolutionnisme en traçant une coupure pour elle-même : Clastres, dans l'état dernier de son travail, maintenait la préexistence et l'autarcie des sociétés contre-Etat, et attribuait leur mécanisme à un pressentiment trop mystérieux de ce qu'elles conjuraient et qui n'existait pas encore. Plus généralement, on s'étonne de la bizarre indifférence que l'ethnologie manifeste encore à l'égard de l 'archéologie. On dirait que les ethnologues, enfermés dans leurs territoires respectifs, veulent bien les comparer entre eux de manière abstraite, ou structurale, à la rigueur, mais refusent de les confronter aux territoires archéologiques qui en compromettraient l'autarcie. Ils tirent des photos de leurs primitifs, mais récusent d'avance la coexistence et la superposition des deux cartes, ethnographique et archéologique. Çatal Hüyük aurait eu pourtant une zone d'influence de trois mille kilomètres ; et comment laisser dans le vague le problème toujours posé du rapport de coexistence entre les sociétés primitives et les empires, même du néolithique ? | We have seen in particular how Pierre Clastres tried to shatter the evolutionist framework by means of the following two theses: ( 1 ) societies termed primitive are not societies without a State, in the sense that they failed to reach a certain stage, but are counter-State societies organizing mechanisms that ward off the State-f orm, which make its crystallization impossible; (2) when the State arises, it is in the form of an irreducible break, since it is not the result of a progressive development of the forces of production (even the "Neolithic revolution" cannot be defined in terms of an economic infrastructure)Y However, one does not depart from evolutionism by establishing a clean break. In the final state of his work, Clastres maintained the preexistence and autarky of counter-State societies, and attributed their workings to an overmysterious presentiment of what they warded off and did not yet exist. More generally, one marvels at the bizarre indifference that ethnology manif ests for archaeology. It seems as though ethnologists, fenced of f in their respective territories, are willing to compare their territories in an abstract, or structural, way, if it comes to that, but ref use to set them against archaeological territories that would compromise their autarky. They take snapshots oftheir primitives but rule out in advance the coexistence and superposition of the two maps, the ethnographical and the archaeological. Catal Htiytik, however, would have had a zone of influence extending two thousand miles; how can the everrecurring problem of the relation of coexistence between primitive societies and empires, even those of Paleolithic times, be left unattended to? | We have seen notably how Pierre Clastres had<br>***tenté de briser le cadre*** <span style="color:green">[attempted to shatter the frame — briser: to smash, stronger than "break"; le cadre: the frame as enclosure, picture-frame and administrative cadre both]</span> evolutionist,<br>***en fonction de*** two theses <span style="color:green">[in function of — the fonction chain (¶8) as method-word: argument as dependent variable of its theses]</span>: 1) the societies<br>***dites*** primitive <span style="color:green">[called, so-called — dit: the naming held at arm's length, the label disowned in passing]</span> were not societies without a State, in the sense that they<br>***n'auraient pas atteint un certain stade*** <span style="color:green">[would not have attained a certain stage — conditional of the rejected imputation: the evolutionist's claim voiced in order to refuse it]</span>, but<br>***contre-État*** societies <span style="color:green">[counter-State — the contre coinage: against as structure, not attitude; cf.
