Clastres's title La société contre l'État]</span>, organizing mechanisms that<br>***conjuraient*** <span style="color:green">[warded off — conjurer: to exorcise, avert by rite; also to conjure: active magic, not a lack]</span> the<br>***forme-État*** <span style="color:green">[State-form — the hyphenated form-compound: the State as morphology, what crystallizes, not an institution]</span>, that rendered its<br>***cristallisation*** impossible <span style="color:green">[crystallization — the chemistry register: the State as precipitate forming in a solution held supersaturated but uncrystallized]</span>; 2) when the State<br>***surgit***, it is under the form of an irreducible<br>***coupure*** <span style="color:green">[cut — couper, the cut-chain (¶5, ¶11): severance, sharper than "break"]</span>, since it is not the consequence of a progressive development of the productive forces (even the « Neolithic revolution » cannot<br>***se définir en fonction de*** an economic infrastructure¹²) <span style="color:green">[define itself in function of — the reflexive + fonction pair again: definition as self-relation to a variable]</span>. However,<br>***on ne rompt pas avec*** evolutionism <span style="color:green">[one does not break with — rompre avec: the rupture idiom of persons and parties, to break off relations: Clastres breaks with the school without escaping it]</span> by<br>***traçant une coupure pour elle-même*** <span style="color:green">[tracing a cut for its own sake — tracer, the drawing register: a cut merely drawn, posited for itself]</span>: Clastres, in the<br>***état dernier*** of his work <span style="color:green">[last state — état, lowercase again (¶9 fn.): the state of the work, the word's civilian double]</span>, maintained the preexistence and<br>***autarcie*** of the counter-State societies <span style="color:green">[autarky — the closed-economy term: self-sufficiency as economic doctrine, about to be turned on the ethnologists themselves]</span>, and attributed their mechanism to a<br>***pressentiment trop mystérieux*** <span style="color:green">[too-mysterious presentiment — pre-sentiment: the pre- chain (¶4) gone psychic, feeling-in-advance; "trop": the flaw is excess mystery, not mystery itself]</span> of what they warded off and which did not yet exist. More generally,<br>***on s'étonne de*** the bizarre indifference <span style="color:green">[one is astonished at — s'étonner: the impersonal on registering scandal, the treatise's raised eyebrow]</span> that ethnology<br>***manifeste encore*** with regard to archaeology <span style="color:green">[still manifests — encore: the reproach of persistence]</span>.<br>***On dirait que*** <span style="color:green">[one would say that — conditional of appearance: hedged seeing, "it looks as though"]</span> the ethnologists,<br>***enfermés dans leurs territoires respectifs*** <span style="color:green">[shut up in their respective territories — enfermer: locked in; the fieldworkers' territories as cells, autarky as confinement]</span>,<br>***veulent bien les comparer*** <span style="color:green">[are quite willing to compare them — vouloir bien: grudging consent, "they'll allow that much"]</span> among themselves in an abstract manner, or structural,<br>***à la rigueur*** <span style="color:green">[at a stretch, if pressed — concessive idiom: the outer limit of what they will allow]</span>, but refuse to<br>***confronter*** them with the archaeological territories <span style="color:green">[confront — the courtroom confrontation: witnesses brought face to face]</span> that would compromise their autarky. They<br>***tirent des photos*** of their primitives <span style="color:green">[take photographs — tirer, also to print, pull a print: the tirage register (¶11, en a tiré)]</span>, but<br>***récusent d'avance*** <span style="color:green">[recuse in advance — juridical: reject as one challenges a witness or juror; d'avance: the pre- chain in procedure, objection lodged before the evidence]</span> the coexistence and superposition of the two maps, ethnographic and archaeological. Çatal Hüyük<br>***aurait eu pourtant*** <span style="color:green">[would nonetheless have had — conditional of the reported finding, kept as conjecture]</span> a zone of influence of three thousand kilometers; and<br>***comment laisser dans le vague*** <span style="color:green">[how to leave in the vague — le vague as a place things are left: the unresolved as a region; also sounding la vague, the wave, on the eve of ¶16's waves]</span> the<br>***problème toujours posé*** <span style="color:green">[ever-posed problem — poser un problème: the problem as standing posit, set and never taken up]</span> of the relation of coexistence between the primitive societies and the empires, even from the Neolithic? |
| ¶13 fr.534 umn.429 | Tant qu'on ne passe pas par l'archéologie, la question d'un rapport ethnologiehistoire se réduit à une confrontation idéaliste, et ne se dégage ,pas du thème absurde de la société sans histoire, ou de société contre l'histoire. Tout n'est pas Etat, justement parce qu'il y a eu des Etats toujours et partout. Ce n'est pas seulement l'écriture qui suppose l'Etat, c'est la parole, la langue et le langage. L'autosuffisance, l'autarcie, l'indépendance, la préexistence des communes primitives est un rêve d'ethnologue : non pas que ces communes dépendent nécessairement d'Etats, mais coexistent avec eux dans un réseau complexe. Il est vraisemblable que les 12. Clastres, La société contre ['Etat. Nous avons vu comment, selon Clastres, la guerre primitive était un des principaux mécanismes conjurant l'Etat, en tant qu'elle maintenait l'opposition et la dispersion de petits groupes segmentaires. Mais aussi, de ce point de vue, la guerre primitive reste subordonnée aux mécanismes de conjuration, et ne s'autonomise pas dans une machine, même quand elle comporte un corps spécialisé. sociétés primitives ont entretenu « dès le début » des rapports lointains les unes avec les autres, et pas seulement de proche en proche, et que ces rapports passaient par des Etats, même si ceux-ci n'en faisaient qu'une capture locale et partielle. Les paroles elles-mêmes et les langues, indépendamment de l'écriture, ne définissent pas des groupes fermés qui se comprennent entre eux, mais déterminent d'abord des rapports entre groupes qui ne se comprennent pas : s'il y a langage, c'est d'abord entre ceux qui ne parlent pas la même langue. Le langage est fait pour cela, pour la traduction, non pour la communication. Et il y a dans les sociétés primitives autant de tendances qui « cherchent » l'Etat, autant de vecteurs qui travaillent en direction de l'Etat, que de mouvements dans l'Etat, ou hors de lui, qui tendent à s'en écarter, s'en prémunir, ou bien le faire évoluer, ou déjà l'abolir : tout coexiste, en perpétuelle interaction. | As long as archaeology is passed over, the question of the relation between ethnology and history is reduced to an idealist confrontation, and fails to wrest itself from the absurd theme of society without history, or society against history. Everything is not of the State precisely because there have been States always and everywhere. Not only does writing presuppose the State, but so do speech and language. The self -sufficiency, autarky, independence, preexistence of primitive communities, is an ethnological : | As long as one does not<br>***passer par*** archaeology <span style="color:green">[pass by way of — the routing idiom: archaeology as obligatory point of passage; the same passer par as relations "passing by way of States" below — method and history routed alike]</span>, the question of an ethnology-history relation<br>***se réduit à*** an idealist confrontation <span style="color:green">[reduces itself to — the reflexive again (¶11 series): the question shrinks on its own, no reducer named]</span>, and<br>***ne se dégage pas de*** the absurd theme <span style="color:green">[does not extricate itself from — se dégager: to free oneself from entanglement, clear oneself; also the fencer's disengage]</span> of society without history, or of society against history.<br>***Tout n'est pas État*** <span style="color:green">[Not everything is State — the bare copula: State as predicate, what things are, not what they belong to]</span>, precisely because there have been States always and everywhere. It is not only writing that supposes the State, it is<br>***la parole, la langue et le langage*** <span style="color:green">[speech, tongue, and language — the Saussurean triad kept distinct: parole the spoken act, langue a particular language-system, langage the faculty as such; and the parole of ¶9's fn.: the binding word, already magico-religious, already State]</span>. The self-sufficiency,<br>***autarcie***, independence, preexistence of the primitive<br>***communes*** <span style="color:green">[communes — communal holdings, the term of ¶8's Unity of the communes; not the softer "communities"]</span> is a<br>***rêve d'ethnologue*** <span style="color:green">[an ethnologist's dream — the dreamer named: the discipline's wish, ad hominem after ¶12's locked territories]</span>: not that these communes necessarily depend on States, but they coexist with them in a complex<br>***réseau*** <span style="color:green">[network — the net register: réseau from rets, the snare-net; the filet chain gone neutral, capture's mesh as mere connectivity]</span>. It is plausible that primitive societies<br>***ont entretenu*** « dès le début » distant relations <span style="color:green">[maintained — entretenir again (¶10): kept up, kept fed; « from the start » in D&G's own scare-quotes: the origin cited as suspect phrase]</span> with one another, and not only<br>***de proche en proche*** <span style="color:green">[from next to next — propagation by contiguity, neighbor to neighbor: the ¶1 nouer à distance inverted]</span>, and that these relations<br>***passaient par*** States, even if the States<br>***n'en faisaient qu'une capture locale et partielle*** <span style="color:green">[made of them only a local and partial capture — faire de X (¶11): the State's signature operation performed weakly; capture graded, not all-or-nothing]</span>. The<br>***paroles*** themselves and the<br>***langues***, independently of writing, do not define closed groups that<br>***se comprennent entre eux*** <span style="color:green">[understand one another among themselves — the reflexive-reciprocal: comprehension as closed circuit, the autarky thesis in linguistic form]</span>, but determine first of all relations between groups that do not understand one another: if there is<br>***langage***, it is first of all between those who do not speak the same<br>***langue***. Language is<br>***fait pour cela*** <span style="color:green">[made for that — fait pour: purpose-built, the artisan's idiom: language as tool with a function]</span>, for translation, not for communication. And there are in primitive societies as many tendencies that «<br>***cherchent*** » the State <span style="color:green">[« seek » — the ¶1 imperative cherchez toujours returned in scare-quotes: the hunt for the State now inside the societies themselves]</span>, as many vectors that<br>***travaillent en direction de*** the State <span style="color:green">[work in the direction of — travailler: labor-verb for tendencies, the surtravail register in vector form]</span>, as there are movements in the State, or outside it, that tend to<br>***s'en écarter, s'en prémunir, ou bien le faire évoluer, ou déjà l'abolir*** <span style="color:green">[move away from it, forearm themselves against it, or make it evolve, or already abolish it — the four-step gradation: distance, immunity, reform, abolition; prémunir: protective anticipation; déjà: the chain at its most compressed — abolition already underway]</span>: everything coexists, in perpetual interaction.<br>¹² Clastres, *La société contre l'État*. We have seen how, according to Clastres, primitive war was one of the principal mechanisms<br>***conjurant*** the State, insofar as it maintained the opposition and dispersion of small segmentary groups. But also, from this point of view, primitive war remains<br>***subordonnée aux mécanismes de conjuration*** <span style="color:green">[subordinated to the mechanisms of warding-off — conjuration: the noun of conjurer, also "conspiracy" in ordinary French: the warding as standing plot against the State]</span>, and does not<br>***s'autonomise*** in a machine <span style="color:green">[autonomize itself — the reflexive-origin series: standing up as a machine in its own right, no maker]</span>, even when it<br>***comporte*** a specialized body <span style="color:green">[comprises — comporter, the inventory-verb of ¶7: a body included as component, not yet autonomous]</span>. <span style="color:green">(text for ¶12's marker, delivered here)</span> |
| ¶14 fr.535 umn.430 | Un évolutionnisme économique est impossible : on ne peut guère croire à une évolution même ramifiée « cueilleurs - chasseurs - éleveurs - agriculteurs - industriels ». Ne vaut pas mieux un évolutionnisme éthologique « nomades - semi-nomades - sédentaires ». Pas davantage un évolutionnisme écologique « autarcie dispersée de groupes locaux - villages et bourgades - villes - Etats ». Il suffit de faire interférer ces évolutions abstraites pour que tout évolutionnisme s'écroule : par exemple, c'est la ville qui crée l'agriculture, sans passer par des bourgades. Par exemple encore, les nomades ne précèdent pas les sédentaires, mais le nomadisme est un mouvement, un devenir qui affecte les sédentaires, autant que la sédentarisation est un arrêt qui fixe les nomades : Gryaznov a montré à cet égard comment le plus ancien nomadisme ne peut s'attribuer exactement qu'à des populations qui abandonnent leur sédentarité quasi urbaine, ou leur itinérance primitive, pour se mettre à nomadiser 13. C'est dans ces conditions que les nomades inventent la machine de guerre, comme ce qui occupe ou remplit l'espace nomade, et s'oppose aux villes et aux Etats qu'elle tend à abolir. Les primitifs avaient déjà des mécanismes de guerre qui concouraient à empêcher la formation d'Etat ; mais ces mécanismes changent quand ils s'autonomisent en une machine spécifique du nomadisme qui riposte aux Etats. | Economic evolutionism is an impossibility; even a ramified evolution, "gatherers-hunters-animal breeders-farmers-industrialists," is hardly believable. An evolutionary ethnology is no better: "nomads-seminomads-sedentaries." Nor an ecological evolutionism: "dispersed autarky oflocal groups-villages and small towns-cities-States." All we need to do is combine these abstract evolutions to make all of evolutionism crumble; for example, it is the city that creates agriculture, without going through small towns. To take another example, the nomads do not precede the sedentaries; rather, nomadism is a movement, a becoming that aff ects sedentaries, just as sedentarization is a stoppage that settles the nomads. Griaznov has shown in this connection that the most ancient nomadism can be accurately attributed only to populations that abandoned their semi urban sedentarity, or their primitive itineration, to set off nomadizing. 1 3 It is under these conditions that the nomads invented the war machine, as that which occupies or fills nomad space and opposes towns and States, which its tendency is to abolish. Primitive peoples already had mechanisms of war that converged to prevent the State formation; but these mechanisms change when they gain autonomy in the form of a specific nomadism machine that strikes back against the States. | An economic evolutionism is impossible:<br>***on ne peut guère croire à*** <span style="color:green">[one can hardly believe in — croire à: belief as credit extended; guère: scarcely — the thesis denied credit, not disproven]</span> an evolution, even a ramified one, « gatherers – hunters – herders – agriculturalists – industrialists ».<br>***Ne vaut pas mieux*** <span style="color:green">[is worth no better — verb-first inversion, clipped attack; valoir, the weighing idiom of ¶5's il vaut mieux: evolutionisms appraised and priced]</span> an<br>***éthologique*** evolutionism <span style="color:green">[ethological — of behavior: nomad/semi-nomad/sedentary as conducts, not peoples]</span> « nomads – semi-nomads – sedentaries ».<br>***Pas davantage*** <span style="color:green">[no more so — the elliptical negator, third strike of the series, each shorter than the last]</span> an ecological evolutionism « dispersed autarky of local groups – villages and<br>***bourgades*** <span style="color:green">[market towns — bigger than village, less than town: the middle rung the city skips]</span> – towns – States ».<br>***Il suffit de faire interférer*** <span style="color:green">[it suffices to make interfere — the minimal-condition formula; interférer: wave interference, the physics register that ¶16–17's convergent and divergent waves will inherit]</span> these abstract evolutions<br>***pour que tout évolutionnisme s'écroule*** <span style="color:green">[for all evolutionism to crumble — s'écrouler: a building's collapse, and reflexive: it falls of itself]</span>: for example, it is the town that creates agriculture,<br>***sans passer par*** bourgades <span style="color:green">[without passing by way of — the routing idiom (¶13): the obligatory waypoint skipped]</span>. For another example, the nomads do not precede the sedentaries, but nomadism is a movement, a<br>***devenir*** <span style="color:green">[becoming — the D&G term: a movement that traverses, not a stage that succeeds]</span> that affects the sedentaries, just as much as sedentarization is an<br>***arrêt*** <span style="color:green">[stop, arrest — also the legal arrêt, a ruling that halts: an arrest that fixes, not a settling]</span> that<br>***fixe*** the nomads: Gryaznov has shown in this regard how the most ancient nomadism<br>***ne peut s'attribuer exactement qu'à*** <span style="color:green">[can attribute itself exactly only to — the reflexive again: attribution as the phenomenon's own self-assignment]</span> populations that abandon their<br>***quasi urbaine*** sedentarity <span style="color:green">[quasi-urban — all but urban: near-identity, not "semi"]</span>, or their primitive<br>***itinérance*** <span style="color:green">[itinerancy — the wandering circuit: a third term, neither sedentary nor yet nomad]</span>,<br>***pour se mettre à nomadiser***¹³ <span style="color:green">[to set about nomadizing — se mettre à: the onset idiom, taking up an activity; nomadiser: nomadism as verb, something done]</span>. It is in these conditions that the nomads invent the machine de guerre, as that which occupies or fills nomad space, and opposes itself to the towns and States that it<br>***tend à abolir*** <span style="color:green">[tends to abolish — tendre à: directedness without accomplishment, the vector-idiom of ¶13]</span>. The primitives already had mechanisms of war that<br>***concouraient à empêcher*** <span style="color:green">[concurred to prevent — concourir: to run together toward, converge on; also the legal concours: joint action]</span> State formation; but these mechanisms change when they<br>***s'autonomisent*** in a machine specific to nomadism, which<br>***riposte*** <span style="color:green">[ripostes — fencing register: the counter-thrust answering an attack]</span> against the States. |
| ¶15 fr.535 umn.430 | On n'en inférera pas, cependant, une évolution même en zigzag qui irait des primitifs aux Etats, des Etats aux machines de guerre nomades : ou du moins le zigzag n'est 13. Selon Gryaznov, ce sont des agriculteurs sédentaires qui se mettent à nomadiser dans la steppe, à l'âge du bronze : c'est le cas d'un mouvement en zigzag dans l'évolution. Cf. Sibérie du Sud) Nagel, pp. 99, 133134. A P P A IW I L DE C A PTUR E pas successif, mais passe par les l ieux d'une topologie qui définit ici des sociétés primitives , là des Etats, là des machines de guerre. Et même quand l'Etat s'approprie la machine de guerre, en en changeant encore la nature, c'est un phénomène de transport, de transfert, et non d'évolution. Le nomade n'existe qu'en devenir, et en interaction ; mais le primitif aussi. L'histoire ne fait que traduire en succession une coexistence de devenirs. Et les collectivités peuvent être transhumantes, semi-sédentaires, sédentaires ou nomades, sans être pour autant des stades préparatoires de l'Etat, qui se trouve déjà là, ailleurs ou à côté. | We cannot, however, inf er from this even a zigzag evolution that would go from primitive peoples to States, from States to nomad war machines; or at least the zigzagging is not successive but passes through the loci of a topology that defines primiti ve societies here, States there, and elsewhere war machines. And even when the State appropriates the war machine, once again changing its nature, it is a phenomenon of transport, of transfer, and not one of evolution. The nomad exists only in becoming, and in interaction; the same goes for the primitive. All history does is to translate a coexistence of becomings into a succession. And collectivities can be transhumant, 7000 B.c.: | ***On n'en inférera pas, cependant*** <span style="color:green">[one will not infer from it, however — the instruction-future + impersonal on (¶2's on ne confondra pas): the treatise forbidding a conclusion]</span>, an evolution even in zigzag that would go from the primitives to States, from States to nomad war machines: or<br>***du moins*** <span style="color:green">[at the least — the fallback qualifier: conceding the zigzag to deny its sequence]</span> the zigzag is not successive, but<br>***passe par les lieux d'une topologie*** <span style="color:green">[passes by way of the places of a topology — the routing idiom again (¶13, ¶14): waypoints, not stages; lieux: positions in a space, the mathematician's loci]</span> that defines here primitive societies, there States, there machines de guerre. And even when the State<br>***s'approprie*** the war machine <span style="color:green">[appropriates to itself — the reflexive of taking-as-one's-own: property-verb, the capture in grammatical miniature]</span>, changing its nature yet again, it is a phenomenon of<br>***transport, de transfert*** <span style="color:green">[transport, transfer — displacement across the topology: movement of place, not advance of stage; transfert also the legal transfer of title]</span>, and not of evolution. The nomad exists only in<br>***devenir***, and in interaction; but the primitive too. History<br>***ne fait que traduire*** <span style="color:green">[does nothing but translate — ne faire que: the restrictive, history's whole work reduced to one operation; traduire: the translation-chain of ¶13 — history renders coexistence into the other idiom, succession; also the legal traduire, to hale before a court]</span> into succession a coexistence of<br>***devenirs***. And the collectivities can be<br>***transhumantes*** <span style="color:green">[transhumant — seasonal herding between fixed pastures: a fourth conduct beside ¶14's series]</span>, semi-sedentary, sedentary, or nomad,<br>***sans être pour autant*** <span style="color:green">[without for all that being — pour autant: the concession-blocker, "it does not follow"]</span> preparatory stages of the State, which<br>***se trouve déjà là*** <span style="color:green">[finds itself already there — the reflexive + déjà chain: the State discovered in place, self-located, no arrival]</span>, elsewhere or<br>***à côté*** <span style="color:green">[beside, next door — lateral position: alongside, never ahead on a line]</span>.<br>¹³ According to Gryaznov, it is sedentary agriculturalists who set about nomadizing in the steppe, at the Bronze Age: it is the case of a zigzag movement in evolution. Cf. *Sibérie du Sud*, Nagel, pp. 99, 133–134. |
| ¶16 fr.536 umn.431 | Peut-on dire au moins que les cueilleurs-chasseurs sont les « vrais » primitifs, et restent malgré tout la base ou le minimum de présupposition de la formation d'Etat, si loin qu'on fasse remonter celle-ci ? On ne peut avoir ce point de vue qu'à condition de se faire une conception de la causalité très insuffisante. Et il est vrai que les sciences de l'homme, avec leurs schémas matérialistes, évolutionnistes ou même dialectiques, sont en retard sur la richesse et la complexité des relations causales telles qu'elles apparaissent en physique ou même en biologie. La physique et la biologie nous mettent en présence de causalités à l'envers, sans finalité\ mais qui n'en témoignent pas moins d'une action du futur sur le présent, ou du présent sur le passé : ainsi l'onde convergente et le potentiel anticipé qui impliquent une inversion du temps. Plus que les coupures ou les zigzags, ce sont ces causalités à l'envers qui brisent l'évolution. De même, dans le domaine qui nous occupe, il ne suffit pas de dire que l'Etat néolithique ou même paléolithique, une fois apparu, réagit sur le monde environnant des cueilleurs-chasseurs ; il agit déjà avant d'apparaître, comme la limite actuelle que ces sociétés primitives conjurent pour leur compte, ou comme le point vers lequel elles convergent, mais qu'elles n'atteindraient pas sans s 'atléantir. Il y a à la fois, dans ces sociétés, des vecteurs qui vont en direction de l'Etat, des mécanismes qui le conjurent, un point de convergence repoussé, mis au dehors à mesure qu'on s'en approche. Conjurer, c'est aussi anticiper. Certes, ce n'est pas du tout de la même façon que l'Etat apparaît à l'existence, et qu'il préexiste au titre de limite conjurée ; d'où l'irréductible contingence. Mais pour donner un sens positif à l'idée d'un < pressentiment » de ce qui n'existe pas encore, il faut montrer comment ce qui n'existe pas agit déjà sous une autre forme que celle de son existence. Une fois apparu, l'Etat réagit sur les cueilleurs-chasseurs en leur imposant l'agriculture, l'élevage, une division poussée du travail, etc. : donc sous forme d'une onde centrifuge ou divergente. | Can it at least be said that the hunter-gatherers are the "true" primitives and remain in spite of it all the basis or minimal presupposition of the State formation, however far back in time we place it? This point of view can be maintained only at the price of a very inadequate conception of causality. And it is true that the human sciences, with their materialist, evolutionary, and even dialectical schemas, lag behind the richness and complexity of causal relations in physics, or even in biology. Physics and biology present us with reverse causalities that are without finality but testif y nonetheless to an action of the future on the present, or of the present on the past, for example, the convergent wave and the anticipated potential, which imply an inversion of time. More than breaks or zigzags, it is these reverse causalities that shatter evolution. Similarly, in the present context, it is not adequate to say that the Neolithic or even Paleolithic State, once it appeared, reacted back on the surrounding world of the hunter-gatherers; it was already acting before it appeared, as the actual limit these primitive societies warded off, or as the point toward which they converged but could not reach without self -destructing. These societies simultaneously have vectors moving in the direction of the State, mechanisms warding it off, and a point of convergence that is repelled, set outside, as fast as it is approached. To ward off is also to anticipate. Of course, it is not at all in the same way that the State appears in existence, and that it preexists in the capacity of a warded-off limit; hence its irreducible contingency. But in order to give a positive meaning to the idea of a "presentiment" of what does not yet exist, it is necessary to demonstrate that what does not yet exist is already in action, in a different form than that of its existence. Once it has appeared, the State reacts back on the hunter-gatherers, imposing upon them agriculture, animal raising, an extensi ve di vision onabor, etc.; it acts, theref ore, in the form of a centrif ugal or divergent wave. | ***Peut-on dire au moins que*** <span style="color:green">[can one say at least that — the concessive question: the adversary's minimal fallback position, staged to be dismantled]</span> the<br>***cueilleurs-chasseurs*** <span style="color:green">[gatherers-hunters — French order: gathering first, against the English fixed compound]</span> are the « true » primitives, and remain despite everything the base or the minimum of presupposition of State formation,<br>***si loin qu'on fasse remonter celle-ci ?*** <span style="color:green">[however far back one makes it go up — remonter: to go back UP in time, the stratigrapher's vertical again (¶8 plus haut, ¶11 beaucoup plus haut): origins climbed toward, not walked back to]</span> One can hold this point of view only on condition of<br>***se faire une conception*** very insufficient of causality <span style="color:green">[making oneself a conception — the reflexive fabrication: the inadequate concept as home manufacture]</span>. And it is true that the sciences of man, with their materialist, evolutionist, or even dialectical schemas,<br>***sont en retard sur*** <span style="color:green">[are in arrears of — en retard sur: lagging behind as a debtor lags on payments, lateness as owed distance]</span> the richness and complexity of causal relations such as they appear in physics or even in biology. Physics and biology<br>***nous mettent en présence de*** <span style="color:green">[put us in the presence of — the formal introduction: causalities presented as one presents persons]</span><br>***causalités à l'envers*** <span style="color:green">[causalities in reverse — à l'envers: wrong-way-round, inside out]</span>, without finality, but which<br>***n'en témoignent pas moins de*** <span style="color:green">[testify no less to — témoigner: the witness-stand register; the double negative of the reluctant concession]</span> an action of the future on the present, or of the present on the past: thus the convergent wave and the<br>***potentiel anticipé*** <span style="color:green">[anticipated potential — the physics term of art is advanced potential: the time-reversed solution, paired with the ingoing wave; anticiper: the ¶12–17 anticipation-chain in the physicist's mouth]</span>, which imply an inversion of time. More than the coupures or the zigzags, it is these causalities in reverse that shatter evolution. Likewise, in the domain that occupies us,<br>***il ne suffit pas de dire*** <span style="color:green">[it does not suffice to say — the ¶14 minimal-condition formula negated: the bar raised]</span> that the Neolithic or even Paleolithic State, once appeared, reacts upon the surrounding world of the gatherers-hunters; it<br>***agit déjà avant d'apparaître*** <span style="color:green">[acts already before appearing — the déjà chain at its paradox-point: action in advance of existence]</span>, as the<br>***limite actuelle*** <span style="color:green">[actual limit — actuel: in act, effective now, the virtual/actual register — not merely "current"]</span> that these primitive societies conjurent<br>***pour leur compte*** <span style="color:green">[on their own account — the compte chain (¶4, en ligne de compte): warding as self-interested bookkeeping, done for one's own ledger]</span>, or as the point toward which they converge, but which they would not attain without<br>***s'anéantir*** <span style="color:green">[annihilating themselves — anéantir: reducing to néant, nothingness]</span>. There is at once, in these societies, vectors that go in the direction of the State, mechanisms that ward it off, a point of convergence<br>***repoussé, mis au dehors à mesure qu'on s'en approche*** <span style="color:green">[pushed back, set outside in step with one's approaching it — à mesure que: proportional, the receding horizon; mettre au dehors: put out of doors, the expulsion-idiom]</span>.<br>***Conjurer, c'est aussi anticiper*** <span style="color:green">[to ward off is also to anticipate — the infinitive aphorism: the paragraph's law in six words]</span>. Certainly, it is not at all in the same way that the State appears in existence, and that it preexists<br>***au titre de*** warded-off limit <span style="color:green">[in the capacity of, under the title of — the juridical title-formula (¶8, en tant que): preexistence held by legal title]</span>;<br>***d'où*** the irreducible contingency. But to give a positive sense to the idea of a « presentiment » of what does not yet exist,<br>***il faut montrer comment*** <span style="color:green">[one must show how — the demand is for the mechanism, not the fact]</span> what does not exist acts already, under another form than that of its existence. Once appeared, the State reacts upon the gatherers-hunters, imposing on them agriculture, herding, a<br>***poussée*** division of labor <span style="color:green">[pushed-far — poussé: driven forward, advanced by force]</span>, etc.: thus under the form of a centrifugal or divergent wave. |
| ¶17 fr.536 umn.431 | Mais, avant d'apparaître, l'Etat agit déjà sous forme de l'onde convergente ou centripète des chas537 seurs cueilleurs, onde qui s'annule précisément au point de convergence qui marquerait l'inversion des signes ou l'apparition d'Etat ( d'où l'instabilité intrinsèque et fonctionnelle de ces sociétés primitives 14). Or, il est nécessaire de ce point de vue de penser la contemporanéité ou la coexistence des deux mouvements inverses, des deux directions du temps - des primitifs « avant » l'Etat, et de l'Etat « après » les primitifs - comme si les deux ondes qui nous paraissent s'exclure, ou se succéder, se déroulaient simultanément dans un champ moléculaire micrologique, micropolitique, « archéologique ». Il y a des mécanismes collectifs qui conjurent et anticipent à la fois la formation d'un pouvoir central. Celui-ci apparaît donc en fonction d'un seuil ou d'un degré tel que ce qui est anticipé prend de la consistance ou non, ce qui est conjuré cesse de l'être et arrive. Et ce seuil de consistance, ou de contrainte, n'est pas évolutif, il coexiste avec son en-deçà. Bien plus, il faudrait distinguer des seuils de consistance : la ville et l'Etat ne sont pas la même chose, quelle que soit leur complémentarité. La « révolution urbaine » et la « révolution étatique » peuvent coïncider, mais non pas se confondre. Dans les deux cas, il y a pouvoir central, mais ce n'est pas la même figure. Certains auteurs ont su distinguer le système impérial ou palatial (palais-temple), et le système citadin, urbain. Il y a ville dans les deux cas, mais dans un cas la ville est une excroissance du palais ou du temple, dans l'autre cas le palais, le temple est une concrétion de la ville. Dans un cas, la ville par excellence est la capitale, dans l'autre cas, c'est la métropole. | But before appearing, the State already acts in the form of the convergent or centripetal wave of the huntergatherers, a wave that cancels itself out precisely at the point of convergence marking the inversion of signs or the appearance of the State (hence the functional and intrinsic instability of these primitive societies ).14 It is necessary from this standpoint to conceptualize the contemporaneousness or coexistence of the two inverse movements, of the two directions of time-of the primitive peoples "before" the State, and of the State "af ter" the primitive peoples-as if the two waves that seem to us to exclude or succeed each other unfolded simultaneously in an "archaeological," micropolitical, micrological, molecular field.<br><br>There exist collective mechanisms that simultaneously ward off and : | But, before appearing, the State<br>***agit déjà*** under the form of the convergent or centripetal wave of the<br>***chasseurs cueilleurs*** <span style="color:green">[hunters gatherers — NOTE: the French itself flips the order here, against ¶16's cueilleurs-chasseurs (twice) and its own next uses; D&G's variation or Minuit slip — preserve and flag, do not harmonize]</span>, a wave that<br>***s'annule*** <span style="color:green">[cancels itself — the reflexive-mathematical self-cancellation of ¶11 (s'annulent): the wave going to zero at its own limit]</span> precisely at the point of convergence that<br>***marquerait*** <span style="color:green">[would mark — conditional: the inversion-point as hypothetical, never quite reached]</span> the inversion of signs or the apparition of State (<br>***d'où*** the intrinsic and functional instability of these primitive societies¹<br>⁴<br>).<br>***Or*** <span style="color:green">[now — the logical pivot: the geometer's "or," introducing the required consequence]</span>, it is necessary from this point of view to<br>***penser la contemporanéité*** <span style="color:green">[to think the contemporaneity — penser + direct object: the philosopher's transitive thinking, a concept to be constructed, not an opinion held]</span> or the coexistence of the two inverse movements, of the two directions of time — of the primitives « before » the State, and of the State « after » the primitives — as if the two waves that seem to us to exclude one another, or succeed one another,<br>***se déroulaient*** simultaneously <span style="color:green">[unrolled themselves — dérouler: the scroll or reel unwinding; and the reflexive series again]</span> in a molecular field,<br>***micrologique, micropolitique, « archéologique »*** <span style="color:green">[micrological, micropolitical, « archaeological » — French order: molecular first, the scare-quotes on archaeological alone, the discipline's name borrowed under caution]</span>. There are collective mechanisms that<br>***conjurent et anticipent à la fois*** <span style="color:green">[ward off and anticipate at once — à la fois: the ¶16 aphorism (conjurer, c'est aussi anticiper) now operationalized in one clause]</span> the formation of a central power. The latter appears therefore<br>***en fonction d'un seuil ou d'un degré*** <span style="color:green">[as a function of a threshold or a degree — the fonction chain gone quantitative: power as dependent variable of a threshold]</span> such that what is anticipated<br>***prend de la consistance*** or not <span style="color:green">[takes on consistency — prendre: the setting of plaster, jam, cement — consistance, the plan de consistance term: the anticipated thing thickening into existence]</span>, what is warded off ceases to be so<br>***et arrive*** <span style="color:green">[and arrives — arriver: both "happens" and "arrives": the warded-off thing pulling in like a train]</span>. And this threshold of consistency, or of<br>***contrainte*** <span style="color:green">[constraint — the legal contrainte: enforcement, distraint, the bailiff's compulsion]</span>, is not evolutionary, it coexists with its<br>***en-deçà*** <span style="color:green">[hither side — the technical spatial term: what lies short of the threshold, on this side of it; a place, not a waiting period]</span>.<br>***Bien plus*** <span style="color:green">[much more — the escalation connective: the argument raising its own stakes]</span>,<br>***il faudrait distinguer*** thresholds of consistency <span style="color:green">[one would have to distinguish — falloir in the conditional: the obligation itself hypothetical, a program announced]</span>: the town and the State are not the same thing,<br>***quelle que soit*** their complementarity <span style="color:green">[whatever be — the concessive subjunctive formula]</span>. The « urban revolution » and the « State revolution » can coincide, but not<br>***se confondre*** <span style="color:green">[be confused with one another — confondre: the ¶2 chain (on ne confondra pas): coincidence without merger, the distinction-verb of the whole plateau]</span>. In the two cases, there is central power, but it is not the same<br>***figure*** <span style="color:green">[figure — geometrical and rhetorical at once: the shape power cuts]</span>. Certain authors<br>***ont su distinguer*** <span style="color:green">[have known how to distinguish — savoir + infinitive: the achievement-idiom, distinction as skill exercised]</span> the imperial or palatial system (palace-temple), and the citadine, urban system. There is town in the two cases, but in one case the town is an<br>***excroissance*** of the palace or the temple <span style="color:green">[outgrowth — excroissance: the pathological register, a growth upon a body]</span>, in the other case the palace, the temple is a<br>***concrétion*** of the town <span style="color:green">[concretion — the geological register: matter accreted from a surrounding medium; the town as the palace's mother-liquor]</span>. In one case, the town<br>***par excellence*** is the capital, in the other case, it is the<br>***métropole*** <span style="color:green">[metropolis — Greek mêtêr-polis, mother-city: the colonial founder-city, mother not seat]</span>. |
| ¶18 fr.537 umn.432 | Déjà Sumer témoigne d'une solution-cité, par différence avec la solution impériale d'Egypte. Mais plus encore c'est le monde méditerranéen, avec les Pelasges, les Phéniciens, les Grecs, les Carthaginois, qui crée un tissu urbain distinct des organismes impériaux de l'Orient 15. Là encore, la question n'est pas d'évolution, mais de deux seuils de consistance, eux-mêmes coexistants. Les différences portent sur plusieurs aspects. 14. Jean Robert dégage cette notion d'une « inversion des signes et des messages » : « Dans une première phase, les informations circulent princip3lement de la périphérie vers le centre mais, à partir d'un certain point critique, la ville émet vers le monde rural des messages de plus en plu.> impératifs » et devient exportatrice (Décoloniser l'espace). 15. Sur les villes chinoises et leur subordination au principe impérial, cf. Balazs, La bureaucratie céleste, Gallimard. Et Braudel, Civilisation matérielle et capitalisme, p. 403 : « Dans l'Inde comme en Chine, les structures sociales refusent la ville à l'avance, lui offrant, dirions-nous, un matériau de mauvais aloi, réfractaire. ( ... ) C'est que la société est prise, ce qui s'appelle prise, dans une sorte de système irréductible, de cristallisation préalable. » La ville est le corrélat de la route. Elle n'existe qu'en fonction d'une circulation, et de circuits ; elle est un point remarquable sur des circuits qui la créent ou qu'elle crée. Elle se définit par des entrées et des sorties, il faut que quelque chose y entre et en sorte. Elle impose une fréquence. Elle opère une polarisation de la matière, inerte, vivante ou humaine ; elle fait que le phylum, les flux passent ici ou là, sur des lignes horizontales. | Sumer already attests to a town solution, as opposed to the imperial solution of Egypt. But to an even greater extent, it was the Mediterranean world, with the Pelasgians, Phoenicians, Greeks, Carthaginians, that created an urban fabric distinct from the imperial organisms of the Orient. 15 Once again, the question is one not of evolution but of two thresholds of consistency that are themselves coexistent. They differ in several respects.<br><br>The town is the correlate of the road. The town exists only as a function of circulation, and of circuits; it is a remarkable point on the circuits that create it, and which it creates. It is defined by entries and exits; something must enter it and exit from it. It imposes a frequency. It effects a polarization of matter, inert, living or human; it causes the phylum, the flow, to pass through specific places, along horizontal lines. | Already Sumer<br>***témoigne d'une solution-cité*** <span style="color:green">[testifies to a city-solution — témoigner, the witness-register of ¶16; solution-cité: the hyphenated compound, city as solution — chemistry hovering: a solution in which things dissolve, against the imperial crystallization of fn. 15]</span>,<br>***par différence avec*** the imperial solution of Egypt <span style="color:green">[by difference with — differential comparison: Dumézil's differential method (¶2 fn.) as connective]</span>. But more still it is the Mediterranean world, with the Pelasgians, the Phoenicians, the Greeks, the Carthaginians, that creates a<br>***tissu urbain*** <span style="color:green">[urban tissue — tissu: woven cloth, the textile register; also anatomical tissue: the city-web as living fabric against the imperial organismes]</span> distinct from the imperial<br>***organismes*** of the Orient¹<br>⁵<br><span style="color:green">[organisms — the body-form: empire as organism, cities as tissue — two biologies of power]</span>.<br>***Là encore*** <span style="color:green">[there again — the refrain-connective (¶3): the same lesson at each site]</span>, the question is not of evolution, but of two thresholds of consistency, themselves coexistent. The differences<br>***portent sur*** several aspects <span style="color:green">[bear upon — porter sur: where the weight of difference rests]</span>. The town is the<br>***corrélat*** of the road <span style="color:green">[correlate — the logician's term: neither cause nor effect, co-given]</span>. It exists only<br>***en fonction d'une circulation*** <span style="color:green">[as a function of a circulation — the fonction chain: the town as dependent variable of flow; circulation: traffic, blood, and money at once]</span>, and of circuits; it is a<br>***point remarquable*** <span style="color:green">[remarkable point — mathematics: the notable point of a curve (inflection, maximum): the town as singularity on the circuit]</span> on circuits that create it or that it creates. It<br>***se définit par des entrées et des sorties*** <span style="color:green">[defines itself by entries and exits — the reflexive again; entrées/sorties: doorways and, in bookkeeping, the ledger's ins and outs — the compte chain at the town gate]</span>,<br>***il faut que*** something enter it and exit from it <span style="color:green">[it must be that — falloir: the town's existence as requirement, throughput mandated]</span>. It<br>***impose une fréquence*** <span style="color:green">[imposes a frequency — the wave register of ¶14–17 continued: the town as oscillator, setting the beat of passage]</span>. It<br>***opère*** a polarization of matter <span style="color:green">[operates — opérer, the ¶1 emperor's verb, then the stock's (¶10), now the town's: each power in turn does the operation]</span>, inert, living or human; it<br>***fait que le phylum, les flux passent ici ou là*** <span style="color:green">[makes it so that the phylum, the flows pass here or there — the faire que causative (¶3); flux plural: many flows; ici ou là: here or there, indeterminate — the town routes without deciding destinations]</span>, on horizontal lines.<br>¹<br>⁴<br>Jean Robert<br>***dégage*** this notion of an « inversion of signs and messages » <span style="color:green">[extracts — dégager: to disengage, draw out (¶13, se dégage): the concept freed from the material]</span>: « In a first phase, the informations circulate principally from the periphery toward the center but, from a certain critical point on, the town emits toward the rural world messages more and more imperative » and becomes an exporter (*Décoloniser l'espace*). <span style="color:green">[Robert is French-original: translated from the supplied French; text for ¶17's marker, delivered here]</span><br>¹<br>⁵<br>On the Chinese towns and their subordination to the imperial principle, cf. Balazs, *La bureaucratie céleste*, Gallimard. And Braudel, *Civilisation matérielle et capitalisme*, p. 403: « In India as in China, the social structures refuse the town<br>***à l'avance*** <span style="color:green">[in advance — the d'avance/pre- chain (¶12): refusal lodged before the fact]</span>, offering it,<br>***dirions-nous*** <span style="color:green">[we would say — Braudel's own hedge, conditional kept]</span>, a material<br>***de mauvais aloi*** <span style="color:green">[of bad alloy — aloi: the coin-metal standard; base coinage: the town given debased matter to work with]</span>,<br>***réfractaire*** <span style="color:green">[refractory — metallurgy: resistant to working, unmeltable]</span>. (…) It is that the society is<br>***prise, ce qui s'appelle prise*** <span style="color:green">[caught, what is called caught — c'est ce qui s'appelle X: the idiom of emphatic naming, "caught, and how"; prise: the capture-noun itself — Braudel handing D&G their title word]</span>, in a sort of irreducible system, of<br>***cristallisation préalable*** <span style="color:green">[prior crystallization — the ¶12 chemistry (cristallisation) meets the pre- chain (préalable): the State-form already precipitated, in advance]</span>. » <span style="color:green">[Braudel is French-original: translated from the supplied French]</span> |
| ¶19 fr.538 umn.432 | C'est un phénomène de trans-consistance, c'est un réseau, parce qu'elle est fondamentalement en rapport avec d'autres villes. Elle représente un seuil de déterritorialisation, car il faut que le matériau quelconque soit suffisamment déterritorialisé pour entrer dans le réseau, se soumettre à la polarisation, suivre le circuit de recodage urbain et routier. Le maximum de déterritorialisation apparaît dans la tendance des villes commerciales et maritimes à se séparer de l'arrière-pays, de la campagne (Athènes, Carthage, Venise . . . ). On a souvent insisté sur le caractère commercial de la ville, mais le commerce y est aussi bien spirituel, comme dans un réseau de monastères ou de cités-temples. Les villes sont des points-circuits de toute nature, qui font contrepoint sur les lignes horizontales ; elles opèrent une intégration complète, mais locale, et de ville en ville. Chacune constitue un pouvoir central, mais de polarisation ou de milieu, de coordination forcée. D'où la prétention égalitaire de ce pouvoir, quelle que soit la forme qu'il prenne, tyrannique, démocratique, oligarchique, aristocratique . . . L e pouvoir d e ville invente l'idée d e magistrature, très différente du fonctionnariat d'Etat 16. Mais qui dira où est la plus grande violence civile ? En effet, l'Etat procède autrement : c'est un phénomène d'intra-consistance. Il fait résonner ensemble des points, qui ne sont pas forcément déjà des villes-pôles, mais des points d'ordre très divers, particularités géographiques, ethniques, linguistiques, morales, économiques, technologiques . . . Il fait résonner la ville avec la campagne. Il opère par stratification, c'est-à-dire forme un ensemble vertical et hiérarchisé qui traverse les lignes horizon16. | It is a phenomenon of transconsistency, a network, because it is fundamentally in contact with other towns. It represents a threshold of deterritorialization, because whatever the material involved, it must be deterritorialized enough to enter the network, to submit to the polarization, to follow the circuit of urban and road recoding. The maximum deterritorialization appears in the tendency of maritime and commercial towns to separate off from the backcountry, from the countryside (Athens, Carthage, Venice). The commercial character of the town has often been emphasized, but the commerce in question is also spiritual, as in a network of monasteries or temple-cities. Towns are circuit-points of every kind, which enter into counterpoint along horizontal lines; they effect a complete but local, town-by-town, integration. Each one constitutes a central power, but it is a power of polarization or of the middle (milieu), off orced coordination. That is why this kind of power has egalitarian pretensions, regardless of the form it takes: tyrannical, democratic, oligarchic, aristocratic. Town power invents the idea of the magis- 7000 B.c.: <br><br>The State indeed proceeds otherwise: it is a phenomenon of intraconsistency. It makes points resonate together, points that are not necessarily already town-poles but very diverse points of order, geographic, ethnic, linguistic, moral, economic, technological particularities. It makes the town resonate with the countryside. It operates by stratification; in other words, it forms a vertical, hierarchized aggregate that spans the horizontal lines in a dimension of depth. | It is a phenomenon of<br>***trans-consistance*** <span style="color:green">[trans-consistency — the consistance chain (¶17) prefixed: consistency across, town to town; paired below with intra-consistance]</span>, it is a<br>***réseau*** <span style="color:green">[network — réseau from rets, the snare-net: the filet chain gone lateral (¶13)]</span>, because the town is fundamentally<br>***en rapport avec*** other towns <span style="color:green">[in relation with — rapport: the relation-word, not mere contact]</span>. It represents a threshold of deterritorialization, for<br>***il faut que le matériau quelconque*** <span style="color:green">[it must be that the whatever-material — quelconque: the mathematician's "any whatever": material indifferent in kind, qualified only by degree]</span> be sufficiently deterritorialized to enter the network,<br>***se soumettre à*** the polarization <span style="color:green">[submit itself to — the ¶8 subjection-verb, reflexive: the material subjects itself]</span>, follow the circuit of urban and road recoding. The maximum of deterritorialization appears in the tendency of commercial and maritime towns to<br>***se séparer de l'arrière-pays*** <span style="color:green">[separate themselves from the back-country — arrière-pays: the hinterland, the land behind: the town turning its back]</span>, from the countryside (Athens, Carthage, Venice…).<br>***On a souvent insisté sur*** <span style="color:green">[one has often insisted on — the impersonal on surveying the literature]</span> the commercial character of the town, but commerce there is<br>***aussi bien*** spiritual <span style="color:green">[just as well — the equalizing idiom: spiritual commerce not metaphor but the same operation]</span>, as in a network of monasteries or temple-cities. Towns are<br>***points-circuits*** of every nature, which<br>***font contrepoint*** <span style="color:green">[make counterpoint — the music register opens: point against point on the horizontal lines; ¶22's melodic lines and harmonic cuts will complete the score]</span> on the horizontal lines; they<br>***opèrent*** a complete, but local, integration, and from town to town <span style="color:green">[operate — the opérer chain (¶1, ¶10, ¶18)]</span>. Each constitutes a central power, but of polarization or of<br>***milieu***, of<br>***coordination forcée*** <span style="color:green">[forced coordination — power as compulsion of the middle, not command from above]</span>.<br>***D'où la prétention égalitaire*** of this power <span style="color:green">[whence the egalitarian claim — prétention: the legal claim asserted, a title pretended to; not "pretensions" as vanity]</span>,<br>***quelle que soit la forme qu'il prenne*** <span style="color:green">[whatever form it may take — the ¶17 concessive subjunctive formula]</span>, tyrannical, democratic, oligarchic, aristocratic… Town power invents the idea of<br>***magistrature***, very different from the State<br>***fonctionnariat***¹<br>⁶<br><span style="color:green">[magistracy vs. functionary-corps — the fonction chain (¶8): two bodies of office, elected charge vs. appointed function]</span>. But<br>***qui dira*** where the greater civil violence is? <span style="color:green">[who will say — the ¶3 formula verbatim (qui dira le meilleur et le pire?): the standing unanswerable, second occurrence]</span> Indeed, the State<br>***procède autrement*** <span style="color:green">[proceeds otherwise — procéder, the ¶1 jurist's verb: the State's mode of operation as procedure]</span>: it is a phenomenon of<br>***intra-consistance***. It<br>***fait résonner ensemble*** points <span style="color:green">[makes resonate together — the faire-causative + the acoustics register: resonance as the State's operation, against the town's frequency (¶18)]</span>, which are not necessarily already town-poles, but points of very diverse order, geographic, ethnic, linguistic, moral, economic, technological particularities… It makes the town resonate with the countryside. It operates by stratification, that is to say forms a vertical and hierarchized ensemble that<br>***traverse*** the horizontal lines<br>***en profondeur*** <span style="color:green">[traverses… in depth — the third dimension entered: the State cuts across the towns' plane]</span>. |
| ¶20 fr.538 umn.433 | En tous ces sens, François Châtelet met en question la notion classique d'Etat-cité, et doute que la cité athénienne puisse être assimilée à un Etat quelconque (<< La Grèce classique, la Raison, l'Etat », in En marge, l'Occident et ses autres, Aubier) . Des problèmes analogues se poseraient pour l'islam, et aussi pour l'Italie, l'Allemagne et les Flandres . à partir du XIe siècle : le pouvoir politique n'y implique pas la forme-Etat. Par exemple, la communauté des villes hanséatiques, sans fonctionnaires, sans armée, et même sans personnalité juridique. La ville est toujours prise dans un réseau de villes, mais, justement, « le réseau des villes » ne coïncide pas avec « la mosaïque d'Etats » : sur tous ces points, cf. les analyses de François Fourquet et Lion Murard, Généalogie des équipements collectifs, 10-18, pp. 79-106. tales en profondeur. Il ne retient donc tels et tels éléments qu'en coupant leurs relations avec d'autres éléments devenus extérieurs, en inhibant, ralentissant ou contrôlant ces relations ; si l'Etat a lui-même un circuit, c'est un circuit intérieur qui dépend d'abord de la résonance, c'est une zone de récurrence qui s'isole ainsi du reste du réseau, quitte à contrôler d'autant plus strictement les rapports avec ce reste. La question n'est pas de savoir si ce qui est retenu est naturel ou artificiel ( frontières ), puisqu'il y a de toute manière déterritorialisation ; mais la déterritorialisation vient ici de ce que le territoire lui-même est pris comme objet, comme matériau à stratifier, à faire résonner. Aussi le pouvoir central d'Etat est-il hiérarchique, et constitue un fonctionnariat ; le centre n'est pas au milieu, mais en haut, puisqu'il ne peut réunir ce qu'il isole que par subordination. | In retaining given elements, it necessarily cuts off their relations with other elements, which become exterior, it inhibits, slows down, or controls those relations; if the State has a circuit of its own, it is an internal circuit dependent primarily upon resonance, it is a zone of recurrence that isolates itself from the remainder of the network, even if in order to do so it must exert even stricter controls over its relations with that remainder. The question is not to find out whether what is retained is natural or artificial (boundaries), because in any event there is deterritorialization. But in this case deterritorialization is a result of the territory itself being taken as an object, as a material to stratif y, to make resonate. Thus the central power of the State is hierarchical, and constitutes a civil-service sector; the center is not in the middle (au milieu), but on top, because the only way it can recombine what it isolates is through subordination. | ⁶<br>In all these senses, François Châtelet<br>***met en question*** <span style="color:green">[puts in question — the ¶11 summons-idiom: the classical notion called in for questioning]</span> the classical notion of the city-State, and doubts that the Athenian city can be assimilated to any State whatever (« La Grèce classique, la Raison, l'État », in *En marge, l'Occident et ses autres*, Aubier). Analogous problems<br>***se poseraient*** <span style="color:green">[would pose themselves — conditional + reflexive: problems self-arising, hypothetically]</span> for Islam, and also for Italy, Germany and Flanders from the eleventh century on: political power there does not imply the<br>***forme-État*** <span style="color:green">[State-form — the ¶12 compound]</span>. For example, the community of the Hanseatic towns, without functionaries, without army, and even without<br>***personnalité juridique*** <span style="color:green">[juridical personality — legal personhood: the league as a body no law recognizes as a person]</span>. The town is always<br>***prise*** in a network of towns <span style="color:green">[taken, caught — prendre: the capture-verb (¶18 fn., prise, ce qui s'appelle prise): even the town is captured, by its own réseau]</span>, but, precisely, « the network of towns » does not coincide with « the mosaic of States »: on all these points, cf. the analyses of François Fourquet and Lion Murard, *Généalogie des équipements collectifs*, 10-18, pp. 79–106.<br><span style="color:green"></span>It therefore<br>***ne retient tels et tels éléments qu'en coupant*** <span style="color:green">[retains such-and-such elements only by cutting — ne… que: the restrictive; retenir: to hold back, keep in; couper: the cut-chain — the State keeps by severing, retention and amputation one act]</span> their relations with other elements<br>***devenus extérieurs*** <span style="color:green">[become exterior — the exteriority made, not found: outside as product of the cut]</span>, by inhibiting, slowing, or controlling these relations; if the State itself has a circuit, it is an interior circuit that depends first of all on resonance, it is a<br>***zone de récurrence*** that<br>***s'isole*** thus from the rest of the network <span style="color:green">[zone of recurrence that isolates itself — the reflexive series: self-quarantine; récurrence: the mathematician's recursion, the State looping on itself]</span>,<br>***quitte à*** control all the more strictly its relations with that rest <span style="color:green">[at the price of, even if it means — quitte à: the risk-acceptance idiom, a cost knowingly booked; and d'autant plus: the proportional chain — isolation and control scale together]</span>. The question is not<br>***de savoir si*** what is retained is natural or artificial (frontiers), since there is in any case deterritorialization; but deterritorialization here comes from this: that the territory itself is<br>***pris comme objet*** <span style="color:green">[taken as object — prendre again: the territory captured, the plateau's title in a participle — apparatus of capture as land-seizure of the very ground]</span>, as material to stratify, to make resonate.<br>***Aussi le pouvoir central d'État est-il*** hierarchical <span style="color:green">[thus is the central power of the State — the formal inversion after aussi: the treatise's QED cadence]</span>, and constitutes a fonctionnariat; the center is not<br>***au milieu***, but<br>***en haut*** <span style="color:green">[not in the middle, but on high — the ¶5 opposition returned verbatim: the sovereign speaking d'en haut vs. the warriors' au milieu, now the State vs. the town]</span>, since it can<br>***réunir ce qu'il isole*** only by subordination <span style="color:green">[reunite what it isolates — the paradox-pair: gathering as the sequel of separating]</span>. |
| ¶21 fr.539 umn.433 | Certes, il y a une multiplicité d'Etats non moins que de villes, mais ce n'est pas le même type de multiplicité : il y a autant d'Etats que de coupes verticales en profondeur, chacune séparée des autres, tandis que la ville est inséparable du réseau horizontal des villes . Chaque Etat est une intégration globale ( et non locale), une redondance de résonance ( et non de fréquence), une opération de stratification du territoire ( et non de polarisation du milieu). On peut reconstituer comment les sociétés primitives conjurent à la fois les deux seuils, tout en les anticipant. Lévi-Strauss montre que les mêmes villages sont susceptibles de deux présentations, l'une segmentaire et égalitaire, l'autre englobante et hiérarchisée. Il y a là comme deux potentiels, l'un qui anticipe un point central commun à deux segments horizontaux, l'autre, au contraire, un point central extérieur à une droite 17. C'est que les sociétés primitives ne manquent pas de formations de pouvoir : elles en ont même beaucoup. Mais, ce qui empêche les points centraux potentiels de cristalliser, de prendre consistance, ce sont précisément les mécanismes qui font que ces formations de pouvoir ne résonnent pas ensemble dans le point supérieur, pas plus qu'elles ne polarisent dans le point commun : les cercles en effet ne sont pas concentriques, et les deux segments ont besoin d'un troisième par lequel ils communiquent 18. C'est en ce sens que les sociétés primitives restent en deçà du seuil-ville autant que du seuil-Etat. Si nous considérons maintenant les deux seuils de consistance, nous voyons bien qu'ils impliquent une déterritorialisation, par rapport aux codes territoriaux primitifs. Et il est vain de se 17. Lévi-Strauss, Anthropologie structurale, Plon, pp. 167-168. 18. | Of course, there is a multiplicity of States no less than of towns, but it is not the same type of multiplicity: there are as many States as there are vertical cross sections in a dimension of depth, each separated from the others, whereas the town is inseparable from the horizontal network of towns. Each State is a global (not local) integration, a redundancy of resonance (not offrequency), an operation of the stratification of the territory (not of the polarization of the milieu).<br><br>It is possible to reconstruct how primitive societies warded off both thresholds while at the same time anticipating them. Levi-Strauss has shown that the same villages are susceptible to two presentations, one segmentary and egalitarian, the other encompassing and hierarchized. These are like two potentials, one anticipating a central point common to two horizontal segments, the other anticipating a central point external to a straight lineP Primitive societies do not lack formations of power; they even have many of them. But what prevents the potential central points from crystallizing, from taking on consistency, are precisely those mechanisms that keep the formations of power both from resonating together in a higher point and from becoming polarized at a common point: the circles are not concentric, and the two segments require a third segment through which to communicate. IS This is the sense in which primitive societies have crossed neither the town-threshold nor the State-threshold.<br><br>If we now turn our attention to the two thresholds of consistency, it is clear that they imply a deterritorialization in relation to the primiti ve territorial codes. It is futile to ask which came first, the city or the State, the | Certainly, there is a multiplicity of States no less than of towns, but it is not the same type of multiplicity: there are as many States as<br>***coupes verticales en profondeur*** <span style="color:green">[vertical cuts in depth — coupe: the couper chain as technical drawing, the architect's or geologist's section: each State one cut of the ground]</span>, each separated from the others, whereas the town is inseparable from the horizontal network of towns. Each State is a global (and not local) integration, a<br>***redondance de résonance*** (and not of frequency) <span style="color:green">[redundancy of resonance — the information-theory register (redundancy) crossed with the ¶19 acoustics: the State repeats itself into consistency; frequency stays with the town, ¶18]</span>, an operation of stratification of the territory (and not of polarization of the milieu).<br>***On peut reconstituer*** <span style="color:green">[one can reconstitute — the archaeologist's verb: the mechanism reassembled from fragments]</span> how primitive societies ward off<br>***à la fois*** the two thresholds,<br>***tout en les anticipant*** <span style="color:green">[both at once… while anticipating them — the ¶16–17 double operation, now aimed at two targets]</span>. Lévi-Strauss shows that the same villages are<br>***susceptibles de deux présentations*** <span style="color:green">[susceptible of two presentations — the geometer's idiom: one figure, two constructions]</span>, one segmentary and egalitarian, the other<br>***englobante*** and hierarchized <span style="color:green">[englobing — englober: to swallow into a globe, encompass: the ¶17 imperial figure in an adjective]</span>. There is there something like<br>***deux potentiels*** <span style="color:green">[two potentials — the ¶16 physics term (potentiel anticipé) returned: the village as field with two latent solutions]</span>, one that anticipates a central point common to two horizontal segments, the other, on the contrary, a central point exterior to a straight line¹<br>⁷<br>. It is that primitive societies<br>***ne manquent pas de*** formations of power <span style="color:green">[do not lack — manquer, the gap-verb (¶11): the lack denied, against the whole anthropology of the "not yet"]</span>: they even have many. But, what prevents the potential central points from<br>***cristalliser, de prendre consistance*** <span style="color:green">[crystallizing, taking on consistency — the ¶12 chemistry and the ¶17 setting-idiom fused: the two thresholds named in their own registers]</span>, these are precisely the mechanisms that<br>***font que*** these formations of power do not resonate together in the superior point,<br>***pas plus qu'elles ne*** polarize in the common point <span style="color:green">[make it so that… no more than they — the faire que causative governing a double negation: prevention engineered, symmetrical]</span>: the circles in effect are not concentric, and the two segments<br>***ont besoin d'un troisième*** <span style="color:green">[need a third — the third term as circuit-breaker: communication routed to prevent closure, fn. 18]</span> by which they communicate¹<br>⁸<br>. It is in this sense that primitive societies<br>***restent en deçà du*** seuil-ville as much as the seuil-État <span style="color:green">[remain on the hither side of — en-deçà, the ¶17 spatial term: position held short of the threshold, not a crossing not-yet-made]</span>. If we consider now the two thresholds of consistency, we see well that they imply a deterritorialization, in relation to the primitive territorial codes. And<br>***il est vain de se demander*** <span style="color:green">[it is vain to ask oneself — the futility-formula: the question disqualified before ¶22 answers why]</span>…<br>¹<br>⁷<br>Lévi-Strauss, *Anthropologie structurale*, Plon, pp. 167–168. |
| ¶22 fr.539 umn.433 | Sur un exemple précis, Louis Berthe analyse la nécessité d'un « troisième village », empêchant le circuit orienté de se fermer : « Aînés et cadets, l'alliance et la hiérarchie chez les Baduj », pp. 214-215. demander ce qui est premier, de la ville ou de l'Etat, de la révolution urbaine ou étatique, puisque les deux sont en présupposition réciproque. Il faut les deux pour opérer le striage de l'espace, lignes mélodiques des villes, coupes harmoniques des Etats. La seule question qui se pose est celle de la possibilité d'un rapport inverse au sein de cette réciprocité. Car, si l'Etat archaïque impérial comporte nécessairement des villes considérables, ces villes lui restent d'autant plus subordonnées que le Palais garde le monopole du commerce extérieur. Au contraire, la ville tend à s'émanciper lorsque le surcodage d'Etat provoque luimême des flux décodés. Un décodage se joint à la déterritorialisation, et l'amplifie : le recodage nécessaire passe alors par une certaine autonomie des villes, ou bien directement par des villes commerçantes et corporatives libérées de la forme-Etat. C'est en ce sens que surgissent des cités qui n'ont plus de rapport avec leur propre terre, parce qu'elles assurent le commerce entre empires, ou, mieux, constituent elles-mêmes avec d'autres cités un réseau commercial affranchi. Il y a donc une aventure propre des villes dans les zones les plus intenses de décodage : ainsi dans le monde égéen de l'Antiquité, dans le monde occidental du Moyen Age et de la Renaissance. Et ne pourrait-on pas dire que le capitalisme est le fruit des villes, et surgit quand un recodage urbain tend à remplacer le surcodage d'Etat ? Ce ne serait pas vrai toutefois . Ce ne sont pas les villes qui créent le capitalisme. | urban or state revolution, because the two are in reciprocal presupposition. Both the melodic lines of the towns and the harmonic cross sections of the States are necessary to effect the striation of space. The only question that arises is the possibility that there may be an inverse relation at the heart of this reciprocity. For although the archaic imperial State necessarily included towns of considerable size, they remained more or less strictly subordinated to the State, depending on how complete the State's monopoly over foreign trade was. On the other hand, the town tended to break free when the State's overcoding itself provoked decoded flows. A decoding was coupled with the deterritorialization, and amplified it; the necessary recoding was then achieved through a certain autonomy of the towns, or else directly through corporative and commercial towns freed from the State-form. Thus towns arose that no longer had a connection to their own land, because they assured the trade between empires, or better, constituted on their own a free commercial network with other towns. There is therefore an adventure specific to towns in the zones where the most intense decoding occurs, for example, the ancient Aegean world or the Western world of the Middle Ages and the Renaissance. Could it not be said that capitalism is the fruit of the towns, and arises when an urban recoding tends to replace State overcoding? This, however, was not the case. The towns did not create capitalism. | ¹<br>⁸<br>On a precise example, Louis Berthe analyzes the necessity of a « third village », preventing the oriented circuit from<br>***se fermer*** <span style="color:green">[closing itself — the reflexive: the circuit that must be kept from self-closure]</span>: « Aînés et cadets, l'alliance et la hiérarchie chez les Baduj », pp. 214–215.<br><span style="color:green">(main text, completing ¶21's suspended sentence:)</span> …<br>***ce qui est premier*** <span style="color:green">[what is first — the Proposition XI title (¶8) returned as the disqualified question: essence-priority, asked in vain]</span>, of the town or the State, of the urban or the State revolution, since the two are in<br>***présupposition réciproque*** <span style="color:green">[reciprocal presupposition — the pre- chain closed into a loop: each the other's prerequisite]</span>.<br>***Il faut les deux pour opérer le striage de l'espace*** <span style="color:green">[both are needed to operate the striation of space — strier: the smooth/striated term of the plateaus; opérer again]</span>,<br>***lignes mélodiques des villes, coupes harmoniques des États*** <span style="color:green">[melodic lines of the towns, harmonic cuts of the States — the ¶19 counterpoint completed: melody horizontal, harmony vertical, coupe once more the cut-as-section — space striated as a score]</span>. The only question that<br>***se pose*** is that of the possibility of an inverse relation<br>***au sein de*** this reciprocity <span style="color:green">[poses itself… in the bosom of — the reflexive; au sein de: within, at the breast of: the inversion nursed inside the loop]</span>. For, if the archaic imperial State necessarily<br>***comporte*** considerable towns <span style="color:green">[comprises — the inventory-verb (¶7, ¶13)]</span>, these towns remain subordinated to it<br>***d'autant plus que*** the Palace keeps the monopoly of foreign commerce <span style="color:green">[all the more as — the proportional-causal chain (¶3, ¶16, ¶20): subordination scaling with monopoly]</span>. On the contrary, the town<br>***tend à s'émanciper*** <span style="color:green">[tends to emancipate itself — émanciper: the manumission register, the slave freed; reflexive: self-freeing]</span> when the State surcodage itself provokes decoded flows. A decoding<br>***se joint à*** the deterritorialization, and amplifies it <span style="color:green">[joins itself to — the reflexive again]</span>: the necessary recoding then<br>***passe par*** a certain autonomy of the towns <span style="color:green">[passes by way of — the routing idiom (¶13–15)]</span>, or else directly by commercial and corporative towns<br>***affranchies de la forme-État*** <span style="color:green">[freed from the State-form — affranchir: manumission again, the freedman's word: towns as freed slaves of the State]</span>. It is in this sense that there<br>***surgissent*** cities <span style="color:green">[loom up — the surgir chain (¶2, ¶7, ¶12): cities as apparitions now]</span> that no longer have a relation with their own land, because they<br>***assurent le commerce*** between empires <span style="color:green">[assure the commerce — assurer, the underwriter's register (¶4): the cities as guarantors of trade between others]</span>, or, better, themselves constitute with other cities a commercial network<br>***affranchi***. There is thus an<br>***aventure propre des villes*** <span style="color:green">[an adventure proper to the towns — aventure: the venture register, commercial adventure (merchant-adventurers) and romance both]</span> in the most intense zones of decoding: thus in the Aegean world of Antiquity, in the Western world of the Middle Ages and the Renaissance. And<br>***ne pourrait-on pas dire que*** <span style="color:green">[could one not say that — the rhetorical conditional question: the thesis floated as temptation, third of the plateau's staged hypotheses (¶4, ¶16)]</span> capitalism is the fruit of the towns, and<br>***surgit*** when an urban recoding tends to replace State surcodage?<br>***Ce ne serait pas vrai toutefois*** <span style="color:green">[this would not be true, however — the conditional verdict: the temptation refused in its own mood]</span>. It is not the towns that create capitalism. |
| ¶23 fr.540 umn.434 | C'est que les villes commerçantes et bancaires, avec leur improductivité, leur indifférence à l'arrière-pays, n'opèrent pas un recodage sans inhiber aussi la conjugaison générale des flux décodés. S'il est vrai qu'elles anticipent le capitalisme, à leur tour elles ne l'anticipent pas sans le conjurer. Elles sont en deçà de ce nouveau seuil. Il faut donc étendre l'hypothèse de mécanismes à, la fois anticipateurs et inhibiteurs : ces mécanismes jouent dans les villes « contre » l'Etat et « contre » le capitalisme, et pas seulement dans les sociétés primitives. Finalement, c'est par la forme-Etat et non par la forme-ville que le capitalisme triomphera : quand les Etats occidentaux seront devenus des modèles de réalisation pour une axiomatique des flux décodés, et auront, à ce titre, réassujetti les villes . Comme dit Braudel, « chaque fois il y a deux coureurs\ l'Etat, la Ville » - deux formes et deux vitesses de déterritorialisation - , « et d'ordinaire l'Etat gagne ( . . . ), il a discipliné les villes, violemment ou non, avec un acharnement instinctif, où que nous tournions nos yeux à travers l'Europe entière ( . . . ), il a rejoint le galop des villes 19 ». A charge 19. Braudel, Civilisation matérielle et capitalisme\ pp. 391-400 (sur les rapports ville-Etat en Occident).
