Et, comme le remarque Braudel, une des de revanche pourtant ; en effet, si c'est l'Etat moderne qui donne au capitalisme ses modèles de réalisation, ce qui se trouve ainsi réalisé, c'est une axiomatique indépendante, mondiale, qui est comme une seule et même Ville, mégapolis ou « mégamachine » dont les Etats sont des parties, des quartiers. | The banking and commercial towns, being unproductive and indifferent to the backcountry, did not perform a recoding without also inhibiting the general conjunction of decoded flows. If it is true that they anticipated capitalism, they in turn did not anticipate it without also warding it off. They do not cross this new threshold. Thus it is necessary to expand the hypothesis of mechanisms both anticipatory and inhibiting: these mechanisms are at play not only in primitive societies but also in the conflict of towns "against" the State and "against" capitalism. Finally, it was through the State-form and not the town-form that capitalism triumphed; this occurred when the Western States became models of realization for an axiomatic of decoded flows, and in that way resubjugated the towns. As Braudel says, there were "always two runners, the state and the town"-two forms and two speeds of deterritorialization-and "the state usually won . . . . everywhere in Europe, it disciplined the towns with instinctive relentlessness, whether or not it used violence . . . . [The states] caught up with the forward gallop of the towns."19 But the relation is a reciprocal one: if it is the modern State that gives capitalism its models of realization, what is thus realized is an independent, worldwide axiomatic that is like a single City, 7000 B.C.: | It is that the commercial and banking towns, with their<br>***improductivité*** <span style="color:green">[unproductivity — the economist's charge worn as trait: towns that make nothing, move everything]</span>, their indifference to the<br>***arrière-pays*** <span style="color:green">[back-country — the hinterland turned away from, ¶19]</span>, do not operate a recoding without also inhibiting the general<br>***conjugaison des flux décodés*** <span style="color:green">[conjugation of decoded flows — conjugaison: the grammar register, flows conjugated together like a verb through its forms; a term of art distinct from mere conjunction]</span>.<br>***S'il est vrai que*** they anticipate capitalism <span style="color:green">[if it is true that — the ¶3 concessive grant]</span>,<br>***à leur tour*** they do not anticipate it<br>***sans le conjurer*** <span style="color:green">[in their turn… without warding it off — the ¶16 law (conjurer, c'est aussi anticiper) applied to the towns themselves: the anticipators become warders]</span>. They are<br>***en deçà de*** this new threshold <span style="color:green">[on the hither side of — en-deçà, third occurrence: position, not failed crossing]</span>.<br>***Il faut donc étendre l'hypothèse*** <span style="color:green">[one must therefore extend the hypothesis — falloir: the argument by requirement, the ¶16 mechanism generalized]</span> of mechanisms at once anticipatory and inhibitory: these mechanisms<br>***jouent*** <span style="color:green">[play — jouer: mechanisms at play, the gaming and clockwork registers both: play as operation and as slack]</span> in the towns « against » the State and « against » capitalism, and not only in primitive societies. Finally, it is by the<br>***forme-État*** and not by the<br>***forme-ville*** that capitalism will triumph: when the Western States<br>***seront devenus*** models of realization <span style="color:green">[will have become — future perfect: the triumph dated from a vantage before it, the plateau's reverse causality in the grammar itself]</span> for an<br>***axiomatique des flux décodés*** <span style="color:green">[axiomatic of decoded flows — the mathematics register: axioms operating directly on flows, without codes]</span>, and will have,<br>***à ce titre*** <span style="color:green">[under that title — the title-formula chain (¶16 au titre de): the resubjection performed by legal title]</span>,<br>***réassujetti*** the towns <span style="color:green">[resubjected — assujettir: made subject again, the sujet inside subjection]</span>. As Braudel says, « each time there are two<br>***coureurs*** <span style="color:green">[runners — the race register: State and Town as competitors on a course]</span>, the State, the Town » — two forms and two speeds of deterritorialization —, « and ordinarily the State wins (…), it has disciplined the towns, violently or not, with an instinctive<br>***acharnement*** <span style="color:green">[relentlessness — acharnement: from chair, flesh — the hound's fury on the carcass, fleshed persistence]</span>, wherever we turn our eyes across the whole of Europe (…), it has<br>***rejoint le galop*** of the towns¹<br>⁹<br>» <span style="color:green">[rejoined the gallop — caught up with: the race run down; translated from the supplied French pending verified English edition]</span>.<br>***À charge de revanche pourtant*** <span style="color:green">[with a return-match owed, however — à charge de revanche: the idiom of the accepted favor carrying an obligation to repay in kind: the State wins, but the debt of a rematch is booked — and the City collects in the next sentence]</span>; in effect, if it is the modern State that gives capitalism its models of realization,<br>***ce qui se trouve ainsi réalisé*** <span style="color:green">[what finds itself thus realized — the reflexive: realization without a realizer]</span> is an independent, worldwide axiomatic, which is like<br>***une seule et même Ville*** <span style="color:green">[one single and same City — capital V: the Town's revenge as apotheosis, all towns one]</span>, megalopolis or « megamachine » <span style="color:green">[Mumford's word returned (¶8): the imperial megamachine reborn as world-city]</span> of which the States are<br>***des parties, des quartiers*** <span style="color:green">[parts, quarters — quartier: the city district, the neighborhood: States as boroughs of the world-City, the ¶7 parties demoted to municipal subdivisions]</span>.<br>¹<br>⁹<br>Braudel, *Civilisation matérielle et capitalisme*, pp. 391–400 (on the town-State relations in the West). And, as Braudel remarks, one of the… |
| ¶24 fr.541 umn.435 | Nous définissons les formations sociales par des processus machiniques, et non pas des modes de production ( qui dépendent au contraire des processus). Ainsi les sociétés primitives se définissent par des mécanismes de conjuration-anticipation ; les sociétés à Etat se définissent par des appareils de capture ; les sociétés urbaines, par des instruments de polarisation ; les sociétés nomades, par des machines de guerre ; les organisations internationales, ou plutôt œcuméniques, se définissent enfin par l'englobement de formations sociales hétérogènes. Or, précisément parce que ces processus sont des variables de coexistence qui font l'objet d'une topologie sociale, les diverses formations correspondantes coexistent. Et elles coexistent de deux façons, de manière extrinsèque et de manière intrinsèque. D'une part, en effet, les sociétés primitives ne conjurent pas la formation d'empire ou d'Etat sans l'anticiper, et ne l'anticipent pas sans qu'elle soit déjà là, faisant partie de leur horizon. Les Etats n'opèrent pas de capture sans que le capturé ne coexiste, ne résiste dans les sociétés primitives, ou ne fuie sous de nouvelles formes, villes, machines de guerre. . . La composition numérique des machines de guerre se superpose à l'organisation lignagère primitive, et simultanément s'oppose à l'organisation géométrique d'Etat, à l'organisation physique de ville. C'est cette coexistence extrinsèque - interaction - qui s'exprime pour elle-même dans les ensembles internationaux. Car ceux-ci n'ont certes pas attendu le capitalisme pour se former : dès le néolithique, même dès le paléolithique, on trouve les traces d'organisations œcuméniques qui témoignent d'un commerce à longue distance, et qui traversent simultanément les formations sociales les plus diverses ( nous l'avons vu pour la métallurgie). | We define social formations by machinic processes and not by modes of production (these on the contrary depend on the processes). Thus primitive societies are defined by mechanisms of prevention-anticipation; State societies are defined by apparatuses of capture; urban societies, by instruments of polarization; nomadic societies, by war machines; and finally international, or rather ecumenical, organizations are defined by the encompassment of heterogeneous social formations. But precisely because these processes are variables of coexistence that are the object of a social topology, the various corresponding formations are coexistent. And they coexist in two fashions, extrinsically and intrinsically. Primitive societies cannot ward off the formation of an empire or State without anticipating it, and they cannot anticipate it without its already being there, forming part of their horizon. And States cannot effect a capture unless what is captured coexists, resists in primitive societies, or escapes under new forms, as towns or war machines . . . The numerical composition of the war machine is superposed upon the primitive lineal organization and simultaneously opposes the geometric organization of the State and the physical organization of the town. It is this extrinsic coexistence-interaction-that is brought to its own expression in international aggregates. For these obviously did not wait for capitalism before forming: as early as Neolithic times, even Paleolithic, we find traces of ecumenical organizations that testif y to the existence of long-distance trade, and simultaneously cut across the most varied of social formations (as we have seen in the case of metallurgy). | We define social formations by<br>***processus machiniques*** <span style="color:green">[machinic processes — process against mode: what runs, not how production is organized]</span>, and not by modes of production (which on the contrary depend on the processes). Thus primitive societies<br>***se définissent par*** <span style="color:green">[define themselves by — the reflexive series: formations self-defining through their process]</span> mechanisms of<br>***conjuration-anticipation*** <span style="color:green">[warding-anticipation — the hyphenated pair: the ¶16 aphorism become a compound noun; conjurer, the plateau's constant]</span>; State societies define themselves by<br>***appareils de capture***; urban societies, by instruments of polarization; nomad societies, by machines de guerre; international, or rather<br>***œcuméniques***, organizations <span style="color:green">[ecumenical — oikoumenê, the inhabited world: the whole-earth register, ecclesial history included]</span>, define themselves finally by the<br>***englobement*** of heterogeneous social formations <span style="color:green">[englobing — the ¶21 englobante: swallowing-into-a-globe as process-noun]</span>.<br>***Or***, precisely because these processes are variables of coexistence that<br>***font l'objet d'une topologie sociale*** <span style="color:green">[are the object of a social topology — faire l'objet de: the set idiom of scholarly treatment, the processes as what the topology studies]</span>, the diverse corresponding formations coexist. And they coexist in two fashions, in an extrinsic manner and in an intrinsic manner.<br>***D'une part, en effet*** <span style="color:green">[on the one hand, indeed — the scaffold-marker: its d'autre part is deferred to ¶28's intrinsic coexistence, the paragraph-pair bridged]</span>, primitive societies do not ward off the formation of empire or State without anticipating it, and do not anticipate it<br>***sans qu'elle soit déjà là*** <span style="color:green">[without its being already there — the déjà chain]</span>, forming part of their horizon. States<br>***n'opèrent pas de capture sans que le capturé ne coexiste, ne résiste… ou ne fuie*** <span style="color:green">[operate no capture without the captured coexisting, resisting, or fleeing — the triple ne-subjunctive; fuir: the flight-verb of the lignes de fuite: the captured escapes as flight, into towns, war machines]</span> under new forms, towns, machines de guerre… The numerical composition of the<br>***machines de guerre*** <span style="color:green">[war machines — plural in the French]</span> superposes itself on the primitive lineage organization, and simultaneously<br>***s'oppose à*** the geometric organization of State, the physical organization of town <span style="color:green">[opposes itself — se superpose / s'oppose: the reflexive near-rhyme, laying-over and setting-against as twin gestures]</span>. It is this extrinsic coexistence — interaction — that<br>***s'exprime pour elle-même*** <span style="color:green">[expresses itself for itself — the ¶12 pour elle-même: extrinsic coexistence attaining its own expression, no longer by-product]</span> in the international ensembles. For these<br>***n'ont certes pas attendu le capitalisme pour se former*** <span style="color:green">[certainly did not wait for capitalism to form themselves — ne pas attendre X pour: the idiom of the head start, doing without waiting for permission]</span>: from the Neolithic, even from the Paleolithic, one finds the traces of ecumenical organizations that<br>***témoignent*** of a long-distance commerce <span style="color:green">[testify — the witness-register (¶16, ¶18)]</span>, and that traverse simultaneously the most diverse social formations (we have seen it for metallurgy). |
| ¶25 fr.541 umn.435 | Le problème de la diffusion, du difEusionnisme, est mal posé tant qu'on présuppose un centre à partir duquel la diffusion se ferait. Il n'y a de diffusion que par mise en communication de potentiels d'ordre très différents raisons de la victoire des Etats sur les villes à partir du xve siècle, c'est que l'Etat seul a la faculté de s'approprier pleinement la machine de guerre : par recrutement territorial des hommes, investissement matériel, industrialisation de la guerre (c'est dans les manufactures d'armes plus que dans les fabriques d'épingles que la production en série et la division mécanique apparaissent) . Les villes commerçantes au contraire ont besoin de guerres rapides, recourent à des mercenaires, et ne peuvent qu'encaster la machine de guerre. toute diffusion procède au milieu, par le milieu, comme tout ce qui < pousse », du type rhizome. Une organisation internationale œcuménique ne procède pas d'un centre impérial qui s'imposerait à un milieu extérieur pour l'homogénéiser ; elle ne se réduit pas davantage à des relations entre formations de même ordre, par exemple entre Etats ( S. D. N . , O . N. U . . . ) . Au contraire, elle constitue un milieu intermédiaire entre les différents ordres coexistants. Aussi bien n'est-elle pas économique ou commerciale exclusivement, elle est aussi bien religieuse, artistique, etc. C'est en ce sens qu'on appellera organisation internationale tout ce qui a l'aptitude de passer par des formations sociales diverses, simultanément, Etats, villes, déserts, machines de guerre, sociétés primitives. Les grandes formations commerçantes historiques n'ont pas simplement des cités-pôles, mais des segments primitifs, impériaux, nomades, par lesquels elles passent, quitte à ressortir sous une autre forme. | The problem of diff usion, or of diff usionism, is badly formulated if one assumes a center at which the diff usion would begin. Diff usion occurs only through the placing in communication of potentials of very different orders: all diffusion happens in the in-between, goes between, like everything that "grows" of the rhizome type. An international ecumenical organization does not proceed from an imperial center that imposes itself upon and homogenizes an exterior milieu; neither is it reducible to relations between formations of the same order, between States, for example (the League of Nations, the United Nations). On the contrary, it constitutes an intermediate milieu between the dif ferent coexistent orders. Therefore it is not exclusively commercial or economic, but is also religious, artistic, etc. From this standpoint, we shall call an international organization anything that has the capacity to move through diverse social formations simultaneously: States, towns, deserts, war machines, primitive societies. The great commercial formations in history do not simply have city-poles, but also primitive, imperial, and nomadic segments through which they pass, perhaps issuing out again in another form. | ¹<br>⁹<br>ᶜ<br>. …one of the reasons for the victory of the States over the towns from the fifteenth century on is that the State alone has the faculty of<br>***s'approprier pleinement*** the war machine <span style="color:green">[appropriating to itself fully — the ¶15 reflexive of taking-as-one's-own]</span>: by territorial recruitment of men, material investment, industrialization of war (it is in the<br>***manufactures d'armes*** more than in the<br>***fabriques d'épingles*** <span style="color:green">[arms manufactories… pin factories — Adam Smith's pin factory: serial production and mechanical division born in the armory, not the pin-works — political economy's founding example displaced]</span> that serial production and mechanical division appear). The commercial towns on the contrary need rapid wars,<br>***recourent à*** mercenaries <span style="color:green">[have recourse to — the legal recourse-idiom]</span>, and<br>***ne peuvent qu'encaster*** the war machine <span style="color:green">[can only encast — the ¶2 coinage returned at the fn.'s point: the towns' constitutive limit stated in the emperor's verb]</span>.<br>The problem of diffusion, of diffusionism, is<br>***mal posé*** <span style="color:green">[badly posed — the problem-posing register (¶12, problème toujours posé): fault in the position, not the answer]</span> as long as one presupposes a center<br>***à partir duquel la diffusion se ferait*** <span style="color:green">[from which the diffusion would make itself — conditional + reflexive: the presupposed origin voiced hypothetically]</span>. There is diffusion only by<br>***mise en communication*** <span style="color:green">[placing in communication — the mise en X operative formula: communication as an act performed, not a state]</span> of<br>***potentiels*** of very different orders <span style="color:green">[potentials — the ¶16/¶21 physics chain: diffusion as discharge between unequal potentials]</span>: all diffusion proceeds<br>***au milieu, par le milieu*** <span style="color:green">[in the middle, by the middle — the milieu chain at full statement (¶5, ¶20): the middle as both place and means]</span>, like everything that «<br>***pousse*** », of the rhizome type <span style="color:green">[« grows », pushes up — pousser: the plant's push; the cross-plateau citation: grass, the Rhizome plateau's own figure, named in its home verb]</span>. An international ecumenical organization does not proceed from an imperial center that<br>***s'imposerait à*** an exterior milieu to homogenize it <span style="color:green">[would impose itself on — conditional + reflexive: the imperial reading refused in its own mood]</span>;<br>***elle ne se réduit pas davantage à*** relations between formations of the same order <span style="color:green">[no more does it reduce itself to — davantage (¶14) + the reflexive réduire (¶13)]</span>, for example between States (S.D.N., O.N.U.…). On the contrary, it constitutes an<br>***intermediate milieu*** between the different coexistent orders.<br>***Aussi bien n'est-elle pas*** exclusively economic or commercial,<br>***elle est aussi bien*** religious, artistic, etc. <span style="color:green">[just as well… just as well — the ¶19 equalizer doubled, with formal inversion: the registers leveled twice over]</span> It is in this sense that one will call international organization everything that has the<br>***aptitude de passer par*** diverse social formations simultaneously <span style="color:green">[aptitude to pass by way of — the routing idiom as capacity: transit as the defining talent]</span>, States, towns, deserts, machines de guerre, primitive societies. The great historical commercial formations do not simply have city-poles, but primitive, imperial, nomad segments, by which they pass,<br>***quitte à ressortir sous une autre forme*** <span style="color:green">[at the cost of coming out again under another form — quitte à (¶20): the risk-idiom, transformation as the booked price of transit, not a chance outcome]</span>. |
| ¶26 fr.542 umn.435 | Samir Amin a profondément raison de dire qu'il n'y a pas de théorie économique des relations internationales, même quand ces relations sont économiques, et cela parce qu'elles sont à cheval sur des formations hétérogènes 20 . Une organisation œcuménique ne part pas d'un Etat même impérial, l'Etat impérial en fait seulement partie, et il en fait partie sur son propre mode, à la mesure de son ordre, qui consiste à en capturer tout ce qu'il peut. Elle ne procède pas par homogénéisation progressive, ni par totalisation, mais par prise de consistance ou consolidation du divers en tant que tel. Par exemple, la religion monothéiste se distingue du culte territorial par une prétention d'universalité. Mais cette prétention n'est pas homogénéisante, elle ne vaut qu'à force de passer partout : tel le christianisme, qui ne devient pas d'empire et de ville sans susciter aussi ses bandes, ses déserts, ses machines de guerre 21 . De même, pas de mouvement artistique qui n'ait ses villes et ses empires, mais aussi ses nomades, ses bandes et ses primitifs. On peut objecter que, du moins avec le capitalisme, les relations économiques internationales, et à la limite toutes les relations internationales, tendent à l'homogénéisation des formations sociales. On citera non seulement la froide destruction concertée des sociétés primitives, mais aussi la chute des dernières formations despotiques - par exemple l'empire ottoman, qui opposait trop de résistance et d'inertie aux exigences capitalistes. Toutefois 20. C'est un thème souvent développé par Samir Amin : « Puisque la théorie des relations entre formations sociales différentes ne peut être économiste, les relations internationales, qui se situent précisément dans ce cadre, ne peuvent donner lieu à une théorie économique » (Le développement inégal, Ed. de Minuit, pp. 124 sq.). 21. Cf. Jacques Lacarrière, Les hommes ivres de Dieu, Fayard. | Samir Amin is totally correct in saying that there can be no economic theory of international relations, even economic ones, because they sit astride heterogeneous formations.20 The point of departure for ecumenical organization is not a State, even an imperial one; the imperial State is only one part of it, and it constitutes a part of it in its own mode, according to its own order, which consists in capturing everything it can. It does not proceed by progressive homogenization, or by totalization, but by the taking on of consistency or the consolidation of the diverse as such. For example, monotheistic religion is distinguished from territorial worship by its pretension to universality. But this pretension is not homogenizing, it makes itself felt only by spreading everywhere; this was the case with Christianity, which became imperial and urban, but not without giving rise to bands, deserts, war machines of its own.21 Similarly, there is no artistic movement that does not have its towns and empires, but also its nomads, bands, and primitives.<br><br>It might be objected that, at least in the case of capitalism, international economic relations, and at the limit all international relations, tend toward the homogenization of social formations. One could cite not only the cold and concerted destruction of primiti ve societies but also the fall of the last despotic formations, for example, the Ottoman Empire, which met capitalist demands with too much resistance and inertia. This objection, however, is only partially accurate. | Samir Amin<br>***a profondément raison de dire*** <span style="color:green">[is profoundly right to say — avoir raison: rightness possessed, held like property]</span> that there is no economic theory of international relations, even when these relations are economic, and this because they are<br>***à cheval sur*** heterogeneous formations²<br>⁰<br><span style="color:green">[astride — on horseback over: the rider's seat across two mounts; the equestrian idiom in the nomads' own plateau]</span>. An ecumenical organization<br>***ne part pas d'un État*** even imperial <span style="color:green">[does not depart from a State — partir de: no point of departure, the diffusion thesis of ¶25 applied]</span>, the imperial State only<br>***en fait partie, et il en fait partie sur son propre mode*** <span style="color:green">[forms part of it, and forms part of it in its own mode — faire partie: the parties/quartiers chain (¶7, ¶23): the empire as member, membership exercised each in its manner]</span>,<br>***à la mesure de son ordre*** <span style="color:green">[to the measure of its order — the mesure chain (¶16): participation scaled to one's own metric]</span>, which consists in capturing from it all that it can. It does not proceed by progressive homogenization, nor by totalization, but by<br>***prise de consistance*** <span style="color:green">[taking-on of consistency — prise: the capture-noun (¶18 fn., ¶20) fused with the ¶17 setting-idiom: consistency itself as a seizure]</span> or consolidation of the diverse<br>***en tant que tel*** <span style="color:green">[as such — the capacity-formula (¶8): diversity consolidated under its own title]</span>. For example, monotheistic religion distinguishes itself from territorial cult by a<br>***prétention d'universalité*** <span style="color:green">[claim of universality — prétention, the legal claim (¶19): universality as title asserted, not fact possessed]</span>. But this claim is not homogenizing, it<br>***ne vaut qu'à force de passer partout*** <span style="color:green">[avails only by dint of passing everywhere — à force de: worth accumulated through repeated effort; valoir (¶14) + the routing passer: the claim earns its validity in transit]</span>: such is Christianity, which<br>***ne devient pas d'empire et de ville sans susciter aussi*** <span style="color:green">[does not become of-empire and of-city without also calling forth — devenir de: the genitive becoming, belonging-to as a becoming; susciter (¶10): raising up its own outsides]</span> its bands, its deserts, its machines de guerre²¹.<br>***De même***, no artistic movement that does not have its towns and its empires, but also its nomads, its<br>***bandes*** and its primitives <span style="color:green">[bands — the pack-register: the gang, the troop, the war machine's social form]</span>.<br>***On peut objecter que*** <span style="color:green">[one can object that — the staged objection: the adversary given the floor, ¶16's concessive question renewed]</span>,<br>***du moins*** with capitalism, international economic relations, and<br>***à la limite*** all international relations <span style="color:green">[at the limit — the ¶3 limit-case operator]</span>, tend toward the homogenization of social formations.<br>***On citera*** <span style="color:green">[one will cite — the instruction-future: the objection's evidence marshaled for it]</span> not only the cold concerted destruction of primitive societies, but also the fall of the last despotic formations — for example the Ottoman empire, which opposed too much resistance and inertia to the capitalist exigencies.<br>***Toutefois*** <span style="color:green">[however — the pivot left hanging: the French sentence breaks across the page into ¶27, the objection suspended mid-turn]</span><br>²<br>⁰<br>It is a theme often developed by Samir Amin: « Since the theory of relations between different social formations cannot be economistic, international relations, which situate themselves precisely in this frame, cannot<br>***donner lieu à*** an economic theory » <span style="color:green">[give place to — the lieu-idiom: no ground on which such a theory could stand; Amin is French-original, translated from the supplied French]</span> (*Le développement inégal*, Éd. de Minuit, pp. 124 ff.).<br>²¹ Cf. Jacques Lacarrière, *Les hommes ivres de Dieu*, Fayard. |
| ¶27 fr.543 umn.436 | cette objection n'est que partiellement juste. Dans la mesure où le capitalisme constitue une axiomatique ( production pour le marché), tous les Etats et toutes les formations sociales tendent à devenir isomorphes, au titre de modèles de réalisation : il n'y a qu'un seul marché mondial centré, le capitaliste, auquel participent même les pays dits socialistes. L'organisation mondiale cesse donc de passer « entre » des formes hétérogènes, puisqu'elle assure l'isomorphie des formations. Mais on aurait tort de confondre l'isomorphisme avec une homogénéité. D'une part, l'isomorphie laisse subsister ou même suscite une grande hétérogénéité des Etats \les Etats démocratiques, totalitaires, à plus forte raison les Etats « socialistes », ne sont pas des façades ) . D'autre part, l'axiomatique capitaliste internationale n'assure effectivement l'isomorphie des formations diverses que là où le marché intérieur se développe et s'élargit, c'est-à-dire « au centre ». Mais elle supporte, bien plus, elle exige une certaine polymorphie périphérique, pour autant qu'elle n'est pas saturée, pour autant qu'elle repousse activement ses propres limites : d'où l'existence de formations sociales hétéromorphes, à la périphérie, qui ne constituent certes pas des survivances ou des formes transitionnelles, puisqu'elles réalisent une production capitaliste ultra-moderne ( pétrole, mines, plantations, biens d'équipements, sidérurgie, chimie . . . ), mais qui n'en sont pas moins précapitalistes, ou extra-capitalistes, en raison d'autres aspects de leur production, et de l'inadéquation forcée de leur marché intérieur au marché mondial 22 . | To the extent that capitalism constitutes an axiomatic (production for the market), all States and all social formations tend to become isomorphic in their capacity as models of realization: there is but one centered world market, the capitalist one, in which even the so-called socialist countries participate. Worldwide organization thus ceases to pass "between" heterogeneous formations since it assures the isomorphy of those formations. But it would be wrong to confuse isomorphy with homogeneity. For one thing, isomorphy allows, and even incites, a great heterogeneity among States (democratic, totalitarian, and, especially, "socialist" States are not facades). For another thing, the international capitalist axiomatic effectively assures the isomorphy of the diverse formations only where the domestic market is developing and expanding, in other words, in "the center." But it tolerates, in fact it requires, a certain peripheral polymorphy, to the extent that it is not saturated, to the extent that it actively repels its own limits;22 this explains the existence, at the periphery, of heteromorphic social formations, which certainly do not constitute vestiges or transitional forms since they realize an ultramodern capitalist production (oil, mines, plantations, industrial equipment, steel, chemistry), but which are nonetheless precapitalist, or extracapitalist, owing to other aspects of their production and to the forced inadequacy of their domestic market in relation to the world market.23 | …this objection is only partially just.<br>***Dans la mesure où*** <span style="color:green">[in the measure that — the mesure chain again: extent as measurement]</span> capitalism constitutes an axiomatic (production for the market), all States and all social formations tend to become isomorphic,<br>***au titre de modèles de réalisation*** <span style="color:green">[under the title of models of realization — the titre-formula (¶16, ¶23): isomorphy held as legal capacity]</span>: there is only one centered world market, the capitalist one, in which even the countries<br>***dits*** socialist participate <span style="color:green">[so-called — dit (¶12): the label held at arm's length]</span>. Worldwide organization therefore ceases to pass « between » heterogeneous forms, since it<br>***assure*** the isomorphy of the formations <span style="color:green">[assures — the underwriter (¶4, ¶22): isomorphy guaranteed like a policy]</span>. But<br>***on aurait tort de confondre*** isomorphism with a homogeneity <span style="color:green">[one would be wrong to confuse — conditional + confondre: the ¶2/¶17 distinction-verb, the treatise's standing warning-form]</span>.<br>***D'une part***, isomorphy<br>***laisse subsister ou même suscite*** a great heterogeneity of States <span style="color:green">[lets subsist or even calls forth — the tolerance/production pair; susciter again: difference not merely permitted but raised up]</span> (democratic, totalitarian States,<br>***à plus forte raison*** the « socialist » States, are not façades) <span style="color:green">[with all the more reason — the a fortiori formula: the strongest case placed last]</span>.<br>***D'autre part***, the international capitalist axiomatic effectively assures the isomorphy of the diverse formations only<br>***là où*** the interior market develops and enlarges itself, that is to say « at the center ». But it<br>***supporte, bien plus, elle exige*** <span style="color:green">[supports — bears, tolerates as a load; bien plus (¶17): the escalation — endurance upgraded to requirement mid-sentence]</span> a certain peripheral polymorphy,<br>***pour autant que*** it is not saturated,<br>***pour autant que*** it<br>***repousse activement ses propres limites*** <span style="color:green">[insofar as… it actively pushes back its own limits — repousser: the ¶16 verb of the receding convergence-point, now reflexively aimed: what primitive societies did to the State-limit, capitalism does to its own]</span>:<br>***d'où*** the existence of heteromorphic social formations, at the periphery, which certainly do not constitute<br>***survivances*** <span style="color:green">[survivals — the anthropologist's term of art (Tylor): relics of an earlier stage — the evolutionist concept named in order to be refused]</span> or transitional forms, since they realize an ultra-modern capitalist production (oil, mines, plantations, capital goods, iron and steel, chemistry…), but which<br>***n'en sont pas moins*** precapitalist, or extra-capitalist <span style="color:green">[are not the less for it — the ¶16 double-negative concessive: modernity granted, the verdict unchanged]</span>, by reason of other aspects of their production, and of the<br>***inadéquation forcée*** of their interior market to the world market²² <span style="color:green">[forced inadequacy — the forcée of ¶19 (coordination forcée): mismatch as something done to them, maintained]</span>.<br>²² <span style="color:green">(French fn. 22 = the Samir Amin note, delivered in ¶28's cell. NUMBERING FLAG: Minnesota prints a marker 22 mid-paragraph at "repels its own limits" and a marker 23 at "world market" — one more note than the French. Check the Minnesota endnotes at collation: either a translator's note is interpolated or the numbering has drifted; map before entering flags against either note.)</span> |
| ¶28 fr.543 umn.436 | Quand elle devient axiomatique capitaliste, l'organisation internationale continue d'impliquer l'hétérogénéité des formations sociales, elle suscite et organise son « tiers-monde » . I l n'y a pas seulement coexistence externe des formations, il y a aussi coexistence intrinsèque des processus machiniques. Anthropos, pp. 373-376).
