Chile) <span style="color:green">[the minimum State of anarcho-capitalism — the book's present tense: Pinochet's Chile as the laboratory]</span>.<br>***À la limite***, the only axioms<br>***retenus*** <span style="color:green">[retained — retenir (¶20): the State keeping-by-cutting, down to three axioms]</span> are the equilibrium of the external sector, the level of reserves and the rate of inflation; «<br>***la population n'est plus une donnée, elle est devenue une conséquence*** » <span style="color:green">[« the population is no longer a given, it has become a consequence » — the données chain (¶10) weaponized: the people demoted from the problem's data to its derivative; quotation unattributed in the supplied text — identify source (Virilio vicinity?) at collation]</span>; as for the<br>***évolutions sauvages***, they appear among others in the variations of employment, the phenomena of rural exodus, of<br>***urbanisation-bidonvilles***, etc. <span style="color:green">[shantytown-urbanization — bidonville: the tin-can town, bidon the oil-drum: the ville chain's slum-form, hyphenated into the process itself]</span> — The case of fascism («<br>***national-socialisme*** ») distinguishes itself from totalitarianism. |
| ¶85 fr.577 umn.462 | Car il coïncide avec le pôle totalitaire par l'écrasement du marché intérieur et la réduction des axiomes . Cependant, la promotion du secteur externe ne se fait pas du tout par appel aux capitaux extérieurs et industrie d'exportation, mais par économie de guerre, qui entraîne un expansionnisme étranger au totalitarisme et une fabrication autonome de capital. Quant au marché intérieur, il est effectué par une production spécifique d'Ersatz. Si bien que le fascisme comporte aussi une prolifération d'axiomes, qui fait qu'on l'a souvent rapproché d'une économie keynésienne. Seulement, c'est une prolifération fictive ou tautologique, un multiolicateur par soustraction, qui fait du fascisme un cas très spécial 56 . 2 . Saturation. - Peut-on distribuer les deux tendances inverses en disant que la saturation du système marque le point d'inversion ? Non, car c'est plutôt la saturation qui est elle-même relative. Si Marx a montré le fonctionnement du capitalisme comme une axiomatique, c'est surtout dans le chapitre célèbre sur la baisse | It coincides with the totalitarian pole in the collapse of the domestic market and the reduction in the number of axioms. However, the promotion of the foreign sector does not at all take place through an appeal to foreign sources of capital and through export industries, but through a war economy, which entails an expansionism foreign to totalitarianism and an autonomous fabrication of capital. As for the domestic market, it is eff ectuated in a specific production of the Ersatz. This means that fascism, too, brings a proliferation ofaxioms, which explains why it has of ten been compared to a Keynesian economy. Fascism, however, is a tautological or fictitious prolif eration, a multiplication by subtraction; this makes it a very special case.62<br>2. Saturation. Can we express the distribution of the two opposite tendencies by saying that the saturation of the system marks the point of inversion? No, for the saturation is itself relative. If Marx demonstrated the functioning of capitalism as an axiomatic, it was above all in the famous chapter on the tendency of the rate of profit to fall. | For it coincides with the totalitarian pole by the<br>***écrasement*** of the interior market and the reduction of the axioms <span style="color:green">[crushing — écraser (¶71, the nations crushing their minorities): active, agented — fascism CRUSHES the domestic market, where totalitarianism's market s'effondre, caves in (¶84): two verbs, two poles, one active one intransitive]</span>. However, the promotion of the external sector<br>***ne se fait pas du tout*** by appeal to exterior capitals and export industry, but by<br>***économie de guerre*** <span style="color:green">[war economy — the guerre chain in its fiscal uniform]</span>, which<br>***entraîne*** an expansionism<br>***étranger au totalitarisme*** <span style="color:green">[carries along an expansionism foreign to totalitarianism — étranger: the pun quietly working — no foreign capital, but an expansionism foreign-to]</span> and an autonomous fabrication of capital. As for the interior market, it is<br>***effectué*** by a specific production of<br>***Ersatz*** <span style="color:green">[Ersatz — the German kept: the substitute-goods economy named in the regime's own language]</span>. So that fascism<br>***comporte*** also a proliferation of axioms,<br>***qui fait qu'on l'a souvent rapproché d'une*** Keynesian economy. Only, it is a<br>***fictive or tautological proliferation, un multiplicateur par soustraction*** <span style="color:green">[a multiplier by subtraction — the arithmetic oxymoron: fascism's Keynesianism as a times-sign built from a minus]</span>, which<br>***fait du fascisme un cas très spécial***⁵<br><span style="color:green">[makes of fascism — the faire de X un Y signature]</span>.<br>***2 Saturation***. — Can one distribute the two<br>***inverse*** tendencies by saying that the<br>***saturation*** of the system marks the<br>***point d'inversion*** <span style="color:green">[point of inversion — the ¶50 term: the capture-point's formal name recalled]</span>? No, for it is rather saturation that is itself relative. If Marx showed the functioning of capitalism as an axiomatic, it is above all in the celebrated chapter on the<br>***baisse tendancielle du taux de profit*** <span style="color:green">[tendential fall of the rate of profit — Marx's term of art, the tendenzieller Fall: the law that is only a tendency]</span>. |
| ¶86 fr.578 umn.463 | tendancielle du taux de profit. Le capitalisme est bien une axiomatique parce qu'il n'a d'autres lois qu'immanentes. Il aimerait à faire croire qu'il se heurte aux limites de l'Univers, à l'extrême limite des ressources et des énergies. Mais il ne se heurte qu'à ses propres limites ( dépréciation périodique du capital existant), et ne repousse ou ne déplace que ses propres limites ( formation d'un capital nouveau, dans de nouvelles industries à fort taux de profit). C'est l'histoire du pétrole et du nucléaire. Et les deux à la fois : c'est en même temps que le capitalisme se heurte à S e S limites et qu'il les déplace, pour les poser plus loin. On dira que la tendance totalitaire, restreindre les axiomes, correspond à l'affrontement des limites, tandis que la tendance social-démocrate correspond au déplacement des limites. | Capitalism is indeed an axiomatic, because it has no laws but immanent ones. It would like for us to believe that it confronts the limits of the Universe, the extreme limit of resources and energy. But all it confronts are its own limits (the periodic depreciation of existing capital); all it repels or displaces are its own limits (the formation of new capital, in new industries with a high profit rate). This is the history of oil and nuclear power. And it does both at once: capitalism confronts its own limits and simultaneously displaces them, setting them down again farther along. It could be said that the totalitarian tendency to restrict the number of axioms corresponds to the confrontation with the limits, whereas the social democratic tendency corresponds to the displacement of the limits. | Capitalism is indeed an axiomatic because it has no other laws than<br>***immanentes***.<br>***Il aimerait à faire croire qu'il se heurte aux limites de l'Univers*** <span style="color:green">[it would like to make believe that it runs up against the limits of the Universe — the conditional + faire croire: belief manufactured (croire à, ¶14); se heurter (¶81): the collision-verb; Univers capitalized: the cosmic alibi]</span>, at the extreme limit of resources and energies. But it<br>***ne se heurte qu'à ses propres limites*** (periodic depreciation of existing capital), and<br>***ne repousse ou ne déplace que ses propres limites*** <span style="color:green">[repels or displaces only its own limits — repousser: the ¶16/¶27 formula verbatim (the receding convergence-point, capitalism repelling its own limits): the plateau's oldest mechanism restated as the profit-rate chapter's content]</span> (formation of a new capital, in new industries with a high rate of profit).<br>***C'est l'histoire du pétrole et du nucléaire.*** <span style="color:green">[It is the history of oil and of nuclear power — the book's present again: the 1970s energy crisis as the limit-displacement's live example]</span> And the two<br>***à la fois***: it is at the same time that capitalism<br>***se heurte à*** its limits and that it displaces them,<br>***pour les poser plus loin*** <span style="color:green">[in order to pose them farther off — poser: the limit re-posited down the road: the receding horizon (¶16, mis au dehors à mesure qu'on s'en approche) now self-administered]</span>.<br>***On dira que*** the totalitarian tendency, to restrict the axioms, corresponds to the<br>***affrontement*** of the limits, while the social-democratic tendency corresponds to the<br>***déplacement*** of the limits <span style="color:green">[confrontation… displacement — the two poles mapped onto the two relations to the limit: ¶30's limite/seuil machinery gone macroeconomic]</span>. |
| ¶87 fr.578 umn.463 | Or l'une ne va pas sans l'autre, soit en deux lieux différents mais coexistants, soit à moments successifs mais étroitement liés, toujours en prise l'une sur l'autre, et même l'une dans l'autre, constituant la même axiomatique. Un exemple typique serait le Brésil actuel, avec son alternative ambiguë « totalitarisme-social démocratie » . En règle générale, les limites sont d'autant plus mobiles qu'on retire en tel endroit des axiomes, mais qu'on en ajoute ailleurs. Ce serait une erreur de se désintéresser de la lutte au niveau des axiomes. Il arrive que l'on considère que tout axiome, dans le capitalisme ou dans l'un de ses Etats, constitue une « récupération ». Mais ce concept désenchanté n'est pas un bon concept. Les remaniements constants de l'axiomatique capitaliste, c'est-àdire les adjonctions ( énonciation de nouveaux axiomes) et les retraits ( création d'axiomes exclusifs ), sont l'objet de luttes qui ne sont nullement réservées à la technocratie. De toutes parts, en effet, les luttes ouvrières débordent le cadre des entreprises qui impliquent surtout des propositions dérivées. Les luttes prtent directement sur les axiomes qui président aux dépenses publiques d'Etat, ou même qui concernent telle ou telle organisation internationale (par exemple, une firme multinationale peut planifier volontairement la liquidation d'une usine dans un pays ). Le danger dès lors d'une bureaucratie ou d'une technocratie ouvrières mondiales qui se chargeraient de ces problèmes ne peut être lui-même conjuré que dans la mesure où des luttes locales prennent directement pour cibles les axiomes nationaux et internationaux, précisément au point de leur insertion dans le champ d'immanence (potentialité du monde rural à cet égard). | But one does not come without the other, either in two different but coexistent places or in two successive but closely linked moments; they always have a hold on each other, or are even contained in each other, constituting the same axiomatic. A typical example would be present-day Brazil, with its ambiguous alternative "totalitarianism-social democracy." As a general rule, the limits are all the more mobile if axioms are subtracted in one place but added elsewhere.<br>It would be an error to take a disinterested stance toward struggle on the level of the axioms. It is sometimes thought that every axiom, in capitalism or in one of its States, constitutes a "recuperation." But this disenchanted concept is not a good one. The constant readjustments of the capitalist axiomatic, in other words, the additions (the enunciation of new axioms) and the withdrawals (the creation of exclusive axioms), are the object of struggles in no way confined to the technocracy. Everywhere, the workers' struggles overspill the framework of the capitalist enterprises, which imply for the most part derivative propositions. The struggles bear directly upon the axioms that preside over the State's public spending, or that even concern a specific international organization (for example, a multinational corpora- : | ***Or l'une ne va pas sans l'autre***, whether in two different but coexistent<br>***lieux***, or at successive but closely<br>***liés*** moments <span style="color:green">[linked — lier: the bond-chain in its adverbial small change]</span>, always<br>***en prise l'une sur l'autre, et même l'une dans l'autre*** <span style="color:green">[in grip on one another, and even one inside the other — en prise: the capture-noun (prise) as mechanical engagement, gears meshed, the wrestler's hold: the two tendencies not alternatives but interlocked mechanism]</span>, constituting the same axiomatic. A typical example<br>***serait le Brésil actuel***, with its ambiguous alternative «<br>***totalitarisme-social démocratie*** » <span style="color:green">[would be present-day Brazil — conditional held; the book's present once more: the military regime's abertura as the hyphen's referent]</span>.<br>***En règle générale, les limites sont d'autant plus mobiles qu'on retire en tel endroit des axiomes, mais qu'on en ajoute ailleurs*** <span style="color:green">[as a general rule, the limits are all the more mobile as one withdraws axioms in such-and-such a place, but adds them elsewhere — d'autant plus que: the proportional-causal — mobility scaling with the subtract-here/add-there differential]</span>.<br>***Ce serait une erreur de se désintéresser de la lutte au niveau des axiomes*** <span style="color:green">[it would be an error to dis-interest oneself from the struggle at the level of the axioms — se désintéresser: the intérêt chain (¶9, ¶30, ¶34) as political warning: losing interest as the marginal error]</span>.<br>***Il arrive que l'on considère que*** every axiom, in capitalism or in one of its States, constitutes a «<br>***récupération*** » <span style="color:green">[recuperation — the soixante-huitard term: co-optation, quoted as the era's own word]</span>. But this<br>***désenchanté*** concept is not a<br>***bon concept*** <span style="color:green">[disenchanted — Weber's Entzauberung in the shadow; not a good concept — the philosophers' blunt criterion: concepts judged like tools]</span>. The constant<br>***remaniements*** of the capitalist axiomatic <span style="color:green">[re-handlings — remanier: main, the hand: the axiomatic reworked by hand, the Manchot's register in the bureaucrat's verb]</span>, that is to say the<br>***adjonctions*** (énonciation of new axioms) and the<br>***retraits*** (creation of exclusive axioms), are<br>***l'objet de luttes*** in no way reserved to the technocracy.<br>***De toutes parts***, in effect, the workers' struggles<br>***débordent le cadre des entreprises***, which imply above all derived propositions <span style="color:green">[overflow the frame of the enterprises — déborder + cadre: the flood-verb at the shop-gate; note the French has simply « des entreprises »]</span>. The struggles<br>***portent directement sur*** the axioms that<br>***président aux*** public expenditures of State, or even that concern such-and-such an international organization (for example, a multinational firm can<br>***volontairement*** plan the liquidation of a factory in a country <span style="color:green">[voluntarily, deliberately — the servitude volontaire word (¶80) on the other side of the ledger: the firm's free will]</span>). The danger<br>***dès lors*** of a worldwide workers' bureaucracy or technocracy that<br>***se chargeraient*** of these problems can itself be<br>***conjuré*** only<br>***dans la mesure où*** local struggles take directly for targets the national and international axioms <span style="color:green">[warded off — conjurer: the plateau's oldest verb, the primitives' art (¶12–16), prescribed to the movement itself: ward off your own bureaucracy as the villages warded off the State — the anticipation-conjuration mechanism become political program]</span>, precisely at the point of their insertion in the<br>***champ d'immanence*** (<br>***potentialité du monde rural*** in this regard <span style="color:green">[the potentiality of the rural world — the peasant coda: the countryside the towns created (¶10) returning as political potential]</span>). |
| ¶88 fr.578 umn.463 | Il y a toujours une différence fondamentale entre les flux vivants et les axiomes qui les subordonnent à des centres de contrôle et de décision, qui leur font correspondre tel ou tel segment, qui en mesurent les quanta. Mais la pression des flux vivants, et des problèmes qu'ils posent et imposent, doit s'exercer à l'intérieur de l'axiomatique, aussi bien pour lutter contre les réductions totalitaires que pour devancer et précipiter les adjonctions, les orienter, et en empêcher la perversion technocratique. 3. Modèles\ isomorphie. - En principe, tous les Etats sont isomorphes, c'est-à-dire sont des domaines de réalisation du capital en fonction d'un seul et même marché mondial extérieur. Mais une première question serait de savoir si l'isomorphie implique une homogénéité ou même une homogénéisation des Etats. Oui, comme on le voit dans l'Europe actuelle, concernant la justice et la police, le code routier, la circulation des marchandises, les coûts de production, etc. Mais ce n'est vrai que dans la mesure où il y a tendance à un marché intérieur unique intégré . Sinon, l 'isomorphisme n 'implique nullement l'homogénéité : il y a isomorphie, mais hétérogénéité, entre Etats totalitaires et sociaux-démocrates, chaque fois que le mode de production est le même. Les règles générales à cet égard sont les suivantes : la consistance, l'ensemble ou l)unité de l\axiomatique sont définis par le capital comme « droit » ou rapport de production (pour le marché) ; l'indépendance respective des axiomes ne contredit nullement cet ensemble, mais vient des divisions et secteurs du mode de production capitaliste ; l'isomorphie des modèles, avec les deux pôles d'adjonction et de soustraction, revient à la distribution dans chaque cas du marché intérieur et du marché extérieur. | There is always a fundamental difference between Ii ving flows and the axioms that subordinate them to centers of control and decision making, that make a given segment correspond to them, which measure their quanta. But the pressure of the living flows, and of the problems they pose and impose, must be exerted inside the axiomatic, as much in order to fight the totalitarian reductions as to anticipate and precipitate the additions, to orient them and prevent their technocratic perversion.<br>3. Models, isomorphie. In principle, all States are isomorphic; in other words, they are domains of realization of capital as a function of a sole external world market. But the first question is whether isomorphy implies a homogeneity or even a homogenization of States. The answer is yes, as can be seen in present-day Europe with respect to justice and the police, the highway code, the circulation of commodities, production costs, etc. But this is true only insofar as there is a tendency toward a single integrated domestic market. Otherwise, isomorphy in no way implies homogeneity: there is isomorphy, but heterogeneity, between totalitarian and social democratic States wherever the mode of production is the same. The general rules regarding this are as follows: the consistency, the totality (l 'ensemble), or unity of the axiomatic are defined by capital as a "right" or relation of production (for the market); the respective independence of the axioms in no way contradicts this totality but derives from the divisions or sectors of the capitalist mode of production; the isomorphy of the models, with the two poles of addition and subtraction, depends on how the domestic and foreign markets are distributed in each case. | There is always a fundamental difference between the<br>***flux vivants*** and the axioms that subordinate them to centers of control and decision, that<br>***leur font correspondre tel ou tel segment*** <span style="color:green">[make such-and-such a segment correspond to them — the faire-correspondre causative (¶38): correspondence manufactured]</span>, that measure their<br>***quanta*** <span style="color:green">[quanta — the physics plural: flows metered in discrete packets]</span>. But the pressure of the living flows, and of the problems they<br>***posent et imposent*** <span style="color:green">[pose and impose — the rhyme-pair: the problem's two pressures, posed to thought and imposed on life]</span>, must<br>***s'exercer à l'intérieur de*** the axiomatic <span style="color:green">[exercise itself inside — s'exercer: the exercise-chain reflexive: the pressure as agent, working from within]</span>, as much to<br>***lutter contre*** the totalitarian reductions as to<br>***devancer et précipiter les adjonctions*** <span style="color:green">[get ahead of and precipitate the additions — devancer (¶37 fn., Ricardo devançait): the head-start verb, distinct from anticiper; précipiter (¶30): haste and the chemist's precipitation — struggles as the catalyst dropping new axioms out of solution]</span>, to orient them, and to prevent their technocratic<br>***perversion*** <span style="color:green">[the ¶80 word: the contract's extreme perversion now the technocrat's]</span>.<br>***3 Modèles, isomorphie***. —<br>***En principe***, all States are isomorphic, that is, are<br>***domaines de réalisation*** of capital<br>***en fonction d'un*** single same exterior world market. But a first question<br>***serait de savoir si*** isomorphy implies a<br>***homogénéité ou même une homogénéisation*** of the States <span style="color:green">[would be to know whether — conditional + savoir si; homogeneity or even homogenization — the -ité/-isation pair: the state versus the process of sameness]</span>. Yes, as one sees in<br>***l'Europe actuelle***, concerning justice and the police, the<br>***code routier*** <span style="color:green">[the highway code — the plateau's code-word in its most banal administrative dress: overcoding down to traffic regulation, the term's civilian cameo]</span>, the circulation of merchandises, the costs of production, etc. But this is true only<br>***dans la mesure où*** there is a tendency toward a single integrated interior market. Otherwise, isomorphism in no way implies homogeneity: there is isomorphy, but heterogeneity, between totalitarian and social-democratic States,<br>***chaque fois que le mode de production est le même***. The general rules in this regard are the following: the<br>***consistance***, the<br>***ensemble*** or the unity of the axiomatic are defined by capital as «<br>***droit*** » or<br>***rapport de production*** (for the market) <span style="color:green">[relation of production — rendered correctly here by Minnesota: the ¶70 "modes" thereby confirmed as a slip, not a policy]</span>; the respective independence of the axioms in no way contradicts this ensemble, but comes from the divisions and sectors of the capitalist<br>***mode de production***; the isomorphy of the models, with the two poles of<br>***adjonction et de soustraction***,<br>***revient à*** the distribution in each case of the interior market and the exterior market <span style="color:green">[comes back to — revenir (¶9): isomorphy's whole question returning to the two markets' split]</span>. |
| ¶89 fr.579 umn.464 | - Mais ce n 'est là qu'une première bipolarité qui vaut pour les Etats du centre, et sous le mode de production capitaliste. Le centre s 'est vu imposer une seconde bipolarité Ouest-Est, entre les Etats capitalistes et les Etats socialistes bureaucratiques. Or, bien que cette nouvelle distinction puisse reprendre certains traits de la précédente (les Etats dits socialistes étant assimilés à des Etats totalitaires), le problème se pose autrement. Les nombreuses théories de « convergence », qui tentent de montrer une certaine homogénéisation des Etats de l'Est et de l'Ouest sont peu convaincantes. Même l 'isomorphisme ne convient pas : il y a réelle hétéromorphie, non seulement parce que le mode de production n 'est pas capitaliste, mais parce que le rapport de production n 'est pas le Capital (ce serait plutôt le Plan) . Si les Etats socialistes sont pourtant encore des modèles de réalisation de l'axiomatique capitaliste, c'est en fonction de l 'existence d'un seul et unique marché mondial extérieur qui reste ici le facteur décisif, au-delà même des rapports de production dont il résulte. Il peut même arriver que le plan bureaucratique socialiste ait comme une fonction parasitaire par rapport au plan du capital, qui témoigne d'une créativité plus grande, du type « virus » . - Enfin la troisième bipolarité fondamentale est celle du centre et | But this is only a first bipolarity, applying to the States that are located at the center and are under the capitalist mode of production. A second, West-East, bipolarity has been imposed on the States of the center, that of the capitalist States and the bureaucratic socialist States. Although this new distinction may share certain traits of the first (the so-called socialist States being assimilable to the totalitarian States), the problem lies elsewhere. The numerous "convergence" theories that attempt to demonstrate a certain homogenization of the States ofthe East and West are not very convincing. Even isomorphism is not applicable: there is a real heteromorphy, not only because the mode of production is not capitalist, but also because the relation of production is not Capital (rather, it is the Plan). If the socialist States are nevertheless still models of realization for 7000 H.C.: | — But this is only a first bipolarity, which<br>***vaut pour*** [applies to] the States of the center, and under the capitalist mode of production. The center<br>***s'est vu imposer*** a second West-East bipolarity <span style="color:green">[saw itself imposed — the reflexive-passive of endured imposition: the bipolarity suffered, not chosen]</span>, between the capitalist States and the bureaucratic socialist States.<br>***Or***, although this new distinction can<br>***reprendre*** certain traits of the preceding one (the States<br>***dits*** socialist being<br>***assimilés*** to totalitarian States <span style="color:green">[being assimilated — participle of fact: the theories DO assimilate them, whatever the merits]</span>),<br>***le problème se pose autrement*** <span style="color:green">[the problem poses itself otherwise — poser: differently, in another manner — not in another place]</span>. The numerous theories of «<br>***convergence*** », which attempt to show a certain homogenization of the States of East and West, are<br>***peu convaincantes***. Even isomorphism<br>***ne convient pas***: there is real heteromorphy, not only because the<br>***mode de production*** is not capitalist, but because the<br>***rapport de production*** is not Capital (<br>***ce serait plutôt le Plan*** <span style="color:green">[it would rather be the Plan — conditional + le Plan capitalized: Gosplan as relation of production, the mode/rapport pair working exactly]</span>). If the socialist States are nonetheless still models of realization of the capitalist axiomatic, it is<br>***en fonction de*** the existence of one single exterior world market, which remains here the decisive factor,<br>***au-delà même des rapports de production dont il résulte***. It can even<br>***arriver*** that the bureaucratic socialist<br>***plan*** has something like a parasitic function in relation to the<br>***plan*** of capital <span style="color:green">[plan — the pun: the economic plan and the plane (plan d'immanence family): two plans, two planes — NOTE Minnesota prints "plan(e)… plan(e)", marking the pun with the parenthetical letter: another of Massumi's method-kindred devices, entered in that dossier with ¶54's compris and ¶19's milieu]</span>, which<br>***témoigne d'une créativité plus grande, du type « virus »*** <span style="color:green">[testifies to a greater creativity, of the « virus » type — the biological figure: the socialist plan as parasite on capital's more inventive plane]</span>. — Finally the third fundamental bipolarity is that of the center and… |
| ¶90 fr.580 umn.465 | de la périphérie (Nord-Sud). En vertu de l'indépendance respective des axiomes, on peut dire avec Samir Amin que les axiomes de la périphérie ne sont pas les mêmes que ceux du centre 57. Et, là encore, la différence et l'indépendance des axiomes ne compromettent nullement la consistance de l'axiomatique d'ensemble. Au contraire, le capitalisme central a besoin de cette périphérie constituée par le tiers monde, dans lequel il installe une grande partie de son industrie la plus moderne, où il ne se contente pas d'investir des capitaux, mais qui lui fournit du capital. Certes, la question de la dépendance des Etats du tiers monde est évidente, mais elle n'est pas la plus importante (elle est héritée de l'ancien colonialisme). Il est évident que même l'indépendance des axiomes n 'a jamais garanti l'indépendance des Etats, mais assure plutôt une division internationale du travail. La question importante, là encore, c'est celle de l'isomorphie par rapport à l'axiomatique mondiale. Or, dans une large mesure, il y a isomorphie entre les Etats-Unis et les tyrannies les plus sanglantes d'Amérique du Sud (ou bien entre la France, l'Angleterre, la R . F. A. et certains E ta ts africains ) . Tou tefois, la bipolarité cen tre-périphérie, Etats du centre et du tiers monde, a beau reprendre à son tour des traits distinctifs des deux bipolarités précédentes, elle y échappe aussi, et pose d'autres problèmes . C'est que, dans une vaste part du tiers monde, le rapport de production général peut être le capital ; et même dans tout le tiers monde, au sens où le secteur socialisé peut se servir de ce rapport, le reprendre à son compte en ce cas. Mais le mode de production n'est pas nécessairement capitaliste, non seulement dans les formes dites archaïques ou transitionnelles, mais dans les secteurs les plus productifs et de haute industrialisation. C'est donc bien un troisième cas, compris dans l'axiomatique mondiale : lorsque le capital agit comme rapport de production, mais dans des modes de production non capitalistes . | (North-South). In view of the respective independence of the axioms, we can join Samir Amin in saying that the axioms of the periphery differ from those of the center.63 And here again, the difference and independence of the axioms in no way compromise the consistency of the overall axiomatic. On the contrary, central capitalism needs the periphery constituted by the Third World, where it locates a large part of its most modern industries; it does not just invest capital in these industries, but is also furnished with capital by them. The issue of the dependence of the Third World States is of course an obvious one, but not the most important one (it was bequeathed by the old colonialism). It is obvious that having independent axioms has never guaranteed the independence of States; rather it ensures an international division of labor. The important question, once again, is that of isomorphy in relation to the worldwide axiomatic. To a large extent, there is isomorphy between the United States and the bloodiest of the South American tyrannies (or between France, England, and West Germany and certain African States). The center-periphery bipolarity, States of the center and States of the Third World, may well exhibit some of the distinguishing traits of the two preceding bipolarities, but it also evades them, raising other problems. Throughout a vast portion of the Third World, the general relation of production is capital-even throughout the entire Third World, in the sense that the socialized sector may utilize that relation, adopting it in this case. But the mode of production is not necessarily capitalist, either in the so-called archaic or transitional forms, or in the most productive, highly industrialized sectors. This indeed represents a third case, included in the worldwide axiomatic: when capital acts as the relation of production but in noncapitalist modes of production. | …of the periphery (North-South).<br>***En vertu de*** the respective independence of the axioms, one can say with Samir Amin that the axioms of the periphery are not the same as those of the center<br>⁵<br>. And,<br>***là encore***, the difference and independence of the axioms in no way compromise the<br>***consistance*** of the<br>***axiomatique d'ensemble***. On the contrary, central capitalism<br>***a besoin de*** this periphery constituted by the third world, in which it installs a great part of its most modern industry, where it<br>***ne se contente pas d'investir*** capitals, but which<br>***lui fournit du capital*** <span style="color:green">[does not content itself with investing… but which furnishes it capital — se contenter (¶29) + the reversal: the periphery as capital's supplier, not merely its site]</span>.<br>***Certes***, the question of the dependence of the third-world States is evident, but it is not the most important (it is<br>***héritée de*** the old colonialism <span style="color:green">[inherited from — the heir-register (¶82): dependence as legacy, not mechanism]</span>). It is evident that even the<br>***independence of the axioms*** has never guaranteed the<br>***independence of the States*** <span style="color:green">[the pun cashed: fn. 48's technical term (independence of axioms) against political independence — one word, two regimes, the sentence's whole irony]</span>, but rather<br>***assure*** an international division of labor. The important question,<br>***là encore***, is that of isomorphy in relation to the worldwide axiomatic.<br>***Or***, in a large measure, there is isomorphy between the United States and the bloodiest tyrannies of South America (or indeed between France, England, the R.F.A. and certain African States) <span style="color:green">[the ¶70 strict complementarities cashed with proper names: the polymorphy thesis made actionable]</span>.<br>***Toutefois***, the center-periphery bipolarity<br>***a beau reprendre à son tour*** distinctive traits of the two preceding bipolarities <span style="color:green">[however much it takes up in its turn — avoir beau: the in-vain concessive: let it resemble all it likes, it escapes]</span>, it also escapes them, and<br>***pose d'autres problèmes***. It is that, in a vast part of the third world, the general<br>***rapport de production*** can be capital; and even in the whole third world, in the sense that the socialized sector can<br>***se servir de ce rapport, le reprendre à son compte*** in that case <span style="color:green">[make use of this relation, take it up on its own account — à son compte: the accounting formula (¶16, ¶58, ¶78): capital adopted onto the socialized sector's own books]</span>. But the mode of production is not necessarily capitalist, not only in the forms<br>***dites*** archaic or<br>***transitionnelles*** <span style="color:green">[so-called transitional — the ¶27 formes transitionnelles refused again]</span>, but in the most productive and highly industrialized sectors. It is therefore indeed a third case,<br>***compris*** in the worldwide axiomatic <span style="color:green">[comprised/understood in — the ¶54 compris pun: included in the axiomatic, and only thereby intelligible]</span>: when capital acts as<br>***rapport de production***, but in non-capitalist<br>***modes de production***.<br><br>⁵<br>Cf. the critical list of the axioms of the periphery, by Samir Amin, *L'accumulation à l'échelle mondiale*, pp. 373–376. <span style="color:green">(French 57 = Minnesota 63)</span> |
| ¶91 fr.580 umn.465 | On parlera alors d'une polymorphie des Etats du tiers monde par rapport aux Etats du centre. Et c'est une dimension de l 'axiomatique non moins nécessaire que les autres : beaucoup plus nécessaire même, car l'hétéromorphie des Etats dits socialistes a été imposée au capitalisme qui la digère tant bien que mal, tandis que la polymorphie des Etats du tiers monde est partiellement organisée par le centre, comme axiome de substitution de la colonisation. - Nous retrouvons toujours la question littérale des modèles de réalisation d'une axiomatique mondiale : l'isomorphie des modèles en principe dans les Etats du centre ; l' hétéromorphie imposée par l'Etat socialiste bureaucratique ; la 57. Cf. la liste critique des axiomes de la périphérie, par Samir Amin L'accumulation à l'échelle mondiale, pp. 373-376. polymorphie organisée des Etats du tiers monde. Là encore, il serait absurde de croire que l'insertion des mouvements populaires dans tout ce champ d'immanence est condamnée d'avance, et de supposer, ou bien qu'il y a de « bons » Etats qui seraient démocratiques, sociaux-démocrates, ou socialistes à l'autre extrême, ou bien au contraire que tous les Etats se valent et sont homogènes. 4. La puissance. - Supposons que l'axiomatique dégage nécessairement une puissance supérieure à celle qu'elle traite, c'est-àdire à celle des ensembles qui lui servent de modèles . C'est comme une puissance du continu, liée à l'axiomatique, et pourtant qui la déborde . Nous reconnaissons cette puissance immédiatement comme puissance de destruction, de guerre, incarnée dans des complexes technologiques militaires, industriels et financiers, en continuité les uns avec les autres. | We may therefore speak of a polymorphy of the Third World States in relation to the States of the center. And this dimension of the axiomatic is no less necessary than the others; it is even much more necessary, for the heteromorphy of the so-called socialist States was imposed upon capitalism, which digested it as best it could, whereas the polymorphy of the Third World States is partially organized by the center, as an axiom providing a substitute for colonization.<br>We are always brought back to the literal question of the models of realization of a worldwide axiomatic: there is in principle an isomorphy of the States of the center, a heteromorphy imposed by the bureaucratic socialist : | ***On parlera alors d'une*** polymorphy of the third-world States in relation to the States of the center. And it is a dimension of the axiomatic no less necessary than the others: even much more necessary, for the heteromorphy of the States<br>***dits*** socialist<br>***a été imposée*** to capitalism, which<br>***la digère tant bien que mal*** <span style="color:green">[digests it as-well-as-badly — tant bien que mal: the limping idiom: digestion half-managed, ongoing (present tense)]</span>, whereas the polymorphy of the third-world States is<br>***partiellement organisée par le centre, comme axiome de substitution de la colonisation*** <span style="color:green">[partially organized BY THE CENTER, as substitution-axiom for colonization — decolonization as an axiom-swap: the colony deleted, the axiom inserted]</span>. — We always find again the<br>***question littérale*** of the models of realization of a worldwide axiomatic: the isomorphy of the models<br>***en principe*** in the States of the center; the heteromorphy<br>***imposée par*** the bureaucratic socialist State;<br>***la polymorphie organisée des États du tiers monde*** <span style="color:green">[the ORGANIZED polymorphy OF the third-world States — organized, per the preceding sentence, by the center: the genitive is possessive, the agent is the center — see flag 1]</span>.<br>***Là encore, il serait absurde de croire que*** the insertion of the popular movements<br>***dans tout ce champ d'immanence*** is<br>***condamnée d'avance*** <span style="color:green">[it would be absurd to believe that the insertion of the popular movements INTO this whole field of immanence is condemned in advance — the ¶87 formula verbatim (insertion dans le champ d'immanence): the struggles inserted into the field, and the verdict-in-advance refused; d'avance: the pre- chain; condamner: the juridical sentence]</span>, and to suppose either that there are « good » States,<br>***qui seraient*** democratic, social-democratic, or socialist at the other extreme, or on the contrary that all the States<br>***se valent*** and are homogeneous <span style="color:green">[are worth one another — the ¶70 « se valant » reflexive again: the two absurdities bracketing the field]</span>.<br>***4 La puissance***. —<br>***Supposons que*** the axiomatic necessarily<br>***dégage*** a<br>***puissance*** superior to the one it<br>***traite***, that is, to that of the<br>***ensembles*** that serve it as models <span style="color:green">[let us suppose — the geometer's imperative: the supposer chain become method; extracts a power superior to the one it treats — puissance/ensembles: the cardinality machinery (fn. 48, ¶70) now the proposition's own frame]</span>. It is like a<br>***puissance du continu***,<br>***liée à*** the axiomatic, and yet<br>***qui la déborde*** <span style="color:green">[a power of the continuum, tied to the axiomatic and yet overflowing it — Cantor's continuum as the figure of what politics cannot master; liée: the bond-chain; déborder: the flood]</span>. We recognize this puissance immediately as<br>***puissance de destruction, de guerre***, incarnated in technological complexes — military, industrial and financial —<br>***en continuité les uns avec les autres*** <span style="color:green">[in continuity with one another — the continu pun closing the clause: the continuum's power incarnate as the continuous complexes — mathematics into ordnance in one word-family]</span>. |
| ¶92 fr.581 umn.466 | D'une part, la guerre suit évidemment le même mouvement que celui du capitalisme : de même que le capital constant croît proportionnellement, la guerre devient de plus en plus « guerre de matériel », où l 'homme ne représente même plus un capital variable d'assujettissement, mais un pur élément d'asservissement machinique. D'autre part et surtout, l'importance croissante du capital constant dans l'axiomatique fait que la dépréciation du capital existant et la formation d'un nouveau capital prennent un rythme et une ampleur qui passent nécessairement par une machine de guerre incarnée maintenant dans les complexes : celle-ci participe activement aux redistributions du monde exigées par l'exploitation des ressources maritimes et planétaires. Il y a un « seuil » continu de la puissance qui accompagne chaque fois le transport des « limites » de l'axiomatique ; comme si la puissance de guerre venait toujours sursaturer la saturation du système, et la conditionnait. - Aux conflits classiques entre Etats du centre ( et colonisation périphérique ) se sont jointes ou plutôt substituées deux grandes lignes conflictuelles, entre l 'Ouest et l'Est, entre le Nord et le Sud, se recoupant l'une l'autre et recouvrant l 'ensemble. Or, non seulement le surarmement de l'Ouest et de l'Est laisse entièrement subsister la réalité des guerres locales, et leur donne une nouvelle force et de nouveaux enjeux ; non seulement il fonde la possibilité « apocalyptique » d'un affrontement direct suivant les deux grands axes ; mais il semble aussi que la machine de guerre prend un sens spécifique supplémentaire, industriel, politique, judiciaire, etc. | On the one hand, war clearly follows the same movement as capitalism: In the same way as the proportion of constant capital keeps growing, war becomes increasingly a "war of materiel" in which the human being no longer even represents a variable capital of subjection, but is instead a pure element of machinic enslavement. On the other hand, and this is the main point, the growing importance of constant capital in the axiomatic means that the depreciation of existing capital and the formation of new capital assume a rhythm and scale that necessarily take the route of a war machine now incarnated in the complexes: the complexes actively contribute to the redistributions of the world necessary for the exploitation of maritime and planetary resources. There is a continuous "threshold" of power that accompanies in every instance the shifting of the axiomatic's limits; it is as though the power of war always supersaturated the system's saturation, and was its necessary condition.<br>The classical conflicts among the States of the center (as well as peripheral colonization) have been joined, or rather replaced, by two great conflictual lines, between West and East and North and South; these lines intersect and together cover everything. But the overarmament ofthe West and East not only leaves the reality of local wars entirely intact and gives them a new force and new stakes; it not only founds the "apocalyptic" possibility of a direct confrontation along the two great axes; it also seems that the war machine takes on a specific supplementary meaning: industrial, political, judicial, etc. | On the one hand, war evidently follows the same movement as that of capitalism: just as constant capital<br>***croît proportionnellement***, war becomes more and more «<br>***guerre de matériel*** » <span style="color:green">[war of materiel — the Great War's term, Materialschlacht: battle as expenditure of stuff]</span>, where man no longer even represents a<br>***capital variable d'assujettissement***, but a pure element of<br>***asservissement machinique*** <span style="color:green">[the ¶75 pair cashed at the front: the soldier past subjection, wired in as component]</span>. On the other hand and above all, the growing importance of constant capital in the axiomatic<br>***fait que*** the depreciation of existing capital and the formation of a new capital<br>***prennent un rythme et une ampleur qui passent nécessairement par*** a war machine now incarnated in the complexes <span style="color:green">[take a rhythm and an amplitude that pass necessarily by way of — the faire que causative + the routing idiom + rythme (¶1: the curious rhythm that animates the State apparatus, now the arms cycle's)]</span>: the latter participates actively in the redistributions of the world<br>***exigées par*** the exploitation of maritime and planetary resources <span style="color:green">[demanded by — exiger (¶56): the exigence, requirement as demand served]</span>. There is a continuous «<br>***seuil*** » of puissance that accompanies each time the<br>***transport*** of the «<br>***limites*** » of the axiomatic <span style="color:green">[a continuous « threshold »… the transport of the « limits » — BOTH terms scare-quoted in the French: the ¶30 limite/seuil distinction (limit = necessary rebeginning, threshold = inevitable change) recalled by its own quotation marks at world scale; transport (¶15): displacement across the topology]</span>; as if the<br>***puissance de guerre*** came always to<br>***sursaturer la saturation*** of the system,<br>***et la conditionnait*** <span style="color:green">[supersaturate the saturation… and conditioned it — sursaturer: the sur- chain (surcodage, surtravail, surprofit, surarmement) meeting the chemistry: the supersaturated solution, war as the excess held past saturation, ready to precipitate; conditionner: bare conditioning]</span>. — To the classical conflicts between States of the center (and peripheral colonization)<br>***se sont jointes ou plutôt substituées*** two great conflictual lines, between West and East, between North and South,<br>***se recoupant l'une l'autre et recouvrant l'ensemble*** <span style="color:green">[re-cutting one another and covering-over the whole — recouper: the couper chain, lines that intersect as mutual cuts; recouvrir: the ¶8 verb (the Urstaat recouvert), now the conflict-lines blanketing the set]</span>.<br>***Or***, not only does the<br>***surarmement*** of West and East <span style="color:green">[over-armament — the sur- chain's newest member]</span> leave entirely subsisting the reality of local wars, and give them a new force and new<br>***enjeux*** <span style="color:green">[stakes — enjeu: what is in the game, the jeu chain: local wars re-staked]</span>; but it seems also that the<br>***machine de guerre*** takes on a specific supplementary sense, industrial, political, judicial, etc. |
| ¶93 fr.581 umn.466 | Il est bien vrai que les Etats dans leur histoire n'ont pas cessé de s'approprier la machine de guerre ; et c'est en même temps que la guerre, dans sa préparation et son effectuation, devenait l'objet exclusif de la machine, mais comme guerre plus ou moins « limitée ». Quant au but, il restait le but politique des Etats. Les différents facteurs qui tendirent à faire de la guerre une guerre « totale », et notamment le facteur fasciste, marquèrent le début d'une inversion du mouvement : comme si le Etats, après la longue période d'appropriation, reconstituaient une machine de guerre autonome, à travers la guerre qu'ils menaient les uns contre les autres . Mais cette machine de guerre libérée ou déchaînée continuait à avoir pour objet la guerre en acte, en tant que guerre devenue totale, illimitée. Toute l'économie fasciste devenait économie de guerre, mais l'économie de guerre avait encore besoin de la guerre totale en tant qu'objet. Dès lors, la guerre fasciste restait sous la formule de Clausewitz, « continuation de la politique avec accompagnement d'autres moyens », bien que ces autres moyens devinssent exclusifs, ou que le but politique entrât en contradiction avec l'objet ( d'où l'idée de Virilio que l'Etat fasciste était un Etat « suicidaire » plus que totalitaire) . C'est seulement après la Seconde Guerre mondiale que l'automatisation, puis l'automation de la machine de guerre, ont produit leur véritable effet. Celle-ci, compte tenu des nouveaux antagonismes qui la traversaient, n'avait plus la guerre pour objet exclusif, mais prenait en charge et pour objet la paix, la politique, l'ordre mondial, bref, le but . | It is indeed true that the States, throughout their history, have repeatedly appropriated the war machine; and it was after the war machine was appropriated that war, its preparation and ef fectuation, became the exclusive object of the machine, but as a more or less "limited" war. As for the aim, it remained the political aim of the States. The various factors that tended to make war a "total war," most notably the fascist factor, marked the beginning of an inversion of the movement: as though the 7000 B.c.: | ⚠<br>QUOTATION FLAG — Clausewitz. German original, and the variant matters: D&G's French reads « continuation de la politique avec accompagnement d'autres moyens » — the accompaniment-version (Vom Kriege's « mit Einmischung anderer Mittel », the admixture formula), NOT the famous « par d'autres moyens » (mit anderen Mitteln). Minnesota prints "the continuation of politics by other means" — the standard slogan restored, D&G's marked choice of the lesser-known variant erased. Verify the German loci; this is a restoration that overwrites a deliberate citation-decision, the strongest case yet for the protocol.<br><br>***Il est bien vrai que*** the States in their history<br>***n'ont pas cessé de s'approprier*** the machine de guerre;<br>***et c'est en même temps que*** war, in its preparation and its<br>***effectuation***, became the exclusive<br>***objet*** of the machine <span style="color:green">[and it is AT THE SAME TIME that — simultaneity: appropriation and war-becoming-object as one movement, not a sequence]</span>, but as war more or less «<br>***limitée*** ». As for the<br>***but***, it remained the political<br>***but*** of the States <span style="color:green">[the aim — but vs. objet: the Clausewitzian pair structuring the whole paragraph: the machine's object (war) against the State's aim (policy)]</span>. The different factors that<br>***tendirent à faire de la guerre une guerre « totale »***, and notably the fascist factor, marked the beginning of an<br>***inversion*** of the movement: as if the States, after the long period of appropriation,<br>***reconstituaient*** an autonomous machine de guerre, through the war they waged against one another. But this<br>***libérée ou déchaînée*** war machine <span style="color:green">[liberated or unchained — déchaîner: the chain broken: the lien/bond family negated — the machine un-bound, the Binder-Gods' work undone by the States themselves]</span> continued to have for<br>***objet***<br>***la guerre en acte*** <span style="color:green">[war in act — en acte: the actus register (actuel chain): war actualized, not merely prepared]</span>, as war become total, unlimited. The whole fascist economy became<br>***économie de guerre***, but the war economy still<br>***avait besoin de*** total war<br>***en tant qu'objet***.<br>***Dès lors***, fascist war remained under the formula of Clausewitz, « continuation of politics<br>***avec accompagnement*** of other means » <span style="color:green">[with accompaniment — the musical accompagnement: politics still the melody, war the accompaniment — the variant's whole point, and why D&G chose it]</span>, although these other means<br>***devinssent*** exclusive,<br>***ou que*** the political but<br>***entrât*** in contradiction with the objet <span style="color:green">[became… or that… entered — imperfect subjunctives; ou que: OR that — a second, alternative degeneration, not a paraphrase of the first]</span> (whence Virilio's idea that the fascist State was a «<br>***suicidaire*** » State more than a totalitarian one <span style="color:green">[suicidal — the ¶31 agencement suicidaire: the alcoholic's worst threshold, statized]</span>). It is only after the Second World War that<br>***l'automatisation, puis l'automation*** of the war machine produced their true effect <span style="color:green">[automatization, then automation — the two words held apart: mechanized process, then self-governing process]</span>. The latter,<br>***compte tenu des*** new antagonisms that traversed it <span style="color:green">[account taken of — the compte tenu formula, fourth occurrence]</span>, no longer had war for exclusive objet, but<br>***prenait en charge et pour objet la paix, la politique, l'ordre mondial, bref, le but*** <span style="color:green">[took in charge and as object peace, politics, the world order, in short, THE AIM — prendre en charge: the administrative custody-idiom; the paragraph's thesis: the machine's object is now the State's aim itself — Clausewitz inverted, the pair collapsed from the machine's side]</span>. |
| ¶94 fr.582 umn.467 | C'est là qu 'apparaît l'inversion de la formule de Clausewitz : c'est la politique qui devient la continuation de la guerre, c'est la paix qui libère techniquement le processus matériel illimité de la guerre totale. La guerre cesse d'être la matérialisation de la machine de guerre, c'est la machine de guerre qui devient elle-même guerre matérialisée. En ce sens, il n 'y avait plus besoin de fascisme. The Fascists were only child precursors, and the absolute peace of survival succeeded where total war had failed. The Third World War was already upon us. | ¶95 fr.583 umn.467 | technico-scientiflque ; comment cette machine de guerre n 'était pas terrible en \onction dl' la gt J('l ll' possible qu'elle nous promettait comme dans un cha n t agl' , 1 I J ;l i au contraire e n fonction de l a paix réelle très spci a l l' l j t l 't·I Il' promouvait et installait déjà ; comment cette mach i ne dl' g t l t' lTt' n'avait plus besoin d'un ennemi qualifié, mais, con forméllll' I l t aux exigences d'une axiomatique, s 'exerçait contre l' « ennl'Illi quelconque » , intérieur ou extérieur ( individu, groupe, c1assl' , peuple, événement, monde ) ; comment en sortait une nOllvelll' conception de la sécurité comme guerre matérialisée, comme inscurité organisée ou catastrophe programmée, distribuée, moléclIlarisée . 5. Tiers indu. - Que l'axiomatique capitaliste ait besoin d'un centre, et que ce centre se soit constitué au nord, à la suite d'un long processus historique, nul ne l'a montré mieux que Braudel : « Il ne peut y avoir d'économie-monde que lorsque le réseau a des mailles suffisamment serrées, que l'échange est assez régulier et volumineux pour donner vie à une zone centrale 59 . » Beaucoup d'auteurs considèrent à cet égard que l'axe Nord-Sud, centrepériphérie, est aujourd'hui encore plus important que l'axe OuestEst, et même le détermine principalement . C'est ce qu'exprime une thèse courante, reprise et développée par Giscard d'Estaing : plus les choses s 'équilibrent au centre entre l'Ouest et l'Est, à commencer par l'équilibre du surarmement, plus elles se déséquilibrent ou de « déstabilisent » du Nord au Sud, et déstabilisent l 'équilibre central. Il est clair que, dans ces formules, le Sud est un terme abstrait qui désigne le tiers monde ou la périphérie ; 58. Paul Virilio, L'insécurité du territoire ; Vitesse et politique ; Défense populaire et luttes écologiques : c'est précisément au-delà du fascisme et de la guerre totale que la machine de guerre trouve son objet complet, dans la paix menaçante de la dissuasion nucléaire. C'est là que le renversement de la formule de Clausewitz prend un sens concret, en même temps que l'Etat politique tend à dépérir et que la machine de guerre s'empare d'un maximum de fonctions civiles (<< mettre l'ensemble de la société civile au régime de la sécurité militaire », « disqualifier l'ensemble de l'habitat planétaire en dépouillant les peuples de leur qualité d'habitant », « effacer la distinction d'un temps de guerre et d'un temps de paix ( . . . ) » : cf. le rôle des médias à cet égard). Un exemple simple serait fourni par certaines polices européennes, quand elles réclament le droit de « tirer à vue » : elles cessent d'être rouage de l'appareil d'Etat pour devenir les pièces d'une machine de guerre. | that this war machine is terrif ying not as a function of a possible war that it promises us, as by blackmail, but, on the contrary, as a function of the real, very special kind of peace it promotes and has already installed; that this war machine no longer needs a qualified enemy but, in conf ormity with the requirements of an axiomatic, operates against the "unspecified enemy," domestic or foreign (an individual, group, class, people, event, world); that there arose from this a new conception of security as materialized war, as organized insecurity or molecularized, distributed, programmed catastrophe.64<br>5 . The included middle. No one has demonstrated more convincingly than Braudel that the capitalist axiomatic requires a center and that this center was constituted in the North, at the outcome of a long historical process: "There can only be a world-economy when the mesh of the network is sufficiently fine, and when exchange is regular and voluminous enough to give rise to a central zone."65 Many authors believe on this account that the North-South, center-periphery axis is more important today than the West-East axis, and even principally determines it. This is expressed in a common thesis, taken up and developed by Valery Giscard d'Estaing: the more equilibrated things become at the center between the West and the East, beginning with the equilibrium of overarmament, the more they become disequilibrated or "destabilized" from North to South and destabilize the central equilibrium. It is clear that in these formulas the South is an abstract term designating the Third World or the periphery; | …how this machine de guerre was not terrible<br>***en fonction de*** the possible war it promised us as in a<br>***chantage*** <span style="color:green">[blackmail — the extortion register (¶49): deterrence as protection racket, refused as the explanation]</span>, but on the contrary en fonction de the<br>***paix réelle très spéciale*** that it promoted and<br>***installait déjà***; how this machine de guerre no longer had need of an<br>***ennemi qualifié***, but, in conformity with the exigencies of an axiomatic,<br>***s'exerçait contre l'« ennemi quelconque »***, interior or exterior (individual, group, class, people, event, world) <span style="color:green">[exercised itself against the « whatever-enemy » — quelconque: the ¶19/¶62 operator militarized: the enemy as unqualified element, the axiomatic's arbitrary x — matching the Objet quelconque as its target-form; qualifié: the ¶59 conjonctions qualifiées, the qualified now obsolete on the battlefield too]</span>; how there came out of it a new conception of<br>***sécurité*** as<br>***guerre matérialisée***, as<br>***insécurité organisée ou catastrophe programmée, distribuée, molécularisée*** <span style="color:green">[organized insecurity or programmed, distributed, molecularized catastrophe — the series in the French order: from program to molecule, scale descending]</span>.<br>***5 Tiers indu***. — <span style="color:green">[The Undue Third — the heading's double pun: (a) on ¶82's tiers exclu, the excluded middle — the third world as the middle that is not excluded; (b) l'indu, the legal term for the not-owed (paiement de l'indu, restitution of undue payment — the compte chain): the third as wrongful inclusion, the party paid what was never owed or denied what was. Minnesota prints "The included middle" — catching pun (a) by inversion, erasing pun (b)'s debt entirely: see flags]</span> That the capitalist axiomatic<br>***ait besoin d'un*** center, and that this center<br>***se soit constitué*** in the north,<br>***à la suite d'un*** long historical process,<br>***nul ne l'a montré mieux que Braudel*** <span style="color:green">[the fronted que-clauses (¶44) with the verb held back: the theses weighed before their prover is named]</span>: « There can be a world-economy only when the<br>***réseau*** has<br>***mailles*** sufficiently tight, when exchange is regular and voluminous enough to give life to a central zone<br>⁵<br>» <span style="color:green">[meshes — Braudel's own mailles: the ¶7/¶55/¶59 mesh-chain in the source's mouth, as prise was at ¶18 fn.; Braudel French-original, translated from the supplied French]</span>. Many authors consider in this regard that the North-South, center-periphery axis is today even more important than the West-East axis, and even determines it principally. Braudel shows how this center of gravity formed in northern Europe, but at the outcome of movements that, starting in the ninth and tenth centuries, put the European spaces of the North and the South in competition or rivalry with one another (this problem is not to be confused with that of the town-form and State-f orm, but does intersect with it).
